UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons

Linzhan Mou, Jiahui Lei, Zhiyang Dou et al.. UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of generating motion for diverse 3D animated characters, where existing methods are limited by their dependence on fixed skeleton topologies or require per-skeleton fine-tuning and reference motions at test time. The authors developed UniMate, a unified diffusion-based foundation model that takes a rigged 3D asset and a text prompt as input and synthesizes articulated motion for arbitrary skeleton structures without any test-time optimization or retraining. The model employs a topology-aware diffusion transformer that encodes skeletal structure through graph-aware attention biases derived from pairwise joint relations and geodesic distances, a spectral rotary position embedding adapted to arbitrary kinematic trees via the graph Laplacian, and a global topological conditioner derived from the rest-pose skeleton. To train the model, the authors curated a large dataset spanning a wide variety of creature types and articulated objects, with unified canonicalization and text descriptions paired to motion sequences. UniMate is reported to outperform state-of-the-art baselines in quality, generalization, and efficiency, while also enabling zero-shot cross-topology transfer, motion in-betweening, expansion, and text-guided editing.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigates two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets, TOI-426 b and TOI-1839 b, situated near the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert—a region of parameter space where close-in planets of intermediate size are rare. Using space-based photometry and ground-based radial velocity measurements, the researchers confirmed the planets' physical properties and detected additional companions in both systems, including a long-period giant planet around TOI-426 and a transiting sub-Neptune around TOI-1839. Despite receiving extreme stellar irradiation, the densities of all three transiting planets require substantial volatile content, and atmospheric escape modeling strongly favors water-dominated rather than hydrogen/helium envelopes, supporting a steam-world interpretation. Interior structure models show that hydrogen/helium envelopes would be lost on timescales far shorter than water-dominated ones, meaning the survival of these volatile-rich planets cannot be explained by photoevaporation of primordial atmospheres alone. A population-level analysis further reveals a statistically significant excess of outer giant companions around low-mass planets near the desert edge compared to those outside it, lending support to high-eccentricity tidal migration as an important mechanism in sculpting the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether machine learning can recover the primordial density field of the Universe from observations of the Epoch of Reionization, thereby retrieving cosmological information lost to non-linear structure formation. The researchers used simulated 21-cm and CO line-intensity maps at high redshift, generated with a dedicated simulation code, as inputs to a three-dimensional U-Net architecture trained to reconstruct initial conditions at a much earlier epoch. The two tracers were chosen deliberately for their complementarity, with 21-cm emission probing diffuse neutral gas and CO probing dense star-forming regions, and realistic instrumental effects mimicking next-generation radio and millimeter surveys were incorporated. In idealized conditions, combining both tracers yielded high-fidelity reconstruction across a broad range of spatial scales, and even under realistic noise the joint reconstruction maintained meaningful correlation with the true initial field on large scales. Crucially, performing simulation-based inference of cosmological parameters on power-spectrum summaries derived from reconstructed fields substantially tightened parameter constraints compared to using unreconstructed maps, demonstrating that such joint multi-tracer reconstruction can recover otherwise inaccessible cosmological information.

WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data

Ji Soo Lee, Xilun Chen, Pierce Chuang et al.. WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a benchmark designed to evaluate whether AI systems can reason over longitudinal wearable sensor data from real users, an area poorly covered by existing benchmarks. The authors constructed a large set of multiple-choice questions derived from wearable time series, blood biomarkers, and demographic information spanning hundreds of days per user, preserving realistic data characteristics such as device noise and individual variability. Questions were organized across two analytical axes distinguishing data computation from physiological interpretation and single-signal from multi-signal reasoning, and were generated using a dual-grounding framework combining established physiological literature with statistically validated population-level patterns. Evaluation of a broad set of proprietary and open-source large language models revealed substantial variation in performance, with most models falling well below ceiling accuracy and the overall range spanning from near-chance to roughly three-quarters correct. The benchmark thus demonstrates both its discriminative power and the considerable gap that remains before AI systems can reliably perform health reasoning over real-world wearable data.

Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence

Urja Pawar, Rajitha Ramanayake, Nabeel Kemal et al.. Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the explanations produced by large language models alongside their decisions actually reflect the factors that behaviorally drive those decisions. The researchers tested two distinct interpretations of explanatory factors: necessity, asking whether changing a cited factor would alter the output, and sufficiency, asking whether retaining a cited factor while removing other information would preserve the output. Using controlled black-box interventions across two synthetic tasks—recommending advisors to clients and judging prompts for harmfulness—they measured necessity and sufficiency scores for factors across eight models from three major model families and compared these scores to the models' own cited rankings. They found only moderate correlations between the cited factor rankings and the behaviorally measured scores, and in a substantial proportion of cases an uncited factor outscored the lowest-ranked cited factor, indicating that model-provided explanations do not reliably identify the most influential factors. The authors conclude that while cited explanations carry some useful signal, they cannot be trusted as reliable guides for system monitoring or oversight, and they propose their framework as a practical black-box reliability check for explanations used in agentic contexts.

Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models

Matthias Busch, Marius Tacke, Sviatlana V. Lamaka et al.. Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether large language models performing well on molecular property benchmarks are genuinely predicting properties or simply retrieving memorized published values. The authors audited a substantial set of frontier language models across numerous regression benchmarks, testing for verbatim digit-level retrieval of known results. They found that verbatim retrieval is widespread but benchmark-specific, with the majority of models showing retrieval on some datasets while other datasets remain largely unaffected. Notably, reasoning level strongly influences retrieval behavior, with higher reasoning settings flagging retrieval far more frequently than lower settings on identical inputs. The study also demonstrates that suppressing retrieval narrows performance differences between models, suggesting that memorization inflates apparent capability differences and that genuine predictive ability is not simply a function of how many values a model has memorized.

STARDaki: a consensus-based STARD extension for standardized reporting of diagnostic accuracy in acute kidney injury.

Yu H, Li Y, Mo GP et al.. STARDaki: a consensus-based STARD extension for standardized reporting of diagnostic accuracy in acute kidney injury.. Intensive care medicine. 2026

This paper addressed the problem of heterogeneous and contradictory evidence surrounding biomarkers used to predict, diagnose, and prognosticate acute kidney injury (AKI). The researchers conducted a systematic literature review of diagnostic test accuracy studies on AKI biomarkers, evaluating their methodological quality and reporting completeness using established checklists (QUADAS-2 and STARD 2015), and then convened an international expert panel to develop consensus-based reporting standards through a modified Delphi process. The review found that the vast majority of identified studies were of insufficient quality or poorly reported, with compliance with existing STARD standards too low to permit meta-analysis. In response, the expert panel developed STARDaki, an extension of the STARD checklist tailored specifically to AKI biomarker studies, incorporating agreed criteria for patient selection, reference standards, and test-retest reliability reporting. The authors conclude that current AKI biomarker research is hampered by poor reporting practices, and that adoption of STARDaki should improve the quality, clinical applicability, and generalizability of future studies in this field.

Biomarkers for Pediatric Acute Respiratory Distress Syndrome: A Systematic Review.

Yang F, Zou J, Zheng L et al.. Biomarkers for Pediatric Acute Respiratory Distress Syndrome: A Systematic Review.. Journal of inflammation research. 2026

This systematic review investigated the potential of biomarkers for diagnosing, predicting, and assessing prognosis in pediatric acute respiratory distress syndrome, a severe and common complication in critically ill children where early recognition remains challenging. The authors conducted a comprehensive search across nine databases and assessed study quality using established tools, extracting data on biomarker characteristics, sampling methods, and outcome measures. Nine biomarkers were identified across four pathophysiological categories — epithelial injury, stress response, inflammatory response, and endothelial injury — with the majority demonstrating strong to moderate diagnostic and predictive performance based on area under the curve values. Despite this promise, the authors found that clinical utility was inconsistent across studies, and the overall evidence base was limited to a small number of moderate-to-high quality studies involving a relatively modest cohort of critically ill children. The review concludes that large-scale, multicenter prospective studies combined with advanced technologies are needed to validate existing biomarkers and identify new candidates to improve PARDS prediction, diagnosis, and management.

Advances in Pathogen Detection by Nanosensors: Biorecognition Strategies, Signal Amplification, and Platform Engineering

Aroofa Zargul, He Liu, Weiyu Zhang et al.. Advances in Pathogen Detection by Nanosensors: Biorecognition Strategies, Signal Amplification, and Platform Engineering. ACS Nano. 2026

This review examines the current state of nanosensor technologies for detecting infectious disease pathogens, motivated by the growing global burden of infectious diseases and antimicrobial resistance. The authors survey three interconnected areas: biorecognition elements such as antibodies, aptamers, bacteriophages, and molecularly imprinted polymers that enable selective pathogen identification; signal amplification strategies including nanomaterial-based enhancement, enzymatic cascades, and isothermal nucleic acid amplification capable of achieving detection at single-cell and femtomolar sensitivity levels; and integrated platform engineering combining CRISPR-Cas systems, artificial intelligence, and microfluidics for multiplexed point-of-care deployment. Performance across these approaches is assessed using standardized metrics including limit of detection, assay speed, specificity, and ease of use. The authors conclude that the convergence of these three domains presents a strategic framework for next-generation diagnostics that could substantially advance infectious disease surveillance and guide therapeutic decision-making, while also identifying persistent translational challenges that must be addressed before widespread clinical implementation.

Molecular Diagnostics for WHO Priority Bacterial Pathogens: A Bibliometric Mapping of Diagnostic Platforms, Resistance Markers, and Antimicrobial Resistance Research Trends.

Ünlü S. Molecular Diagnostics for WHO Priority Bacterial Pathogens: A Bibliometric Mapping of Diagnostic Platforms, Resistance Markers, and Antimicrobial Resistance Research Trends.. MicrobiologyOpen. 2026

This study mapped the scientific literature on molecular diagnostics applied to WHO priority bacterial pathogens, examining how diagnostic platforms, resistance markers, and antimicrobial resistance research have evolved together over time. Using a bibliometric approach on Scopus-indexed publications spanning a quarter century, the authors analyzed nearly two thousand articles through specialized software to identify thematic clusters, temporal trends, and leading contributors. The field expanded markedly after 2018, with methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus, Mycobacterium tuberculosis, and carbapenem-resistant Enterobacterales forming the dominant research cores, while conventional PCR-based nucleic acid amplification and whole-genome sequencing emerged as the principal diagnostic platforms. The authors found that the field has shifted from simple pathogen detection toward a translational domain encompassing resistance prediction, genomic epidemiology, and clinical decision support. They conclude that effective diagnostic development and stewardship require hybrid workflows that couple rapid resistance-marker assays with genome-based characterization, particularly to extend coverage to underrepresented organisms and settings.

Integrated evaluation of rapid diagnostic testing, genotypic-phenotypic resistance profiling, and AI-Assisted prediction models for antimicrobial stewardship and clinical outcomes in a resource-limited setting.

Hammad M, Arif R, Fardoos S et al.. Integrated evaluation of rapid diagnostic testing, genotypic-phenotypic resistance profiling, and AI-Assisted prediction models for antimicrobial stewardship and clinical outcomes in a resource-limited setting.. PloS one. 2026

This prospective observational study examined whether combining rapid diagnostic testing, molecular resistance gene profiling, and an artificial intelligence prediction model could improve antibiotic prescribing and patient outcomes compared to conventional susceptibility testing alone. Conducted at a hospital in Pakistan, the study enrolled adult patients with suspected bacterial infections and assessed outcomes including time to effective therapy, antibiotic appropriateness, length of hospital stay, and mortality. The integrated approach dramatically shortened the time to pathogen identification and to initiation of effective therapy, while substantially increasing the proportion of patients receiving appropriate antibiotics and reducing broad-spectrum antibiotic use. Clinical outcomes also improved, with shorter hospital stays and lower mortality observed in patients managed through the integrated strategy, and the AI model showed strong discriminatory performance for predicting antimicrobial resistance. The authors conclude that this combined diagnostic and predictive framework represents a potentially cost-effective and scalable strategy for combating antimicrobial resistance in resource-limited healthcare settings.

PMAxx dye-assisted colorimetric dual DNAzyme-LAMP (PD-cDDLAMP) for rapid phenotypic antimicrobial susceptibility testing of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt;.

Sewid AH, Ramos JH, Gelalcha BD et al.. PMAxx dye-assisted colorimetric dual DNAzyme-LAMP (PD-cDDLAMP) for rapid phenotypic antimicrobial susceptibility testing of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt;.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

This paper addressed the need for faster and simpler antimicrobial susceptibility testing (AST) by developing a nucleic acid-based phenotypic method called PD-cDDLAMP. The approach combines a photoreactive DNA-crosslinking dye (PMAxx) with a colorimetric dual DNAzyme loop-mediated isothermal amplification (LAMP) readout, exploiting the fact that PMAxx selectively penetrates membrane-compromised, antibiotic-susceptible bacteria and crosslinks their DNA, thereby suppressing amplification signals while leaving resistant bacteria's DNA available for detection. This design enables a clear "signal-on/signal-off" colorimetric distinction between resistant and susceptible bacteria after only thirty minutes of antibiotic exposure, far shorter than the hours required by comparable LAMP-based phenotypic AST methods. Tested against Escherichia coli exposed to ampicillin and tetracycline, the assay demonstrated high sensitivity, specificity, and accuracy, and was successfully validated in spiked milk samples. The method's minimal instrumentation requirements and short workflow make it a promising point-of-care tool for both clinical and agricultural settings in the context of antimicrobial resistance surveillance.

Rapid diagnostics innovations for urinary tract infections using molecular biology, artificial intelligence and antimicrobial resistance surveillance: a comprehensive review.

Goyal S, Sharma P, Aggarwal D et al.. Rapid diagnostics innovations for urinary tract infections using molecular biology, artificial intelligence and antimicrobial resistance surveillance: a comprehensive review.. Molecular biology reports. 2026

This review examines the landscape of emerging diagnostic technologies for urinary tract infections, a globally prevalent bacterial infection complicated by high recurrence, polymicrobial etiology, biofilm formation, and rapidly expanding antimicrobial resistance. The authors survey a range of molecular biology innovations including nucleic acid amplification techniques, biosensor-based platforms, microfluidic lab-on-chip systems, and omics-based approaches, evaluating their capacity to enable rapid, sensitive, and pathogen-specific detection compared to slow traditional culture-based methods. A central focus is the integration of these molecular tools with artificial intelligence and machine learning models to accelerate diagnostic outcomes and improve antibiotic susceptibility testing. The review argues that these combined technologies can enhance diagnostic precision, guide targeted antimicrobial therapy, and strengthen antimicrobial resistance surveillance programs. Challenges surrounding medical ethics, regulatory frameworks, and practical implementation are also discussed alongside the translational potential of next-generation diagnostics for more effective resistance management in urinary tract infection care.

Rapid molecular detection, prevalence and antimicrobial resistance of Ureaplasma urealyticum in genital tract specimens: a study at a district general hospital in China.

Zhao L, Jiang Y, Zong Y et al.. Rapid molecular detection, prevalence and antimicrobial resistance of Ureaplasma urealyticum in genital tract specimens: a study at a district general hospital in China.. Diagnostic microbiology and infectious disease. 2026

This study investigated the prevalence, antimicrobial resistance, and rapid molecular detection of *Ureaplasma urealyticum* in genital tract specimens from outpatients presenting with genitourinary complaints at a district general hospital in China. Researchers applied a simultaneous amplification and testing (SAT) method to detect the pathogen, evaluating its diagnostic performance against culture-based methods and real-time fluorescence qPCR, while also conducting antimicrobial susceptibility testing on culture-positive isolates. A substantial proportion of samples tested positive for *U. urealyticum* by culture, and resistance rates varied considerably across the antibiotics tested, with the highest resistance observed against ofloxacin and sparfloxacin, while minocycline and doxycycline retained the greatest activity. The SAT method demonstrated high sensitivity and specificity, performing comparably to qPCR with no statistically significant differences in either metric. The authors conclude that SAT represents a promising diagnostic alternative for rapid detection of *U. urealyticum* infection, offering practical advantages in speed and potential cost-effectiveness despite not outperforming qPCR.

Evaluation of an adapted relative growth method by MALDI-TOF MS for rapid determination of the susceptibility of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; to levofloxacin.

Liu Y, Xie N, Hu W et al.. Evaluation of an adapted relative growth method by MALDI-TOF MS for rapid determination of the susceptibility of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; to levofloxacin.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

This study addressed the urgent need for faster antimicrobial susceptibility testing by developing a rapid diagnostic platform called AI MedLab MS to determine levofloxacin susceptibility in *Escherichia coli*. The platform integrates dynamic MALDI-TOF mass spectrometry spectral analysis with a robust dynamic relative growth algorithm and machine learning, capturing how bacterial mass spectral profiles evolve over time during antibiotic exposure rather than relying on single-timepoint measurements. A key methodological innovation was analyzing the entire bacterial population rather than individual colonies, thereby accounting for intrasample heterogeneity and improving detection of resistant subpopulations. The system was developed using clinical isolates and prospectively validated on an independent set, delivering susceptibility results within two hours with an overall accuracy of around eighty percent and notably high precision for resistant isolates. The authors conclude that this dynamic trajectory-based approach overcomes core limitations of conventional methods and has the potential to support timely clinical decision-making and antimicrobial stewardship.

Executive Summary: Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026

Hallie C. Prescott, Massimo Antonelli, Waleed Alhazzani et al.. Executive Summary: Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026. Critical Care Medicine. 2026

The 2026 Surviving Sepsis Campaign guidelines represent a comprehensive update to international recommendations for the identification and management of sepsis and septic shock in adult patients, covering evidence published through mid-2025. The guidelines were developed using GRADE methodology, encompassing screening, infection management, hemodynamic support, respiratory care, adjunctive therapies, and long-term outcomes across a total of statements that are classified as strong or conditional recommendations, good practice statements, or acknowledgments of insufficient evidence. Notable new recommendations include prehospital sepsis screening and antibiotic administration for patients with long transport times, selective use of anaerobic antibiotic coverage based on infection risk factors, active fluid removal following the acute resuscitation phase, and selective digestive decontamination in settings with low antimicrobial resistance prevalence. Key changes from the 2021 guidelines include a shift toward crystalloids alone over supplemental albumin for fluid resuscitation, updated guidance permitting either invasive or noninvasive blood pressure monitoring in septic shock, and a streamlined recommendation against routine empiric antifungal therapy. Several prior recommendations were upgraded or downgraded in strength or certainty, including a strengthened recommendation for prolonged beta-lactam infusion and antibiotic de-escalation, and a downgraded recommendation for norepinephrine as the sole first-line vasopressor agent.

Accelerating scientific discovery with Co-Scientist

Juraj Gottweis, Wei‐Hung Weng, Alexander Daryin et al.. Accelerating scientific discovery with Co-Scientist. Nature. 2026

This paper introduces Co-Scientist, a multi-agent artificial intelligence system built on the Gemini platform, designed to augment scientific discovery by generating, critiquing, and refining novel research hypotheses conditioned on user-defined research objectives and existing scientific evidence. The system employs an asynchronous task execution framework for flexible compute scaling alongside a tournament-based evolution process through which hypothesis quality improves over time with increased test-time compute. Although designed as a general-purpose tool, the system was validated in three biomedical domains: drug repurposing, novel target discovery, and elucidating mechanisms of antimicrobial resistance. Notably, Co-Scientist identified new drug-repurposing candidates and synergistic combination therapies for acute myeloid leukaemia that were subsequently confirmed through in vitro experiments. These results suggest that such multi-agent AI systems have meaningful potential to accelerate real-world scientific discovery across complex biomedical problems.

Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration

Thirumalaisamy Suryaprabha, Chunghyeon Choi, Yanfang Wu et al.. Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration. npj Flexible Electronics. 2026

This review examines the current state of smart wearable and implantable biosensors designed for continuous, real-time health monitoring in the context of personalized and preventive medicine. The paper surveys advances in flexible, stretchable, and biocompatible materials that allow devices to integrate seamlessly with the body over extended periods, as well as multimodal sensing approaches capable of tracking multiple biophysical and biochemical signals simultaneously. The role of artificial intelligence in improving signal processing and generating predictive health insights is also assessed. Despite significant progress, the review identifies persistent challenges including motion artifacts, energy autonomy, data privacy, and the clinical interpretability of sensor outputs. The paper synthesizes developments across materials science, device architecture, and AI-assisted strategies to provide a comprehensive overview of the field.

Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications

Deepak S. Chauhan, Rajendra Prasad, Mukesh Dhanka et al.. Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines nanoparticle-based phototherapy systems, focusing on how light-activated nanoparticles can be engineered to treat cancer and chronic diseases including cardiovascular, neurodegenerative, metabolic, and autoimmune conditions. The authors survey core components and delivery platforms—such as gold nanoparticles, liposomes, and dendrimers—that enhance light absorption, improve targeting, and amplify therapeutic effects like reactive oxygen species generation in photodynamic therapy and localized heating in photothermal therapy. At the molecular level, the review dissects how these light-triggered interventions induce oxidative stress, activate apoptotic and autophagic cascades, and modulate immune responses by altering cytokine profiles and T-cell activity. The authors also critically evaluate regulatory-approved agents and recent clinical trial findings, identifying key barriers to broader clinical adoption including nanoparticle optimization, effective light delivery, and regulatory challenges. Overall, the review positions nanoparticle-based phototherapy as a transformative, noninvasive strategy with strong potential to reshape treatment across a wide spectrum of diseases.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether machine learning can reconstruct the primordial density field of the Universe from late-time line-intensity mapping observations during the Epoch of Reionization. The authors used simulated 21-cm and CO line-intensity maps generated at redshift approximately eight to train a three-dimensional U-Net architecture, aiming to recover initial conditions at redshift around 300 while incorporating realistic instrumental effects representative of next-generation surveys. The two tracers were chosen deliberately for their complementarity, with 21-cm emission sampling underdense neutral regions and CO tracing overdense star-forming environments. In idealized conditions, combining both tracers yields high cross-correlation with the true initial density field across a broad range of scales, and even under realistic noise the combined approach maintains meaningful reconstruction performance at large scales. Crucially, performing simulation-based inference of cosmological parameters from power-spectrum summaries after reconstruction tightens constraints on key parameters such as the amplitude of matter fluctuations and the spectral index by approximately a factor of two, demonstrating that joint multi-tracer analysis paired with this reconstruction approach can recover cosmological information otherwise obscured by nonlinear evolution and astrophysical processes.

WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data

Ji Soo Lee, Xilun Chen, Pierce Chuang et al.. WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a benchmark designed to evaluate whether AI systems can reason over real longitudinal wearable health data from actual users. The benchmark consists of thousands of multiple-choice questions derived from wearable time series, blood biomarkers, and demographic information spanning hundreds of days per user, preserving authentic data characteristics such as device noise and individual variability. Questions are organized across two reasoning axes—data versus health reasoning, and single- versus cross-signal reasoning—and are constructed using a dual-grounding framework that combines literature-based physiological findings with statistically validated population-level patterns. Evaluation of a range of proprietary and open-source large language models reveals substantial variation in performance, with most models falling well below ceiling accuracy and the benchmark remaining largely unsolved. The work positions this benchmark as a realistic and diagnostic tool for assessing the health reasoning capabilities of language models over real-world wearable data.

MEOX: Compact Multimodal Mixture-of-Experts for Earth Observation

Mohanad Albughdadi. MEOX: Compact Multimodal Mixture-of-Experts for Earth Observation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces MEOX, a compact multimodal masked autoencoder designed for Earth observation that can handle heterogeneous sensors and missing data without requiring a large parameter count. The architecture uses sensor-specific adapters, sparse mixture-of-experts blocks with shared and private low-rank components, and metadata tokens to enable modality-flexible representation learning within a very small parameter budget. The model was pretrained on a large multimodal Earth observation dataset using modality-balanced masked reconstruction and structured sensor dropout, then evaluated on multiple remote sensing classification and segmentation benchmarks under both frozen and finetuned transfer conditions. MEOX achieves competitive or superior performance compared to larger existing models on several benchmark tasks, including land cover segmentation and scene classification, demonstrating that strong sensor-flexible representations can be learned compactly. Diagnostic analyses further reveal how expert routing differentiates by modality, spatial location, and function, while metadata tokens provide measurable gains in downstream probing tasks.

Moral Advice as Interactional Negotiation: Framing, User Pressure, and Social Position in Large Language Model Responses

Minne Chen, Yourong Yao. Moral Advice as Interactional Negotiation: Framing, User Pressure, and Social Position in Large Language Model Responses. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated how large language models provide moral advice in contested situations, focusing on how framing, user pressure, and social characteristics of the advice-seeker shape model responses over the course of a conversation. Using a factorial vignette experiment, the researchers presented an AI model with eldercare dilemmas that varied in how they were framed and who was described as facing them, then systematically challenged the model's initial positions across multiple conversational rounds. They found that framing strongly influenced baseline stances, with caregiving-oriented framing producing near-uniform endorsement that proved highly susceptible to user pressure, while non-caregiving framing generated more variable but also more resistant response trajectories. Social position mattered as well, with female personas and the presence of sisters increasing the likelihood that the model would accommodate non-caregiving choices. The authors conclude that LLM moral advice functions as an interactional negotiation rather than the expression of a stable ethical framework, raising concerns that users may mistake this pressure-sensitive and socially differentiated output for objective guidance.

Does Your Agent's Memory Survive a Model Upgrade? A Controlled Study of Memory Portability

Ankit Goyal, Jaideep Ray. Does Your Agent's Memory Survive a Model Upgrade? A Controlled Study of Memory Portability. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated whether the memory stores used by AI agents remain functional after the underlying language model is upgraded, a practically important but largely unstudied problem. The researchers compared four memory representation strategies—verbatim long-context histories, retrieval-augmented generation with chunked text, model-compressed natural-language notes, and fixed-schema knowledge graphs—across synthetic agent histories using controlled swap experiments with two small open-weight models. They found that fixed-schema knowledge graphs transferred with near-zero accuracy change across model swaps, making them the most portable format, while compressed notes showed strong coupling to the model that wrote them, with accuracy shifting substantially and asymmetrically depending on migration direction. In retrieval-augmented systems, mixing embeddings from different model versions captured only a fraction of the accuracy gain achievable through complete re-embedding, and diagnostic analysis revealed that most errors in notes-based memory stem from information lost at the time of writing rather than from retrieval failures. Critically, attempting to repair degraded memory using only the stored notes consistently failed to meet recovery targets, whereas retaining the original raw history enabled successful repair in the majority of tested cases, underscoring the practical importance of preserving source data through model transitions.

RoboSPA: Can VLA Models Go Beyond Simple Scenes and Short-Horizon Tasks?

Zhenxuan Fan, Bo Zhang, Yutong Lin et al.. RoboSPA: Can VLA Models Go Beyond Simple Scenes and Short-Horizon Tasks?. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether Vision-Language-Action models can handle the spatial and procedural complexity required for realistic robotic manipulation, beyond the simple scenes and short-horizon tasks on which they are typically evaluated. The authors introduce RoboSPA, a large-scale dataset and benchmark designed to assess two core reasoning dimensions: fine-grained spatial reasoning and long-horizon procedural planning, organized across task categories and difficulty levels that systematically increase spatial ambiguity and procedural complexity. A large collection of trajectories was gathered across multiple robot embodiments and diverse environments, and the benchmark introduces diagnostic metrics that go beyond binary task success to provide more detailed insight into model failures. Experiments on representative VLA models reveal that current systems consistently struggle with complex spatial relations, precise low-level execution, and memory-intensive planning. The authors present RoboSPA as a challenging diagnostic benchmark intended to guide development of more capable and generalizable embodied agents.

AI-Assisted Molecular Biosensors: Design Strategies for Wearable and Real-Time Monitoring

Sishi Zhu, Jie Zhang, Xuming He et al.. AI-Assisted Molecular Biosensors: Design Strategies for Wearable and Real-Time Monitoring. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review paper examines how artificial intelligence has been integrated into the design and operation of molecular biosensors, with a particular focus on wearable and real-time monitoring applications. The authors systematically survey how AI contributes across multiple stages of biosensor development, including biomarker discovery, receptor design, sensing material engineering, and signal processing for optical, electrochemical, and microfluidic platforms. AI-driven approaches such as forward prediction and inverse design frameworks are highlighted for optimizing plasmonic and nanophotonic structures, while machine learning techniques address challenges like noise reduction, dimensionality reduction, and multimodal data fusion. The review places special emphasis on wearable biosensors, where difficulties including weak signal strength, motion artifacts, and variable physiological conditions demand robust, intelligent solutions. The authors conclude by identifying current limitations and future opportunities in the broader field of intelligent biosensing technology.

Advancing non-faradaic impedance biosensors: sensitivity enhancement strategies using microfluidics, multiscale labeling, and CMOS technology

Nam-Seog Kim, Jungkyu Kim. Advancing non-faradaic impedance biosensors: sensitivity enhancement strategies using microfluidics, multiscale labeling, and CMOS technology. Microsystems & Nanoengineering. 2026

This review examines non-Faradaic impedance biosensors, which detect analytes by measuring changes in electrical properties without requiring electrochemical reactions, making them simpler to operate than their Faradaic counterparts but traditionally limited by poor sensitivity. The authors survey three key strategies for overcoming this sensitivity limitation: enhancement using micro- and nanoparticle labeling, integration with microfluidic systems to improve analyte transport to the sensor surface, and incorporation of complementary metal-oxide-semiconductor technology for high-resolution on-chip detection. By analyzing how these approaches work individually and in combination, the review demonstrates that their synergistic application has enabled detection of proteins at femtomolar concentrations and bacteria at the single-cell level. The authors evaluate the strengths and limitations of each strategy and identify emerging directions in materials and system integration. The review ultimately aims to provide a development roadmap for next-generation non-Faradaic biosensing platforms applicable to healthcare diagnostics, environmental monitoring, and biomedical research.

Fabrication of photoresist SU-8 pyrolyzed carbon electrodes and their integration in a microfluidic hematocrit detection system.

Lee HY, Rogers JA, Kendrick C et al.. Fabrication of photoresist SU-8 pyrolyzed carbon electrodes and their integration in a microfluidic hematocrit detection system.. Biomedical microdevices. 2026

This paper investigated the fabrication of pyrolytic carbon electrodes derived from the photoresist SU-8 and their application in microfluidic hematocrit detection. The researchers developed a three-step pyrolysis protocol to overcome adhesion, geometric distortion, and limited surface area problems associated with conventional two-step pyrolysis of low-aspect-ratio planar electrode designs. The resulting carbon electrodes were integrated into a polydimethylsiloxane microfluidic device, where hematocrit was determined by measuring current responses from red blood cells suspended in phosphate-buffered saline, revealing a linear relationship between current and red blood cell concentration. Surface characterization showed that the carbon electrodes possessed substantially greater roughness and thickness compared to platinum electrodes, yielding increased electroactive surface area that reduced charge-transfer and concentration overpotentials and improved electrochemical performance. The device achieved precision and accuracy for hematocrit determination comparable to platinum-based systems, demonstrating that this low-cost, scalable fabrication approach is a viable alternative to noble metal electrodes for integrated diagnostic microdevices.

Zinc finger protein arrays in an epoxy-functionalized, pump-free microfluidic device for amplification-free, colorimetric detection of double-stranded DNA.

Bhuiyan SAM, Shumway R, Trinkle C et al.. Zinc finger protein arrays in an epoxy-functionalized, pump-free microfluidic device for amplification-free, colorimetric detection of double-stranded DNA.. The Analyst. 2026

This study developed a microfluidic platform for detecting pathogen-specific double-stranded DNA without requiring amplification, denaturation, or labeling steps. The researchers engineered zinc finger proteins targeting the *stx2* gene of *E. coli* O157 and immobilized them onto epoxy-functionalized surfaces within a pump-free, capillary-driven microfluidic device, coupling detection to sequence-enabled reassembly of β-lactamase to produce a colorimetric readout. The capillary flow behavior followed Lucas-Washburn dynamics, enabling predictable, instrument-free fluid handling, and the assay produced a visible yellow-to-red color shift upon target binding within approximately ten minutes. The platform achieved a low picomolar limit of detection and demonstrated high specificity, generating distinctly elevated signals only for the cognate target sequence compared to non-target and irrelevant DNA. Overall, the work establishes a practical framework for translating programmable protein-DNA recognition into a low-cost, point-of-care diagnostic tool for direct pathogen detection.

Microfluidic and Paper-Based Recombinase Polymerase Amplification Systems for Decentralized Diagnostics and Biosurveillance.

Wang HM, Lee SZ, Fu LM. Microfluidic and Paper-Based Recombinase Polymerase Amplification Systems for Decentralized Diagnostics and Biosurveillance.. Micromachines. 2026

This review examines recombinase polymerase amplification as a molecular diagnostic strategy suited to decentralized settings, focusing on how its isothermal, low-temperature operation makes it compatible with portable and resource-limited platforms. The authors surveyed recent progress in integrating RPA with microfluidic and paper-based formats, including centrifugal chips, capillary-driven devices, electrochemical biosensors, CRISPR-coupled assays, and sample-to-answer cartridges. Applications covered span biomedical diagnostics for viral, bacterial, and parasitic diseases as well as food safety, environmental monitoring, and One Health biosurveillance. The review highlights an ongoing shift toward intelligent diagnostic workflows that incorporate smartphone imaging, artificial intelligence-assisted interpretation, digital partitioning, and cloud connectivity to enhance portability and decision support. The authors conclude that while the field is advancing rapidly, challenges in sample preparation, multiplex stability, reagent storage, and regulatory validation must be addressed before widespread practical deployment can be achieved.

Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration

Thirumalaisamy Suryaprabha, Chunghyeon Choi, Yanfang Wu et al.. Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration. npj Flexible Electronics. 2026

This review examines the landscape of smart wearable and implantable biosensors designed for continuous, real-time monitoring of biophysical and biochemical signals in the context of personalized and preventive healthcare. The paper surveys advances in flexible, stretchable, and biocompatible materials that enable long-term comfort and stable integration with the body, alongside multimodal sensing approaches that improve measurement robustness. Enhancing heat and mass transfer in hybrid nanofluid with gyrotactic microbes and local thermal non equlibrium effects using artificial neural network

Mouloud Aoudia, Faiza Benabdallah, Salah Uddin Khan et al.. Enhancing heat and mass transfer in hybrid nanofluid with gyrotactic microbes and local thermal non equlibrium effects using artificial neural network. Discover Nano. 2026

This study examined the bioconvection flow of a hybrid nanofluid over a stretching sheet containing gyrotactic microorganisms, incorporating local thermal non-equilibrium effects alongside magnetic fields, thermal radiation, and Hall current. The governing nonlinear partial differential equations were reduced to ordinary differential equations via similarity transformations and solved numerically using the bvp4c method, with an artificial neural network trained via Bayesian regularization backpropagation used to predict and validate the results. Performance of the neural network model was evaluated through regression analysis, error histograms, and mean square error metrics across training, testing, and validation phases. A key finding is that increasing the interphase heat transfer parameter raises the liquid-phase thermal profile while simultaneously reducing the solid-phase thermal profile, reflecting the distinct thermal responses under non-equilibrium conditions. The framework is proposed as applicable to microscale drug delivery, lab-on-chip devices, micro-solar collectors, and biomedical cooling systems where precise control of heat, mass, and microorganism transfer is required.

Beyond Extracellular Vesicle (EV) Hype: Practical Solutions and Remaining Hurdles in EV Research, Manufacturing, and Clinical Translation

David J. Lundy, Zoe L. Chau, Sheng‐You Chen et al.. Beyond Extracellular Vesicle (EV) Hype: Practical Solutions and Remaining Hurdles in EV Research, Manufacturing, and Clinical Translation. Advanced Science. 2026

This review examines the current state of extracellular vesicle research, focusing on the analytical and translational challenges that constrain progress from laboratory discovery toward clinical application. The authors synthesize advances in EV isolation, detection, and single-particle analysis, which have uncovered increasing molecular and functional heterogeneity while also exposing fundamental limitations in how EV identity and biological activity are defined. Key bottlenecks identified include incomplete mechanistic understanding of EV biology, an absence of standardized measurement approaches, inconsistencies in regulatory classification, and difficulties in establishing operational definitions grounded in source material, isolation methods, and molecular markers. The review discusses updated regulatory frameworks and revisions to the MISEV guidelines, and presents updated workflow considerations spanning EV production and characterization, including orthogonal analytical methods, quantification of co-isolates, and potency assays designed to support reproducibility and quality control. The authors argue that advancing the field requires a shift from descriptive studies toward rigorous, quantitative measurement science capable of supporting EV preparations as reproducible therapeutic products.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether machine learning can reconstruct the primordial density field of the Universe from observations of the Epoch of Reionization, aiming to recover cosmological information otherwise obscured by non-linear structure formation. The researchers used simulated 21-cm and CO line-intensity maps at high redshift, generated with the LIMFAST simulation framework, as inputs to a three-dimensional U-Net neural network trained to infer initial conditions at a much earlier cosmic epoch. The two tracers were chosen deliberately for their complementarity, with 21-cm emission sensitive to neutral, underdense intergalactic gas and CO tracing dense, star-forming regions. When combining both tracers under realistic instrumental conditions mimicking planned surveys, the reconstruction maintained strong cross-correlation with the true initial density field across a useful range of spatial scales, and simulation-based inference showed that parameter uncertainties on the amplitude of matter fluctuations and the spectral index roughly halved compared to inference without reconstruction. The study concludes that joint multi-tracer analysis paired with such deep learning reconstruction offers a viable path to recovering otherwise inaccessible cosmological information from forthcoming line-intensity mapping surveys.

WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data

Ji Soo Lee, Xilun Chen, Pierce Chuang et al.. WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces WearableQA, a benchmark designed to evaluate whether AI systems can reason over real-world longitudinal wearable health data from actual users. The benchmark was constructed from wearable time series, blood biomarkers, and demographic information spanning hundreds of days per user, organized into multiple-choice questions covering sixteen question types that distinguish between data computation and physiological interpretation, as well as single-signal versus multi-signal reasoning. To generate reliable questions at scale, the authors developed a dual-grounding framework combining literature-derived physiological knowledge with statistically validated patterns observed in real-world wearable data. Evaluation of a range of proprietary and open-source large language models revealed substantial variation in performance and showed that the benchmark remains largely unsolved, with most models performing well below ceiling despite exceeding chance levels.

Customized spin spirals in ferromagnetic thin films

Anjali Panchwanee, Kai Schlage, Dieter Lott et al.. Customized spin spirals in ferromagnetic thin films. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated how to design and stabilize vertical spin spirals in ferromagnetic thin films without requiring external magnetic fields or complex multilayer stacks. The researchers used oblique-incidence deposition to introduce two crossed uniaxial magnetic anisotropies at the upper and lower boundaries of a single magnetic thin film, with each anisotropy independently tunable in direction and strength. By combining polarized neutron reflectometry with nuclear resonant scattering, they directly determined how the magnetic spin orientation varies as a function of depth through the film. They found that the resulting spin-spiral profile — including its angular range and depth dependence — can be precisely controlled at room temperature by adjusting these surface anisotropies as independent design parameters. The work establishes a single-film fabrication strategy for customizable non-collinear spin structures with potential relevance to nanoscale energy storage, magnetic sensing, and ferromagnetic-resonance filtering applications.

MEOX: Compact Multimodal Mixture-of-Experts for Earth Observation

Mohanad Albughdadi. MEOX: Compact Multimodal Mixture-of-Experts for Earth Observation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces MEOX, a compact multimodal masked autoencoder designed for Earth observation that can handle heterogeneous sensors and missing data without requiring a large parameter budget. The model uses sensor-specific adapters, sparse expert blocks with shared and private low-rank components, and metadata tokens to enable flexible multimodal fusion while constraining parameter growth, with rotary attention allowing inference at spatial resolutions different from pretraining. It was pretrained on over a million multimodal Earth observation samples using modality-balanced masked reconstruction and structured sensor dropout, then evaluated on multiple remote sensing classification and segmentation benchmarks. The model achieves competitive or superior performance compared to larger existing approaches on tasks including land cover segmentation and scene classification, demonstrating that strong sensor-flexible representations can be learned within a compact architecture. Diagnostic analyses of expert routing and retrieval experiments further characterize how the model organizes modality-specific and spatial information internally.

TherMosaic: Accelerating Perceived Thermal Transitions Through Spatiotemporal Thermal Feedback

Zining Zhang, Jiasheng Li, Myungin Lee et al.. TherMosaic: Accelerating Perceived Thermal Transitions Through Spatiotemporal Thermal Feedback. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigated how to make thermal feedback devices feel more responsive by reducing the perceptual lag that occurs when thermoelectric hardware changes temperature too slowly for real-time interactive applications. The researchers developed TherMosaic, a spatiotemporal approach that exploits two known perceptual phenomena—spatial summation and thermal adaptation—to accelerate perceived temperature transitions at the fingertip using an array of independently controlled Peltier modules. Through a series of controlled perceptual studies, they demonstrated that distributing thermal stimulation across multiple elements can maintain stable hot or cold sensations even when individual elements deviate from the target temperature, and that adaptation helps sustain these percepts during dynamic stimulation changes. Combining these mechanisms reduced perceived transition time by roughly a third to two-fifths compared to baseline conditions. The approach was then implemented in a wearable device and validated in virtual reality, where it successfully reduced perceived thermal lag and improved alignment between thermal and visual events during interactive use.

Does Your Agent's Memory Survive a Model Upgrade? A Controlled Study of Memory Portability

Ankit Goyal, Jaideep Ray. Does Your Agent's Memory Survive a Model Upgrade? A Controlled Study of Memory Portability. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether AI agent memory systems remain functional when the underlying language model is upgraded, a scenario that is common in practice but rarely studied systematically. The researchers compared four memory storage strategies—verbatim long-context history, retrieval-augmented generation with chunked text, model-compressed natural-language notes, and a fixed-schema knowledge graph—by testing how well each format transfers across model swaps using synthetic conversation histories with known ground-truth answers. They found that fixed-schema knowledge graphs transferred almost perfectly across model upgrades, while compressed natural-language notes showed strong coupling to the model that wrote them, with accuracy shifting substantially and asymmetrically depending on migration direction. In retrieval-augmented systems, mixing old and new embedding versions in a single index captured only a fraction of the accuracy gains achievable through complete re-embedding, and diagnostic analysis revealed that most note-based failures stemmed from information lost during initial compression rather than retrieval problems. The study concludes that preserving raw source histories is essential for memory repair after model upgrades, that migration testing must account for direction-specific effects, and that embedding spaces should be kept strictly isolated during transitions.

AI-Assisted Molecular Biosensors: Design Strategies for Wearable and Real-Time Monitoring

Sishi Zhu, Jie Zhang, Xuming He et al.. AI-Assisted Molecular Biosensors: Design Strategies for Wearable and Real-Time Monitoring. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review examines how artificial intelligence is reshaping the design and operation of molecular biosensors, with particular emphasis on wearable and real-time monitoring applications. The authors survey how AI-driven approaches accelerate biomarker discovery, guide the engineering of high-affinity receptors such as proteins and aptamers, and optimize plasmonic and nanophotonic sensing structures through forward prediction and inverse design frameworks. AI integration is shown to enhance multiple biosensing platforms—including optical, electrochemical, and microfluidic systems—by enabling automatic feature extraction, noise reduction, dimensionality reduction, and multimodal data fusion. These capabilities are highlighted as especially critical for wearable biosensors, where weak signals, motion artifacts, and variable physiological conditions demand high robustness and reliability. The review concludes by mapping current challenges and future opportunities for intelligent biosensing technologies in continuous health monitoring.

Advancing non-faradaic impedance biosensors: sensitivity enhancement strategies using microfluidics, multiscale labeling, and CMOS technology

Nam-Seog Kim, Jungkyu Kim. Advancing non-faradaic impedance biosensors: sensitivity enhancement strategies using microfluidics, multiscale labeling, and CMOS technology. Microsystems & Nanoengineering. 2026

This review examines non-Faradaic impedance biosensors, which detect changes in electrical properties without requiring electrochemical reactions, and addresses their historically limited sensitivity as a barrier to practical diagnostic applications. The authors survey three complementary enhancement strategies: micro- and nanoparticle-based signal amplification, microfluidic integration to improve analyte mass transport, and complementary metal-oxide-semiconductor technology to enable on-chip, high-resolution sensing. By analyzing how these approaches work individually and in combination, the review characterizes their respective strengths and limitations and assesses their synergistic potential. Combined implementation of these strategies has pushed detection capabilities to femtomolar concentrations for proteins and single-bacterium sensitivity, representing substantial gains over conventional non-Faradaic methods. The review concludes by mapping emerging directions in materials and system integration for applications spanning healthcare diagnostics, environmental monitoring, and biomedical research.

Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances

Pranay Amruth Maroju, Angad S. Sidhu, Amogh R. Motaganahalli et al.. Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances. Antibiotics. 2026

This review examines the biology, clinical significance, and therapeutic challenges of bacterial biofilms, which are structured microbial communities encased in a self-produced matrix of polysaccharides, proteins, extracellular DNA, and lipids. The authors synthesize current knowledge on biofilm development stages, the protective roles of the matrix, and the physiological heterogeneity that emerges during maturation, explaining how these features impede antimicrobial penetration and shield bacteria from host immunity. The review also catalogues the resistance mechanisms underlying biofilm tolerance, including limited antibiotic diffusion, enzymatic drug inactivation, efflux pump upregulation, persister cell formation, and horizontal gene transfer, and maps these onto clinically relevant conditions such as cystic fibrosis, chronic wounds, osteomyelitis, device-associated infections, and breast implant illness. Conventional and emerging detection and real-time monitoring approaches are discussed alongside therapeutic strategies that combine antibiotics with matrix-disrupting enzymes, quorum-sensing inhibitors, bacteriophages, metabolic reactivators, and nanotechnology-based delivery systems. The authors conclude that because biofilm persistence arises from multiple interconnected mechanisms, effective management demands integrated detection and multifaceted treatment strategies, with advances in multi-omics and systems-level modeling considered essential for next-generation prevention and therapy.

Advancements in biosensor Technologies for Pathogen Detection: Innovations and comparison with conventional diagnostic approaches.

Singhal P, Misra S, Prakash A et al.. Advancements in biosensor Technologies for Pathogen Detection: Innovations and comparison with conventional diagnostic approaches.. Journal of microbiological methods. 2026

This review paper examined the state of biosensor technologies for detecting pathogens across clinical, food safety, and environmental monitoring contexts, comparing them against established conventional diagnostic approaches. The authors surveyed electrochemical, optical, nanoparticle, microfluidic, and paper-based biosensor platforms, discussing their underlying sensing principles, design strategies, and respective advantages and limitations. Conventional methods such as culture-based techniques, ELISA, and PCR were highlighted as reliable but constrained by lengthy processing times, complex instrumentation, and the requirement for trained personnel, making them less suited for rapid or on-site deployment. Biosensors were found to offer compelling advantages including portability, high sensitivity, multiplexing capability, and point-of-care applicability, with recent advances in nanomaterials, biorecognition strategies, and signal-transduction mechanisms further expanding their detection potential. Nevertheless, the review identifies persistent challenges around sensor stability, reproducibility, fabrication scalability, and rigorous validation as critical barriers that must be addressed before widespread practical implementation can be realized.

Development of a NiCoFe-LDH/Ketjenblack-enhanced electrochemical antibacterial peptide biosensor for Aeromonas hydrophila detection in seawater samples.

Kızılkurtlu AA, Arancı K, Gülyüz S et al.. Development of a NiCoFe-LDH/Ketjenblack-enhanced electrochemical antibacterial peptide biosensor for Aeromonas hydrophila detection in seawater samples.. Mikrochimica acta. 2026

This paper developed an electrochemical biosensor for detecting the fish pathogen Aeromonas hydrophila in aquaculture settings, with particular focus on seawater applicability. The sensor was constructed by modifying a screen-printed carbon electrode with a nanocomposite of ternary NiCoFe layered double hydroxide combined with Ketjenblack carbon, synthesized via one-pot precipitation, and an antibacterial peptide was used as the biorecognition element to capture the target bacterium. The nanocomposite was characterized as a high-activity nanosheet structure facilitating rapid electron transfer, and the design couples signal amplification from the layered double hydroxide-carbon material with peptide-mediated bacterial recognition. Under optimized conditions the biosensor achieved detection within half an hour at room temperature, with a wide linear range, a very low limit of detection, minimal cross-reactivity with non-target bacteria, and strong performance when tested in spiked seawater samples. The authors report this as the first electrochemical, antibacterial peptide-based biosensor for A. hydrophila validated in seawater, highlighting its potential utility for rapid pathogen monitoring in aquaculture environments.

Self-Cleaning Electrochemical Biosensor Based on MCOF@Ag for Simultaneous Pathogen Detection and Photocatalytic Inactivation.

Bai Y, Xiao L, Cai L et al.. Self-Cleaning Electrochemical Biosensor Based on MCOF@Ag for Simultaneous Pathogen Detection and Photocatalytic Inactivation.. Analytical chemistry. 2026

This paper developed a dual-function electrochemical biosensor capable of both detecting and eliminating bacterial pathogens using a single electrode platform. The researchers synthesized a silver nanoparticle-decorated metal-covalent organic framework with a nanoflower-like structure, then functionalized it with aptamers specific to *Staphylococcus aureus* to enable selective bacterial capture and electrochemical quantification. The biosensor achieved highly sensitive detection of the target pathogen across a broad concentration range with an exceptionally low detection limit. Beyond detection, the material's photocatalytic activity under visible light enabled near-complete inactivation of captured bacteria through reactive oxygen species generation, after which the electrode could be regenerated and reused. The study demonstrates a strategy for integrating pathogen detection and photocatalytic disinfection into a single self-cleaning device, with implications for infectious disease surveillance and control.

Paper-Based Biosensor Using Dual-Recognition Molecules for Detection of &lt;i&gt;Staphylococcus aureus&lt;/i&gt; in Milk.

Hou W, Li X, Li J et al.. Paper-Based Biosensor Using Dual-Recognition Molecules for Detection of &lt;i&gt;Staphylococcus aureus&lt;/i&gt; in Milk.. Biosensors. 2026

This paper developed a paper-based biosensor for the rapid detection of *Staphylococcus aureus* in milk, addressing the need for simple, on-site methods to identify this common foodborne pathogen in dairy products. The biosensor employed a dual-recognition strategy combining a milk matrix-specific aptamer, selected through a SELEX process adapted to the milk environment, with polyclonal antibodies that capture the bacterium via multiple epitopes on the paper substrate. Colloidal gold nanoparticles served as visual signal reporters conjugated to the aptamer, enabling detection without any instrumentation. The system achieved a low detection limit and rapid turnaround, completing results within minutes. The authors conclude that the synergistic combination of aptamer and antibody recognition enhances both sensitivity and specificity, offering a cost-effective tool for dairy safety monitoring.

A modular APEX biosensor enables sensitive and visualized monitoring of viral protease activity.

Li B, Dong B, Chen Y et al.. A modular APEX biosensor enables sensitive and visualized monitoring of viral protease activity.. Virologica Sinica. 2026

The paper developed modular biosensors to detect viral protease activity within living cells and to screen antiviral drug candidates in physiologically relevant conditions. The researchers engineered two biosensors based on split ascorbate peroxidase fragments that reassemble upon cleavage of a protease recognition sequence, restoring enzymatic activity and generating amplified fluorescent signals. Using the coronavirus main protease as a model system, the sensors demonstrated extremely high signal-to-noise ratios and successfully avoided the false positives that plague traditional fluorescent protein and FRET-based approaches caused by non-specific conformational interference from test compounds. The platform accurately identified known high-potency inhibitors and could be readily adapted to detect proteases from diverse viruses, including multiple coronaviruses, enteroviruses, Epstein-Barr virus, and hepatitis A virus, simply by swapping the recognition sequence. The authors conclude that these biosensors offer a reliable, highly specific intracellular tool to accelerate broad-spectrum antiviral drug discovery against both emerging and established viral threats.

Recent Progress in Nanoparticle-Based Biosensors for Monitoring &lt;i&gt;Shigella&lt;/i&gt; spp. in Food Safety: A Critical Review.
