This review examines the development and application of nanoparticle-based biosensors for detecting Shigella spp., a highly infectious foodborne pathogen, in food safety contexts. The authors critically surveyed recent advances across multiple biosensor platforms—including optical, electrochemical, lateral flow, and hybrid systems—evaluating them in terms of signal amplification strategies, biorecognition elements, biological targets, and performance in real food matrices. Detection approaches span gene-level targeting, particularly of the virulence gene ipaH for reliable genus-level identification, and whole-cell recognition methods that allow faster analysis with minimal sample preparation. Optical biosensors such as fluorescence, SERS, and LSPR approaches demonstrated low detection limits and robustness in complex matrices, while electrochemical biosensors offered portability and operational simplicity suited to field screening. The review concludes that validation in real food matrices generally shows acceptable recoveries and agreement with reference methods, and it proposes a design-oriented framework to guide the selection of nanoparticle-based biosensors by integrating nanomaterial function, sensor design, and performance requirements.

Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives

Shehwaz Anwar, Hajed Obaid A. Alharbi, Ali Yousif Babiker et al.. Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review paper examines the dual role of reactive oxygen species in biological systems, where they act as essential signaling molecules at physiological concentrations but drive disease when produced in excess or dysregulated. The authors synthesize current knowledge on the major types, sources, and normal functions of reactive oxygen species alongside the molecular pathways through which oxidative stress causes cellular damage and signaling disruption. The review critically evaluates contemporary and emerging methods for detecting and quantifying oxidative stress, including biochemical assays, fluorescent probes, biosensors, and advanced imaging approaches. It also surveys the contribution of oxidative stress to the pathophysiology of cancer, diabetes, cardiovascular disease, neurodegeneration, and inflammatory conditions. The authors conclude by highlighting future directions in precision redox medicine, emphasizing targeted antioxidant-based diagnostic and therapeutic strategies informed by improved detection technologies and deeper mechanistic understanding of redox biology.

Harnessing biogenic nanoparticles for combating antibiotic resistance: green synthesis, mechanistic insights, and biotechnological applications

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Ibrahim Mohammed Hussaini et al.. Harnessing biogenic nanoparticles for combating antibiotic resistance: green synthesis, mechanistic insights, and biotechnological applications. Frontiers in Bioengineering and Biotechnology. 2026

This review examines biogenic nanoparticles as a potential strategy to address the growing global crisis of antimicrobial resistance, focusing on how green synthesis methods using plants, microorganisms, algae, and isolated biomolecules produce nanomaterials with inherent antimicrobial properties. The authors surveyed recent advances in the biosynthesis of multiple nanoparticle types, including silver, gold, copper oxide, zinc oxide, iron oxide, titanium dioxide, and selenium, exploring how biological metabolites govern the reduction of metal ions and the growth, size, morphology, and stabilization of the resulting particles. These biogenic nanoparticles were found to act through diverse antimicrobial mechanisms, including disruption of bacterial membranes, generation of reactive oxygen species, interference with metabolic pathways, damage to DNA and proteins, and inhibition of both quorum sensing and biofilm formation, with activity demonstrated against several WHO-priority and ESKAPE pathogens. Synergistic combinations of biogenic nanoparticles with antibiotics, essential oils, or phytochemicals showed potential for reducing required drug concentrations and improving efficacy against resistant strains, while physicochemical tunability has supported experimental applications in wound care, antimicrobial coatings, drug delivery, biosensing, and food packaging. The review concludes that despite these promising findings, significant obstacles remain, including inconsistent synthesis protocols, limited scalability, and insufficient toxicological and environmental safety data, all of which must be resolved before clinical translation can be seriously pursued.

Biohacking the human gut microbiome for precision health and therapeutic innovation

Jhommara Bautista, Andrés López-Cortés. Biohacking the human gut microbiome for precision health and therapeutic innovation. Frontiers in Microbiology. 2026

This narrative review examines how biohacking — the self-directed use of biotechnology, digital tools, and lifestyle interventions — intersects with gut microbiome science to enable individualized, precision-health approaches. The authors synthesize current evidence across dietary modulation, microbial therapeutics such as probiotics and fecal microbiota transplantation, and synthetic biology strategies including engineered bacterial strains and phage consortia, situating these within multi-omics and continuous-phenotyping frameworks. Mechanistically, the review identifies microbial metabolites including short-chain fatty acids, bile acid derivatives, and tryptophan catabolites as key endocrine-like mediators linking gut ecology to host immunity, metabolism, and neuroendocrine signaling, with translational relevance to metabolic disease, neurocognition, and oncology. The authors also highlight emerging digital infrastructures — wearables, biosensors, and AI-driven health twins — as enabling real-time, closed-loop modulation of host-microbe dynamics. They conclude by proposing a translational roadmap that addresses current barriers including methodological heterogeneity, regulatory fragmentation, safety concerns around unsupervised interventions, and cyberbiosecurity vulnerabilities, arguing that with appropriate governance and standardization, microbiome-focused biohacking could mature into a reproducible component of preventive and personalized medicine.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study confirmed two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets located near the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert, a region of orbital parameter space largely devoid of intermediate-sized planets, using space-based photometry and ground-based radial velocity measurements combined with Gaussian process modeling of stellar activity. Both planets orbit solar-type stars on very short periods, receive over a thousand times Earth's irradiation, and yet their measured densities demand substantial volatile content, which interior structure and atmospheric escape modeling indicates is best explained by water-dominated rather than hydrogen/helium envelopes, pointing to a steam-world interpretation. The systems also harbor additional companions, including an outer giant planet in one system, prompting a broader population analysis that revealed a striking statistical excess of outer giant companions around low-mass short-period planets specifically within the tidal survival band near the desert edge compared to those outside it. This companion excess supports high-eccentricity tidal migration as a key mechanism delivering planets to the lower desert edge, where gravitational interactions with outer giants can drive inward migration followed by orbital circularization. Together, the survival of volatile-rich planets in such extreme irradiation environments and the companion statistics challenge models that attribute the Neptunian desert boundary solely to atmospheric photoevaporation of primordial envelopes.

Ion abundances in the plasma tail of 3I/ATLAS show that it is N2-rich

Lea Ferellec, Cyrielle Opitom, Colin Snodgrass. Ion abundances in the plasma tail of 3I/ATLAS show that it is N2-rich. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated the chemical composition of the third interstellar object, 3I/ATLAS, by analyzing ion emissions in its plasma tail. Using integral-field spectroscopic observations obtained with WHT/WEAVE-LIFU shortly after perihelion, the researchers simultaneously detected multiple ionic species including N₂⁺, CO⁺, CO₂⁺, H₂O⁺, and CH⁺ in the anti-solar tail. By applying fluorescence factors to the measured ion abundances, they derived abundance ratios among these species and used the N₂⁺/CO⁺ ratio to establish a lower limit on the N₂/CO ratio in the comet. This ratio indicates that 3I/ATLAS is substantially enriched in N₂ relative to Solar System comets, a characteristic interpreted as evidence of cold formation conditions in its host planetary system. The overall pattern of ionic abundances is broadly consistent with 3I/ATLAS being enriched in hypervolatiles, offering a point of comparison between planetary formation processes in the Solar System and around other stars.

Propagation Model for SSC attacks: Why SBOM (tools) don't tell the whole truth

Ljubica Grgic, Lazar Maksimovic, Pavel Laskov. Propagation Model for SSC attacks: Why SBOM (tools) don't tell the whole truth. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates the limitations of Software Bill of Materials (SBOM) tools in securing software supply chains, arguing that these tools fail to capture how vulnerabilities propagate through an entire chain. The authors developed a four-stage propagation model covering structural exposure, vulnerability class presence, code reachability, and taint path analysis to better represent exploitability effects. They empirically evaluated four open-source SBOM tools against this model using three software projects and the Log4j vulnerability as a test case. Their findings reveal that current SBOM tools only reliably address the first two stages, while the more critical capabilities of code reachability analysis and taint path analysis are absent from the SBOM ecosystem. The authors conclude that centering propagation effects in software supply chain security research is necessary to prevent localized cyber risks from escalating into systemic threats.

Coincidence-based spectral engineering for spectral matching in cascaded downconversion

Mohammed Charafi, Alexandre Z. Leger, Samridhi Gambhir et al.. Coincidence-based spectral engineering for spectral matching in cascaded downconversion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates three-photon states produced through cascaded spontaneous parametric downconversion, where a key challenge is achieving spectral compatibility between successive nonlinear optical stages. The authors demonstrate that filtering the herald photon in coincidence-based detection conditionally reshapes the spectrum of its entangled partner, allowing it to match the acceptance bandwidth of the second nonlinear stage without modifying the sources themselves. Using an electro-optically gated spectrometer, they directly measured the conditional spectra governing the cascaded process and used these measurements to quantitatively predict improvements in second-stage conversion efficiency per detected herald. They verified these predictions through photon-triplet measurements, confirming enhanced performance at fixed heralding rates. The work establishes coincidence-based spectral engineering as a practical approach to coupling broadband and narrowband downconversion stages, especially when detector saturation or spectral mismatch would otherwise limit performance.

When LLM Decompilers Recompile More and Preserve Less

Chang Liu, Edward Raff, Kristopher Micinski. When LLM Decompilers Recompile More and Preserve Less. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examines a critical flaw in how large language model (LLM)-based decompilers are evaluated, specifically that recompilability and re-executability metrics can reward outputs that are syntactically clean yet semantically incorrect. The authors demonstrate that a decompiled function may successfully build and pass all provided tests while diverging from the original binary's behavior on other valid inputs, and that real security vulnerabilities present in the original code can silently disappear from the LLM's output. To detect these failures, they developed Decompile-Diverge, a behavioral comparison oracle that synthesizes test drivers and uses fuzzing to generate inputs from the reference binary, then checks whether the decompiled version produces matching behavior across that input corpus. Testing across multiple LLM decompilation systems, they found that functions passing all shipped tests still exhibit behavioral divergence in a meaningful fraction of cases, with rates reaching as high as roughly one in eight for some systems. Source-level analysis reveals that this divergence arises because LLMs replace the visible unknowns left by traditional decompilers with plausible-looking but incorrect constructs such as fabricated fields, types, callees, and guards.

Dust Substructures and Line Perturbations driven by a Forming Planet in J16120

Anibal Sierra, Andrés F. Izquierdo, Paola Pinilla et al.. Dust Substructures and Line Perturbations driven by a Forming Planet in J16120. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigated signs of planet formation in the protoplanetary disc surrounding the young star 2MASS J16120668-301027, building on previous multi-wavelength hints of an actively forming planet. Using high-resolution millimetre interferometric observations with ALMA, the researchers mapped both dust continuum emission and carbon monoxide line emission to probe the disc's structure and gas kinematics in detail. Visibility modelling of the continuum revealed an inner disc and two dust rings, with the overall morphology better described by an eccentric disc model than a symmetric one. Analysis of the gas kinematics uncovered a localized increase in velocity dispersion, kink-like features, and a transition between sub- and super-Keplerian velocities at the location and along the orbit of a previously identified circumplanetary disc candidate, consistent with predictions from planet–disc interaction simulations. Together, the dust substructures and kinematic signatures are interpreted as compelling evidence that a planet is actively forming within the disc gap.

Utility of multiplex PCR panels for optimum management of lower respiratory Infections Employing FilmArray Pneumonia Plus Testing-results of the UPLIFT study.

Venkitakrishnan R, Basheer BA, Cleetus M et al.. Utility of multiplex PCR panels for optimum management of lower respiratory Infections Employing FilmArray Pneumonia Plus Testing-results of the UPLIFT study.. Lung India : official organ of Indian Chest Society. 2026

This study investigated the clinical utility of a multiplex PCR panel (FilmArray Pneumonia Plus Panel) compared to conventional culture for diagnosing lower respiratory tract infections and guiding antimicrobial therapy in hospitalized patients. Researchers enrolled patients with lower respiratory tract infections, documenting pathogen detection rates, antimicrobial resistance gene identification, turnaround times, antibiotic modifications, and clinical outcomes for both diagnostic approaches. The multiplex PCR panel detected at least one pathogen in the substantial majority of specimens tested, far exceeding the detection rate achieved by conventional culture alone. Patients whose pathogens were identified by the panel experienced shorter durations of antibiotic therapy and briefer hospital stays compared to those with negative panel results, and antimicrobial regimens were successfully modified in a large proportion of panel-positive cases. The authors conclude that multiplex PCR testing offers superior diagnostic sensitivity, faster results, better resistance detection, and meaningful clinical benefits over conventional culture in the management of hospitalized lower respiratory tract infection patients.

Comparative detection of respiratory pathogens in pediatric and adult patients using a multiplex panel.

Kim YJ, Jang WS, Park S et al.. Comparative detection of respiratory pathogens in pediatric and adult patients using a multiplex panel.. Medicine. 2026

This study compared respiratory pathogen profiles between pediatric and adult patients presenting with acute respiratory symptoms during a period of heightened respiratory illness activity in 2024, following disruptions to viral circulation patterns caused by the coronavirus disease 2019 pandemic. Researchers tested specimens from over six hundred hospital patients using multiplex polymerase chain reaction assays capable of detecting both viral and bacterial pathogens, supplementing this with demographic data from electronic medical records. They found clear age-related differences in pathogen distribution, with enterovirus/rhinovirus being most prevalent among children and SARS-CoV-2 predominating in adults. Mycoplasma pneumoniae was more common in the pediatric group and showed a male predominance, while co-detection of multiple pathogens occurred in a notable subset of both age groups, including a specific co-detection pattern of M. pneumoniae with enterovirus/rhinovirus in pediatric cases. The study concludes that respiratory pathogen epidemiology differs meaningfully between children and adults, and provides useful surveillance data on detection and co-detection patterns across age groups.

Complementary detection of respiratory pathogens and antimicrobial-resistance markers by multiplex PCR alongside culture in a High-AMR Egyptian cohort.

Elmahdy YA, Elgebaly AS, Al-Adl M et al.. Complementary detection of respiratory pathogens and antimicrobial-resistance markers by multiplex PCR alongside culture in a High-AMR Egyptian cohort.. Journal of applied microbiology. 2026

This study examined whether combining multiplex molecular testing with conventional culture improves pathogen detection and antimicrobial resistance profiling in patients with lower respiratory tract infections in Egypt, a setting characterized by high rates of antimicrobial resistance. Using a retrospective cross-sectional design, researchers analyzed paired sputum and blood culture specimens processed through both standard culture methods and multiplex PCR panels capable of identifying pathogens and resistance markers simultaneously. Molecular testing detected at least one target in a substantially higher proportion of sputum specimens than culture alone, and the two methods showed considerable organism-level discordance, underscoring that each approach captures distinct diagnostic information. Resistance markers for extended-spectrum beta-lactamases and carbapenemases were frequently identified, and the presence of carbapenemases and Gram-negative organisms were independently associated with phenotypic resistance. The findings support integrating multiplex PCR alongside conventional culture to broaden pathogen detection and enhance resistance characterization in resource-limited, high-resistance clinical environments.

Scenario-Driven Rapid Testing for Top Pathogens in Pediatric Respiratory Infections: Clinical and Economic Value from Emergency Triage to Precision Anti-Infective Management in the PICU.

Chen J, Wang H, Li Y et al.. Scenario-Driven Rapid Testing for Top Pathogens in Pediatric Respiratory Infections: Clinical and Economic Value from Emergency Triage to Precision Anti-Infective Management in the PICU.. Pathogens (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This narrative review examines how rapid diagnostic testing for respiratory pathogens can be optimized in pediatric settings, from emergency triage through intensive care. The authors address a central paradox: despite expanding diagnostic technologies including rapid antigen tests, nucleic acid amplification tests, multiplex panels, and metagenomic sequencing, evidence from pediatric emergency trials shows that broad pathogen detection alone does not reliably reduce antibiotic prescribing or healthcare costs. To resolve this, the review proposes a scenario-driven framework in which "top pathogens" are defined contextually by age group, clinical syndrome, care setting, season, and factors such as treatment implications, transmissibility, and antimicrobial resistance relevance. The authors systematically discuss how testing strategies should differ across distinct clinical scenarios including emergency triage, ward management, severe pneumonia, PICU care, hospital-acquired and ventilator-associated pneumonia, and outbreak settings, and they outline the economic mechanisms through which appropriately targeted testing may generate value. The review concludes by proposing implementation principles grounded in diagnostic and antimicrobial stewardship, local epidemiology, and real-world cost-effectiveness evaluation, positioning the scenario-driven approach as a practical bridge between broad syndromic testing and precision infectious disease management in children.

Distribution Patterns of Non-SARS-CoV-2 Respiratory Pathogens Among Patients With Influenza-Like Illness During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Cross-Sectional Study From Bangladesh.

Sultana S, Taher A, Haque N et al.. Distribution Patterns of Non-SARS-CoV-2 Respiratory Pathogens Among Patients With Influenza-Like Illness During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Cross-Sectional Study From Bangladesh.. Cureus. 2026

This cross-sectional study investigated the non-SARS-CoV-2 respiratory pathogens responsible for influenza-like illness in adults who tested negative for SARS-CoV-2 at a tertiary care hospital in Bangladesh during the latter half of 2022, a period when diagnostic attention was heavily skewed toward COVID-19. Researchers enrolled adults presenting with influenza-like symptoms and analyzed nasopharyngeal swabs using multiplex real-time PCR capable of detecting a broad range of viral and bacterial pathogens. Over a third of participants tested positive for at least one non-SARS-CoV-2 pathogen, with human rhinovirus dominating among viral detections and Streptococcus pneumoniae dominating among bacterial detections. Co-infections were present in a subset of patients, with rhinovirus–influenza B and S. pneumoniae–Haemophilus influenzae pairings showing the strongest, though statistically non-significant after correction, co-occurrence trends. The authors conclude that broad-spectrum multiplex PCR testing should be incorporated into the routine workup of SARS-CoV-2-negative patients with persistent respiratory symptoms to capture the substantial burden of other circulating pathogens.

Enterococcus Species: Multifaceted Probiotic Potential and Safety Considerations

Ojonugwa Precious John, Kayode Olayinka Afolabi, Anayochukwu Chibuike Ngene et al.. Enterococcus Species: Multifaceted Probiotic Potential and Safety Considerations. Microorganisms. 2026

This paper examines the dual nature of *Enterococcus* species as both potentially beneficial microorganisms and safety concerns in health and food contexts. The authors conducted a comprehensive review of the probiotic, antimicrobial, and functional roles these bacteria play, including their use as protective cultures, antibiotic alternatives, and microbiome modulators, as well as their contributions to food fermentation and production of pathogen-inhibiting compounds such as bacteriocins. Alongside these benefits, the review addresses serious safety concerns arising from the association of certain enterococci with healthcare-associated infections and their capacity to harbor and spread antimicrobial resistance genes. The authors argue that these dual risks and benefits necessitate rigorous safety evaluations before enterococci are deployed in clinical or agrifood applications. The paper aims to serve as a broad reference resource to guide future research, regulatory frameworks, and practical applications involving beneficial enterococci.

Genomic analysis of high pathogenicity avian influenza viruses from Antarctica reveals multiple introductions from South America

Maria Ogrzewalska, Ralph Eric Thijl Vanstreels, Elisa Cavalcante Pereira et al.. Genomic analysis of high pathogenicity avian influenza viruses from Antarctica reveals multiple introductions from South America. Nature Communications. 2026

This study investigated the spread of highly pathogenic avian influenza virus H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b into Antarctica, where it poses a serious threat to polar wildlife. Researchers detected the virus in carcasses of eight species across two austral summer seasons in the South Shetland Islands, then performed whole-genome sequencing, mutational profiling, and phylogenetic analysis to trace the origins of these infections. The genomic analyses revealed that the viruses did not arise from a single introduction but instead descended from multiple independent events, with one group of strains clustering with viruses previously circulating in marine mammals in South America and another group clustering with strains detected in South Georgia. These findings indicate that South Georgia acts as an epidemiological stepping stone facilitating viral spread between South America and Antarctica. Overall, the study demonstrates extensive connectivity between the South American and Antarctic wildlife disease ecosystems, underscoring the ongoing risk of further incursions into vulnerable polar species.

Metagenomic and Targeted Next-Generation Sequencing in Infectious Disease Diagnostics: Current Applications, Challenges, and Future Perspectives

Rong Rong, Yuni Long, Yujing Li et al.. Metagenomic and Targeted Next-Generation Sequencing in Infectious Disease Diagnostics: Current Applications, Challenges, and Future Perspectives. Diagnostics. 2026

This review examines the current and emerging roles of metagenomic and targeted next-generation sequencing technologies in infectious disease diagnostics and public health. The authors surveyed the clinical applications of both approaches across settings including pneumonia, critical illness, and pediatric respiratory infections, assessing their utility for pathogen detection, antimicrobial resistance profiling, virulence characterization, and outbreak investigation. The review addresses persistent limitations of these technologies, including challenges related to sensitivity, specificity, standardization, bioinformatic complexity, and cost. The authors also explore the integration of sequencing-based surveillance with population-level public health strategies such as wastewater epidemiology for monitoring healthcare-associated infections and antimicrobial resistance. Finally, the paper outlines the steps required to move these genomic tools from research contexts into routine clinical and public health practice.

Serologic Evidence of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza A(H5N1) Virus Infection in a Veterinary Professional Exposed to an Infected Domestic Cat — Los Angeles County, California, December 2024–January 2025

Aisling Vaughan, Allison Joyce, Elizabeth Traub et al.. Serologic Evidence of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza A(H5N1) Virus Infection in a Veterinary Professional Exposed to an Infected Domestic Cat — Los Angeles County, California, December 2024–January 2025. MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report. 2026

This study investigated potential human infection with highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) following exposure to infected domestic cats in Los Angeles County. A cluster of domestic cats became ill after consuming raw animal products and tested positive for H5N1 virus, prompting public health authorities to monitor all people who had contact with these animals and later conduct a serosurvey among exposed individuals. Although none of the exposed persons tested positive by molecular testing during the acute monitoring period, serological testing subsequently revealed H5N1-specific antibodies in one asymptomatic veterinary professional who had not used respiratory or eye protection during cat exposure and had no other known risk factors for infection. These findings provide serological evidence of possible cat-to-human transmission of H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b virus, representing a notable spillover event from a domestic animal to a human. The authors conclude that the findings underscore concerns about cat-to-human transmission risk and emphasize the importance of infection control practices, including use of personal protective equipment, in veterinary settings.

Skuas as sentinels of high pathogenicity avian influenza H5N1 on the Antarctic Peninsula in the 2024/2025 austral summer

Michelle Wille, Will Abbott, Darrel Day et al.. Skuas as sentinels of high pathogenicity avian influenza H5N1 on the Antarctic Peninsula in the 2024/2025 austral summer. Microbial Genomics. 2026

This study investigated the presence and spread of high pathogenicity avian influenza H5N1 in Antarctica during the 2024/2025 austral summer, building on the first confirmed incursion detected the previous season. Researchers conducted five cruise ship-facilitated sampling expeditions across the South Shetland Islands and Antarctic Peninsula, collecting large numbers of environmental faecal samples from apparently healthy penguins and marine mammals, as well as sampling skua carcasses. No evidence of H5N1 was found in the faecal samples from apparently healthy animals, but the virus was detected in nearly all skua carcasses examined, which were frequently found in close proximity to penguin sub-colonies. Genomic analysis revealed that the viruses from Antarctic Peninsula skuas formed a single lineage distinct from those detected during the previous austral summer, though sequences from both the Antarctic Peninsula and South Shetland Islands in the current season clustered together. The findings confirm that H5N1 is recurring in Antarctica despite its extreme geographic isolation, highlighting skuas as important sentinels for surveillance and underscoring the need for continued monitoring to protect vulnerable wildlife populations.

Symptom clusters in Long COVID in the UK: prospective community-based cohort study using unsupervised machine learning

Jasmine Aherne, Ines Henriques-Cadby, Thomas House. Symptom clusters in Long COVID in the UK: prospective community-based cohort study using unsupervised machine learning. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigated symptom patterns in Long COVID by analyzing a large community-based cohort from the UK's national COVID-19 Infection Survey, which tracked self-reported persistence of symptoms following SARS-CoV-2 infection. Using unsupervised machine learning approaches—including Jaccard distance matrices, multidimensional scaling, and hierarchical clustering—the researchers mapped how symptoms co-occur and group together across hundreds of thousands of survey responses, stratified by time since symptom onset. The analysis revealed distinct clusters corresponding to neurological/systemic, gastrointestinal, and respiratory symptom groupings, with these organ-system boundaries becoming less defined as the condition progressed over time. Shortness of breath, weakness/tiredness, and muscle ache emerged as core symptoms throughout the condition, while overall symptom burden and multi-system involvement increased with longer duration. The study also identified three possible phenotypes of early Long COVID and demonstrated that the symptom experience evolves substantially from early to late stages.

Identification and Estimation of Intergenerational Income Mobility Measures

Alejandro Puerta-Cuartas. Identification and Estimation of Intergenerational Income Mobility Measures. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of measuring intergenerational income mobility when researchers observe income only at specific points in a person's life rather than over their full lifetime, a limitation that introduces life-cycle bias into standard estimates. The author develops a missing data framework that combines nonparametric identification with Neyman-orthogonal moments to construct debiased machine learning estimators, using observable characteristics and plausible statistical assumptions to recover unbiased measures of lifetime economic status transmission. The framework is applied to estimate the intergenerational elasticity for the United States using a major longitudinal household survey across multiple birth cohorts spanning several decades. Whereas conventional approaches produce estimates in a moderate range, the proposed method yields substantially higher estimates of intergenerational persistence, suggesting that standard methods have systematically underestimated how strongly children's economic outcomes are tied to those of their parents. These corrected estimates align with recent evidence based on long income averages over mid-career periods, supporting the conclusion that economic status in the United States is transmitted across generations to a greater degree than previously recognized.

AFDM-Enabled ISAC in Dynamic Environments: Fundamentals, Technologies and Opportunities

Linchu Chen, Zhendong Li, Zhou Su et al.. AFDM-Enabled ISAC in Dynamic Environments: Fundamentals, Technologies and Opportunities. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examines the use of affine frequency-division multiplexing as a waveform for integrated sensing and communication systems operating in dynamic environments. The authors address the core difficulties posed by severe Doppler effects, rapidly time-varying channels, and the coupling between delay and Doppler shifts that challenge conventional waveform designs. Through a comprehensive review, they outline the fundamental principles of the proposed waveform, analyze scenarios where it offers meaningful performance gains, and detail key enabling technologies alongside case studies. The paper concludes by identifying open research challenges and future directions, positioning the work as a broad reference for those developing next-generation dual-function radar-communication systems in high-mobility settings.

The Generative AI Gold Rush in Theoretical and Computational Research

Xiaoshn Nee, Haobo Zhong, Xiaomin Ni. The Generative AI Gold Rush in Theoretical and Computational Research. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether generative AI has measurably altered the rate of academic output in theoretical and computational research, with a particular focus on Mathematics submissions to arXiv. The researchers assembled a large panel of monthly submission data across twenty arXiv archives spanning several years, along with an author-level panel for Mathematics, and used a regularized synthetic control method to construct a counterfactual against which actual output in early 2026 could be compared. Mathematics submissions were found to be substantially above both their prior-year level and the synthetic counterfactual, with the anomaly being the largest among eligible placebo archives, suggesting the shift is genuine rather than artifactual. At the author level, the growth was concentrated in the high-output tail, with notably larger shares of authors producing many submissions in a short period, consistent with AI tools enabling individual researchers to dramatically increase their output rate. The authors interpret these findings as evidence of a delayed capability-threshold diffusion effect, and argue that verification, selection, and editorial attention now represent the central governance challenges facing research communities.

Analytical Statistics of Vortex Beams in a Turbulent Channel for OAM-Multiplexed FSO Communications

Xinyi Chu, Bo Cao, Renzhi Yuan et al.. Analytical Statistics of Vortex Beams in a Turbulent Channel for OAM-Multiplexed FSO Communications. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how atmospheric turbulence degrades orbital angular momentum multiplexed free-space optical communication systems by developing analytical models for the statistical behavior of vortex beams propagating through turbulent channels. The authors derived expressions for receiver-plane irradiance statistics, including average irradiance and scintillation index under moderate to strong turbulence using extended Rytov theory and a closed-form frequency-domain diffraction kernel. They further derived demultiplexed port-power statistics—including average port power, modal crosstalk, port-power variance, and cross-port covariance—using a complex Gaussian expansion of Laguerre-Gaussian vortex fields within the extended Huygens-Fresnel framework, and used these to evaluate symbol-error rate performance. The analytical results were validated against phase-screen simulations across varying turbulence strengths and beam parameters, showing strong consistency. A key finding is that symbol-error rate performance is more sensitive to the spacing between orbital angular momentum modes when the receiver aperture is small compared to when it is large.

lateral flow immunoassay diagnostics

Topic mode · generated September 07, 2026 at 17:48 UTC · 18 items

Recent advances in lateral flow immunoassay for virus detection: current achievements, challenges and future prospects.

Li L, Liu F, He X et al.. Recent advances in lateral flow immunoassay for virus detection: current achievements, challenges and future prospects.. Talanta. 2026

This review examines lateral flow immunoassay technology as a diagnostic platform for viral infectious diseases, reframing it beyond its conventional role as a point-of-care tool toward a broader point-of-need self-testing platform capable of large-scale epidemic screening. The authors systematically survey recent advances across three main dimensions: signal generation and transduction strategies employing diverse nanomaterials such as colorimetric, fluorescent, magnetic, surface-enhanced Raman scattering, and photothermal systems; multimodal and multiplex detection approaches to improve sensitivity and throughput; and the integration of machine learning for signal interpretation and system optimization. The review also comprehensively covers applications in detecting viral antigens, antibodies, and nucleic acids. Current limitations including sensitivity constraints, target specificity, and practical challenges around cost, usability, and stability are critically assessed. The authors conclude that with advances in nanomaterials, intelligent data processing, and multi-target detection, lateral flow immunoassay holds transformative potential as an emergency pandemic screening tool and for strengthening decentralized routine testing within global public health infrastructure.

Beyond lateral flow: vertical flow immunoassays as thermodynamically assisted rapid diagnostics.

Nainar SNS, Chu YY, Lin HC et al.. Beyond lateral flow: vertical flow immunoassays as thermodynamically assisted rapid diagnostics.. Chemical communications (Cambridge, England). 2026

This review examines vertical flow immunoassays (VFAs) as a superior alternative to the widely used lateral flow immunoassay (LFA) format for point-of-care diagnostics. The authors analyze the fundamental physical and chemical limitations of LFAs—including poor analyte capture kinetics, high non-specific background, one-dimensional multiplexing constraints, and the hook effect—and explain how VFAs overcome these through a stacked-membrane architecture that decouples convective delivery from immunoreaction kinetics. By enabling thermodynamic washing, two-dimensional capture arrays, and sequential surface chemistry, VFAs achieve dramatically improved specificity and eliminate the hook effect, with head-to-head comparisons against matched LFAs demonstrating substantial improvements in detection limits. Integration with surface-enhanced Raman spectroscopy, electrochemical transduction, nanoparticle amplification, and deep-learning analysis has further extended VFA sensitivity into the sub-picogram-per-milliliter range. The review maps VFA applications across clinical, food safety, and environmental domains while critically examining the engineering and commercial barriers that have so far prevented this higher-sensitivity platform from achieving widespread deployment.

Polydopamine nanoparticle-based colorimetric-photothermal dual-mode lateral flow immunoassay for thromboembolism diagnosis.

Yin J, Qiu Y, Nie Y et al.. Polydopamine nanoparticle-based colorimetric-photothermal dual-mode lateral flow immunoassay for thromboembolism diagnosis.. RSC advances. 2026

This paper investigated whether polydopamine nanoparticles could serve as superior signal labels in lateral flow immunoassays to overcome the sensitivity limitations of conventional gold nanoparticle-based single-mode colorimetric detection. The researchers constructed a dual-mode colorimetric-photothermal lateral flow immunoassay platform exploiting the high molar extinction coefficient and broadband ultraviolet-to-near-infrared absorption of polydopamine nanoparticles, applying it to detect two thromboembolism-related biomarkers, thrombomodulin and α₂-plasmin inhibitor-plasmin complex, in clinical serum samples. The photothermal readout mode substantially improved detection sensitivity compared with colorimetric readout alone, and clinical validation using serum samples showed good agreement with established clinical methods. The authors conclude that polydopamine nanoparticle-based dual-mode lateral flow immunoassay offers an effective strategy for enhancing analytical sensitivity and reliability in point-of-care diagnostics for thrombosis.

A lateral flow microarray immunoassay to detect USUV NS1-specific antibodies in tissues of deceased blackbirds in the Netherlands.

Godarzi B, Holicki CM, Chandler FD et al.. A lateral flow microarray immunoassay to detect USUV NS1-specific antibodies in tissues of deceased blackbirds in the Netherlands.. Virus research. 2026

This study investigated whether a lateral flow microarray immunoassay could be used to detect Usutu virus-specific antibodies in tissues from deceased Eurasian blackbirds, addressing a gap in current surveillance systems that rely on sera from live birds. The researchers tested blood clots and lung and liver tissue extracts from blackbird carcasses found in the Netherlands using an assay targeting antibodies against the USUTU non-structural 1 protein. Lung tissue extracts showed the greatest likelihood of detecting these antibodies in birds confirmed positive by RT-PCR, and the assay demonstrated minimal cross-reactivity with West Nile virus antigens. The findings establish proof of concept that this immunoassay can specifically detect Usutu virus antibodies in deceased birds, suggesting it could serve as a rapid field-deployable tool for serological surveillance of both live and dead avian specimens in the future.

Boronate-mediated antibody orientation: a molecular simulation-guided strategy for ultra-sensitive lateral flow immunoassays.

Yang Z, Yan X, Yang Z et al.. Boronate-mediated antibody orientation: a molecular simulation-guided strategy for ultra-sensitive lateral flow immunoassays.. Mikrochimica acta. 2026

This paper investigated how antibody orientation during immobilization affects the sensitivity and consistency of lateral flow immunoassays, with random immobilization identified as a key limiting factor. The researchers developed a boronate affinity-based strategy that exploits covalent reactions between boronic acid and cis-diol groups on antibody Fc-region glycans to achieve site-specific, oriented antibody attachment, and used molecular dynamics simulations to elucidate the mechanistic basis of this interaction. Simulations revealed that the boronate-antibody configuration exhibited highly favorable binding energy and rapid conformational stabilization, proceeding through initial electrostatic guidance followed by hydrogen-bond and covalent bond formation. Experimentally, the oriented immobilization approach yielded near-complete antibody conjugation efficiency and improved antigen-binding domain exposure and functional affinity compared to conventional passive adsorption. When applied to detecting the mycotoxin zearalenone in corn, the resulting lateral flow assay achieved a detection limit dramatically lower than traditional colloidal gold methods, with strong agreement to confirmatory chromatography-mass spectrometry analysis, establishing the approach as a broadly applicable strategy for next-generation rapid diagnostics.

On-site serological screening of Usutu virus in wild birds: Field evaluation of a lateral flow microarray immunoassay using the WHO REASSURED framework.

Godarzi B, Holicki CM, Chandler FD et al.. On-site serological screening of Usutu virus in wild birds: Field evaluation of a lateral flow microarray immunoassay using the WHO REASSURED framework.. Journal of virological methods. 2026

This study evaluated a field-deployable serological test for detecting arbovirus antibodies in wild birds captured at bird-ringing sites in the Netherlands, where Usutu virus is established and West Nile virus incidence is increasing. The researchers applied a species-independent Lateral Flow Microarray ImmunoAssay capable of simultaneously detecting antibodies against both Usutu virus and West Nile virus non-structural protein 1, testing it on wild birds in the field and comparing results against the gold-standard Focus Reduction Neutralization Test. The assay demonstrated high sensitivity and specificity for Usutu virus detection and successfully identified seroconversion in a Eurasian blackbird, which was confirmed by the reference method. Performance was also assessed against the WHO REASSURED framework for point-of-care diagnostics, where the assay achieved a strong proportion of the maximum possible score. The authors conclude that the assay is feasible for on-site arbovirus surveillance in wild birds, offering a rapid, multi-analyte, and equipment-light format suited to field conditions and resource-limited settings, pending broader validation.

Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives

Shehwaz Anwar, Hajed Obaid A. Alharbi, Ali Yousif Babiker et al.. Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review paper examined the dual roles of reactive oxygen species in biological systems, encompassing both their essential physiological functions and their pathological contributions when dysregulated. The authors synthesized current knowledge on the major types, sources, and signaling functions of reactive oxygen species alongside the molecular pathways through which oxidative stress drives cellular damage. The review also critically evaluated contemporary and emerging methods for detecting and quantifying oxidative stress, including biochemical assays, fluorescent probes, biosensors, and advanced imaging techniques. The pathophysiological involvement of oxidative stress was assessed across a range of major diseases, including cancer, diabetes, cardiovascular disease, neurodegenerative conditions, and inflammatory disorders. The authors conclude by advocating for precision redox medicine, emphasizing that advances in detection technologies and deeper mechanistic understanding could enable more targeted antioxidant-based diagnostic and therapeutic strategies.

Rethinking Extracellular Vesicle Signaling

Wojciech Chrzanowski, Joy Wolfram. Rethinking Extracellular Vesicle Signaling. Advanced Materials. 2026

This paper examines the mechanisms by which extracellular vesicles mediate intercellular communication, questioning the dominant assumption that cargo must be internalized into recipient cells to exert biological effects. The authors propose two alternative signaling modes based on surface binding alone — termed "bind-and-stay," where vesicles remain attached without internalization, and "bind-and-leave," where vesicles transiently bind and then disengage to interact with additional cells. This latter mode challenges the conventional assumption that a single vesicle signals to only one recipient cell, suggesting instead that one vesicle could sequentially activate multiple cells. The paper offers a forward-looking perspective on the relative efficiency and effectiveness of these different signaling modes and discusses experimental approaches that could be used to investigate them. The authors argue that a more complete understanding of extracellular vesicle signaling mechanisms is essential for both basic biology and the development of vesicle-based therapeutics.

Mechanistic advances in microbial nanobiotechnology and their applications in sustainable agriculture, environment and biomedicine

Mir Sajad Rabani, Meenakshi Shrivastav, Mudasir Fayaz et al.. Mechanistic advances in microbial nanobiotechnology and their applications in sustainable agriculture, environment and biomedicine. Discover Nano. 2026

This review examines microbial nanobiotechnology, a field combining microbiology and nanotechnology in which microorganisms such as bacteria, fungi, and algae are used to biosynthesize nanoparticles through environmentally friendly and cost-effective biochemical processes. The authors survey the tools, mechanisms, and applications of this approach, with particular attention to how genetic engineering and synthetic biology have advanced the controlled production and characterization of biogenic nanoparticles. These nanoparticles have shown promise across biomedical domains including targeted drug delivery, imaging, and antimicrobial treatments, as well as in environmental remediation such as wastewater treatment and pollution control, and in agricultural and energy applications. Despite this broad potential, the review identifies persistent challenges including scalability, biosafety concerns, regulatory hurdles, and ethical considerations that must be resolved before widespread commercial adoption can occur. The authors conclude that multidisciplinary collaboration will be essential to move microbial nanobiotechnology from research settings into practical solutions for global challenges in health, environment, and industry.

Cellulose-based sensors for decentralized monitoring in precision agriculture

Mirinal Kumar Rayappa, José M. R. Flauzino, Max Grell et al.. Cellulose-based sensors for decentralized monitoring in precision agriculture. Nature Communications. 2026

This paper reviews the emerging role of cellulose as a foundational material for sensors designed to monitor crops and soils directly in agricultural fields. The authors examine a range of sensor platforms, from simple paper-based assays to flexible wearable devices, all built on cellulose's properties as a lightweight and biodegradable structural polymer. These sensors are designed to measure key agricultural parameters including nutrients, moisture, plant stress, and disease without requiring laboratory infrastructure. The paper argues that as materials engineering advances alongside digital connectivity, cellulose-based sensor networks could enable real-time, distributed monitoring of plant and soil health. The authors conclude that such technologies have the potential to drive a broader transition toward more data-driven and sustainable agricultural practices.

Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads

Karan Malhotra, Louise L. Hansen, Samuel Han et al.. Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads. Journal of the American Chemical Society. 2026

This paper developed an enzyme-free platform for ultrasensitive protein detection on magnetic beads, aimed at making advanced diagnostic assays accessible in resource-limited settings and low- and middle-income countries. The approach, called efMOSAIC, uses hybridization chain reaction to generate signal amplifiers that replace the enzyme-based components found in existing digital protein detection methods such as Simoa and MOSAIC. The platform was validated for detecting cytokines from human plasma and demonstrated the capability for multiplexed biomarker detection. The enzyme-free signal amplifiers achieved detection limits comparable to established ultrasensitive assays while remaining stable when stored at room temperature for up to six months, eliminating the need for cold chain infrastructure. By combining simple workflows with temperature-stable reagents, efMOSAIC is proposed as a means of broadening access to ultrasensitive protein quantification beyond centralized laboratory settings.

In vitro, cellular and in vivo studies of amyloid oligomers structure and toxicity: Challenges and advances

Magdalena I. Ivanova, Carmelo La Rosa, Ayyalusamy Ramamoorthy. In vitro, cellular and in vivo studies of amyloid oligomers structure and toxicity: Challenges and advances. Protein Science. 2026

This review examines the current state of knowledge and methodology surrounding oligomeric assemblies of amyloidogenic proteins implicated in neurodegenerative and systemic diseases. Because these oligomers are low in abundance, structurally heterogeneous, and transient, studying them presents substantial technical challenges that the paper systematically addresses. The authors survey a broad toolkit of approaches spanning biophysical techniques such as cryo-electron microscopy and atomic force microscopy, computational methods including molecular dynamics simulations, and biological models ranging from cellular assays and organoids to animal models. The review also highlights emerging strategies for detecting misfolded proteins in complex biological environments and live-cell systems. The authors argue that integrating complementary findings across diverse experimental platforms is essential for achieving mechanistic clarity and ultimately translating insights into therapeutic strategies against these pathogenic species.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the primordial density field of the Universe can be reconstructed from observations of the Epoch of Reionization, with the goal of recovering cosmological information lost to non-linear structure formation. The researchers used simulated 21-cm and CO line-intensity maps generated with the LIMFAST code, applying a three-dimensional U-Net neural network to map these late-time observables back to initial conditions at very high redshift. The two tracers were chosen deliberately for their complementarity, as 21-cm emission captures neutral low-density gas while CO traces dense star-forming regions, together spanning a broader range of cosmic environments. When combining both tracers under realistic instrumental noise models mimicking next-generation surveys, the reconstruction achieves strong cross-correlation with the true initial density field across a range of scales, outperforming either tracer alone. Crucially, performing simulation-based inference on power-spectrum summaries derived from reconstructed fields tightens constraints on key cosmological parameters such as the amplitude of matter fluctuations and the spectral index, demonstrating that joint multi-tracer reconstruction with machine learning can recover otherwise inaccessible cosmological information from upcoming surveys.

WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data

Ji Soo Lee, Xilun Chen, Pierce Chuang et al.. WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper introduces WearableQA, a benchmark designed to evaluate whether AI systems can reason over real longitudinal wearable health data from actual users. The benchmark comprises thousands of multiple-choice questions derived from wearable time series, blood biomarkers, and demographic information spanning hundreds of days per user, organized across question types that distinguish data computation from physiological interpretation and single-signal from multi-signal reasoning. To construct reliable questions at scale, the authors developed a dual-grounding framework combining literature-based physiological findings with statistically validated population-level patterns observed in the real-world wearable data. Evaluation of a range of proprietary and open-source large language models revealed substantial variation in performance, with the benchmark proving far from solved as most models fell below modest accuracy thresholds despite a low random-chance baseline. The authors conclude that WearableQA provides a realistic and diagnostically informative benchmark for assessing LLM reasoning over authentic wearable health records.

Stability Patterns for Spherical and Projective Braid Groups

Sarah Anderson. Stability Patterns for Spherical and Projective Braid Groups. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates homological and representation stability patterns for braid groups and pure braid groups associated with the sphere and the real projective plane, two cases previously excluded from known stability results. Classical results by McDuff–Segal and Church established stability for configuration spaces on manifolds with boundary and compact manifolds, respectively, but these require the configuration spaces to be Eilenberg–MacLane spaces, a condition that fails for the sphere and real projective plane. The authors use results from Cohen–Pakianathan together with the theory of FI-homology to overcome this obstruction. They prove that the equivariant homology of ordered configuration spaces exhibits representation stability, while that of unordered configuration spaces exhibits stable periodicity, extending known stability patterns to these previously intractable cases as a special instance of a more general result.

MEOX: Compact Multimodal Mixture-of-Experts for Earth Observation

Mohanad Albughdadi. MEOX: Compact Multimodal Mixture-of-Experts for Earth Observation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces MEOX, a compact multimodal masked autoencoder designed for Earth observation that handles heterogeneous sensors and missing data without requiring large model architectures. The approach uses sensor-specific adapters, validity signals, and sparse mixture-of-experts blocks to process modality-dependent information before fusing it into a shared spatial representation, with metadata tokens incorporated to provide contextual information. The model is pretrained on over a million multimodal Earth observation samples using masked reconstruction and structured sensor dropout, then evaluated on multiple remote sensing benchmarks covering segmentation and classification tasks. MEOX achieves competitive or superior performance compared to larger existing models on several benchmarks, including outperforming a comparable sparse-expert baseline on key tasks, while maintaining a parameter count well below typical large-scale Earth observation models. The work demonstrates that sensor-flexible, metadata-aware representation learning with strong downstream transfer can be achieved within a compact parameter budget through careful architectural design.

Electromagnetic alignment and jet precession around supermassive black holes: Quasi-periodic oscillations in tidal disruption events

Pau Amaro-Seoane, Leif Lui, Alejandro Torres-Orjuela et al.. Electromagnetic alignment and jet precession around supermassive black holes: Quasi-periodic oscillations in tidal disruption events. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates quasi-periodic oscillations and jet behavior in tidal disruption events by modeling the electromagnetic interactions between a precessing accretion disk and a spinning supermassive black hole. The authors use a covariant formulation of electromagnetic angular-momentum transfer, in which general-relativistic frame-dragging tears a misaligned transient accretion flow into an isolated inner mini-disk whose precession drives jet axis rotation and produces geometric flux modulations in X-ray and radio observations. By expanding the Noether current density over the event horizon in a small-spin limit with a split-monopole magnetic field topology consistent with the Blandford-Znajek mechanism, they derive a closed-form analytical torque showing that electromagnetic back-reaction induces retrograde precession that couples with prograde Lense-Thirring precession to set the global oscillation frequency. The model predicts a specific frequency drift pattern—an initial lengthening followed by asymptotic shortening of inter-flare intervals—along with a monotonically attenuating peak-to-trough flux ratio and a residual late-time modulation amplitude caused by magnetic flux depletion stalling disk alignment. Crucially, the authors propose a method to extract black hole spin and magnetic flux density directly from the temporal derivatives of this frequency drift, providing an approach that operates independently of spectral continuum fitting.

Does Your Agent's Memory Survive a Model Upgrade? A Controlled Study of Memory Portability

Ankit Goyal, Jaideep Ray. Does Your Agent's Memory Survive a Model Upgrade? A Controlled Study of Memory Portability. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated whether AI agent memory systems remain functional when the underlying language model is upgraded, a problem typically overlooked in practice. The researchers compared four memory storage strategies—verbatim long-context history, retrieval-augmented generation with chunked text, model-compressed natural-language notes, and a fixed-schema knowledge graph—by testing how well each format transferred across model swaps using synthetic histories with exact-scoring evaluation. They found that fixed-schema knowledge graphs were highly robust to model changes, while compressed notes were strongly coupled to the model that wrote them, with accuracy shifting substantially and asymmetrically depending on the direction of migration. In retrieval-augmented systems, using a mixed embedding index from two different embedding versions captured only a fraction of the accuracy gain achievable through full re-embedding, and diagnostic analysis revealed that most performance loss in notes stemmed from information destroyed during initial compression, whereas retrieval failures dominated losses in the retrieval-augmented approach. The study concludes that safe memory migration requires direction-specific testing, strict isolation of embedding spaces, and preservation of original source histories to enable recovery when memory formats fail to transfer.

Can exercise affect immune function to increase susceptibility to infection?

Richard J. Simpson, Richard J. Simpson, Maree Gleeson et al.. Can exercise affect immune function to increase susceptibility to infection?. PubMed. 2026

This debate paper examines the contested question of whether exercise itself can suppress immune function and increase susceptibility to infection. The authors present opposing arguments addressing whether high-performance athletes are genuinely more infection-prone than the general population, whether exercise independently alters immunity beyond the confounding effects of factors such as sleep disruption, anxiety, travel, nutritional deficits, and environmental extremes, and whether the biomarkers and research methods used to assess immune health in this context are adequate. There is broad agreement that regular, moderate-intensity exercise of limited duration is beneficial for immune defense, particularly in older adults and those with chronic illness, while arduous exercise typical of elite athletes and military personnel has long been thought to suppress immunity. However, the paper highlights a growing challenge to the view that exercise per se causes immunosuppression, noting that the many non-exercise stressors inherent to these populations confound interpretation of the evidence. Both sides agree that infection susceptibility is multifactorial, and the authors call for more rigorous empirical research to determine whether exercise is itself a causative factor in elevated infection risk among those engaged in high-intensity training.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

Yuqing Liu, Kun Zhu, Weijun Peng et al.. Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review paper examines how the integration of multiomics technologies with artificial intelligence is reshaping precision drug discovery and clinical translation. The authors synthesize current applications of genome-wide association studies, transcriptomics, proteomics, and metabolomics alongside deep learning frameworks to analyze complex biological datasets underlying disease pathogenesis. The review covers how this combined approach advances target identification, lead optimization, drug repositioning, de novo compound discovery, virtual screening, pharmacokinetic modeling, and safety assessment. Applications in oncology, neurodegenerative diseases, and cardiovascular conditions are highlighted as illustrative cases of this convergence in practice. The authors conclude that while challenges such as data harmonization, reproducibility, and algorithmic bias remain, the fusion of multiomics and artificial intelligence represents a transformative step toward more precise therapeutic interventions and bench-to-clinic translational medicine.
