Oral Nirmatrelvir–Ritonavir for Covid-19 in Higher-Risk Outpatients

Christopher C. Butler, Andrew Pinto, Victoria Harris et al.. Oral Nirmatrelvir–Ritonavir for Covid-19 in Higher-Risk Outpatients. New England Journal of Medicine. 2026

These two open-label randomized platform trials investigated whether nirmatrelvir–ritonavir reduces severe outcomes in higher-risk outpatients with COVID-19 who were predominantly vaccinated or had prior natural immunity, a population distinct from the unvaccinated high-risk individuals in whom the drug originally demonstrated benefit. Conducted in the United Kingdom and Canada, the trials enrolled adults who tested positive for SARS-CoV-2 and had been symptomatic for five days or fewer, randomly assigning them to receive either nirmatrelvir–ritonavir plus usual care or usual care alone, with hospitalization or death within 28 days as the primary outcome. Across both trials, rates of hospitalization or death were very low and similar between treatment and control groups, and the probability of superiority for nirmatrelvir–ritonavir did not reach a convincing threshold in either study. Although a substudy confirmed that the drug did reduce viral load by end of treatment, this virological effect did not translate into a clinical benefit in this vaccinated population. The investigators conclude that nirmatrelvir–ritonavir does not reduce hospitalization or death among vaccinated higher-risk individuals with SARS-CoV-2 infection.

Chitosan Derivatives: Challenges and Opportunities in the Green and Sustainable Transition Era

Ana Morais, Rita Lima, Madalena Pinto et al.. Chitosan Derivatives: Challenges and Opportunities in the Green and Sustainable Transition Era. Molecules. 2026

This review examines the production of chitosan derivatives through the lens of green and sustainable chemistry, addressing the full pipeline from chitin isolation to chemical modification of chitosan. The authors surveyed existing methodologies for extracting chitin from natural sources, converting it to chitosan via deacetylation, and further functionalizing the polymer, evaluating each step for environmental impact and efficiency. The review highlights sustainable technological advances including continuous-flow processes, click chemistry, and innovative purification strategies as promising eco-efficient alternatives to conventional approaches. The authors argue that interdisciplinary collaboration and the integration of green chemistry principles across all production stages are critical for developing chitosan derivatives that are both high-performing and environmentally responsible.

The cell with many faces: lung macrophage plasticity and function in response to environmental and pathogenic insults

Shubham Dubey, Junaid Khan, AS Alishlash et al.. The cell with many faces: lung macrophage plasticity and function in response to environmental and pathogenic insults. Physiological Reviews. 2026

This review examines how alveolar macrophages, as key immune sentinels of the lung, respond to a broad range of environmental and pathogenic insults through dynamic shifts in their functional and phenotypic states. The authors synthesize current knowledge on how exposures to particulate matter, atypical and opportunistic bacteria, and respiratory viruses drive transitions between pro-inflammatory and reparative macrophage phenotypes, disrupting core activities such as phagocytosis, efferocytosis, and cytokine production while triggering oxidative stress, inflammasome activation, and programmed cell death. Although these responses are necessary for pathogen and particle clearance, the review highlights how they can also sustain inflammation and promote tissue remodeling and fibrosis, contributing to chronic lung diseases including chronic obstructive pulmonary disease, pneumoconiosis, and post-COVID interstitial lung disease. The authors also discuss how environmental and microbial exposures reshape alveolar macrophage receptor profiles, intracellular signaling, and crosstalk with epithelial and mesenchymal cells to collectively determine disease trajectories. The review identifies existing knowledge gaps and frames alveolar macrophage plasticity as a promising therapeutic target for both exposure-induced and pathogen-mediated lung pathology.

Beyond Scalar Flexibility: From Eligible AI Workloads to Dependable Load Relief

Meiyi Li. Beyond Scalar Flexibility: From Eligible AI Workloads to Dependable Load Relief. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how to accurately characterize the grid flexibility of large-scale GPU data centers, replacing the common assumption that flexibility can be represented as a fixed percentage of load. Using a multi-month production trace spanning hundreds of thousands of GPUs, the authors reconstruct hourly power observations and derive a workload-semantic flexibility envelope that accounts for eligible curtailment while preserving allocated-GPU idle power. They find that available relief declines meaningfully as event duration extends from one hour to twenty-four hours, and that the fraction of capacity available at high reliability levels also erodes substantially over longer commitments. A simple scalar representation of flexibility systematically misstates these quantities, either overstating or understating relief depending on how it is calibrated, with the error varying considerably across durations and reliability levels. Aggregating across multiple clusters improves the firmness of available relief but is limited by cross-cluster covariance, while delay-based deferral of new workloads contributes negligible additional capacity, leading the authors to propose contractual flexibility terms expressed in duration, reliability, portfolio, and realizability dimensions rather than a single percentage.

Data-Driven Generator Transient Prediction for Digital Twin Decision Support

Emad Sadeghi, Emerson Miller, Kerry Sado et al.. Data-Driven Generator Transient Prediction for Digital Twin Decision Support. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of predicting generator transient behavior in real time to support decision-making within digital twin frameworks, specifically by evaluating proposed power load commands before they are executed. The authors developed an event-conditioned variant of Hankel Dynamic Mode Decomposition with Control, which incorporates delay-coordinate lifting and event-weighted basis features to better capture sparse load-transition dynamics that govern voltage and frequency deviations during transients. A split-conformal calibration layer was layered onto the surrogate model to produce statistically valid joint prediction bands for voltage and frequency, giving operators quantified confidence intervals alongside point forecasts. The approach achieved prediction errors on the order of one volt and a fraction of a hertz during event windows, with joint coverage closely matching the nominal target, and the entire open-loop rollout ran far faster than real time on a single processor core. The results demonstrate that this framework can serve as a computationally efficient advisory tool for generator digital twins, combining accurate transient prediction with calibrated uncertainty quantification.

Headroom-Aware Stochastic Adaptive Model Predictive Control for Load Frequency Control in Microgrids

Erfan Mehdipour Abadi, Shuo Yuan, Le Yi Wang et al.. Headroom-Aware Stochastic Adaptive Model Predictive Control for Load Frequency Control in Microgrids. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates load frequency control in microgrids with high penetration of inverter-based resources, where the stochastic and time-varying nature of renewable energy headroom causes conventional model predictive control strategies to issue control commands that exceed physically deliverable regulation capacity. The authors identify this control-actuator mismatch as a key source of degraded frequency regulation performance, particularly under tight reserve margins. To address this, two headroom-aware control strategies are developed: stochastic headroom constrained MPC, which enforces feasibility through time-varying input constraints derived from headroom predictions, and stochastic adaptive MPC, which embeds headroom awareness into the objective function by adaptively penalizing control actions based on predicted headroom availability. Simulation results confirm that conventional MPC-based load frequency control suffers from stochastic saturation events, while both proposed strategies improve regulation performance. The stochastic headroom constrained approach eliminates saturation entirely, whereas the adaptive approach achieves substantial mitigation with reduced computational burden, making it more suitable for real-time implementation.

Large Language Models for HVAC Operations in Building Energy Systems: A Critical Review of Methods, Applications, and Deployment Readiness

Alexander Neubauer, Tianzhen Hong, Han Li et al.. Large Language Models for HVAC Operations in Building Energy Systems: A Critical Review of Methods, Applications, and Deployment Readiness. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This review examined how large language models are being applied to heating, ventilation, and air conditioning operations within building energy systems, synthesizing peer-reviewed literature published from 2023 through early 2026. The authors systematically coded studies across application families—including building energy modelling, load forecasting, and control—and assessed each for evidence quality, deployment readiness, and the appropriate boundary between LLM involvement and physical HVAC decision-making. The corpus was heavily concentrated in building energy modelling, pilot-level evidence was rare, and no study demonstrated sustained real-world operational deployment. The review concludes that none of the examined work is ready for immediate industry adoption, with the vast majority classified as research-only, though bounded human-in-the-loop applications such as point-name normalization, document-grounded operator support, and advisory interfaces around physics-based controllers show near-term promise. Overall, current evidence supports LLMs functioning primarily as semantic and workflow layers rather than autonomous controllers, with conventional machine learning, model predictive control, and reinforcement learning remaining more mature for high-frequency control and numerical forecasting tasks.

An Aeroelastic Solver Integrating Reformulated-Vortex-Particle and Finite-Element Methods across Non-Conforming Interfaces

Neeraj Balachandar, A. Padmaprabhan, Vishnu R. Unni. An Aeroelastic Solver Integrating Reformulated-Vortex-Particle and Finite-Element Methods across Non-Conforming Interfaces. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a modular aeroelastic solver called VarFlExI designed to model two-way fluid-structure interaction of flexible lifting surfaces. The framework couples a reformulated Vortex Particle Method for solving incompressible Navier-Stokes equations—avoiding costly volumetric meshing—with a finite element structural solver based on a Reissner-Mindlin plate formulation integrated using the generalized-α method and solved within the FEniCS framework. The two solvers are linked through an explicit staggered partitioned scheme that enforces conservation of virtual work for load and displacement transfer across non-matching interfaces, using separate work-conservative force and reverse-geometry transfer operators through a common intermediate interface. The coupled framework is validated against water-tunnel experiments, demonstrating accurate prediction of the full aeroelastic response, and is assessed through sensitivity analyses spanning varied flow conditions, structural properties, and coupling parameters. The meshless aerodynamic solver is reported to deliver accurate results at substantially reduced computational cost compared to traditional volumetric mesh approaches.

On the Concept of an Optimal Portfolio of Uncertain Flexible Loads

Julie Rousseau, Philipp Heer, Kristina Orehounig et al.. On the Concept of an Optimal Portfolio of Uncertain Flexible Loads. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how flexible loads can be optimally grouped to provide reserves in power systems, addressing the challenges posed by their limited energy capacity and uncertain availability compared to conventional generators. The study was motivated by new reserve market rules introduced by the Danish Transmission System Operator that incorporate energy constraints and relaxed reliability requirements, framing the problem as a joint chance-constrained reserve quantification task. The authors derived two analytical reformulations of this difficult optimization problem: an exact formulation applicable when one reserve direction dominates, and an approximate formulation for other cases. They introduced the concept of an optimal portfolio of flexible loads, showing theoretically and through case studies that adding loads with similar expected values but different stochastic behaviors can alter the total reserves a portfolio can provide. Numerical results demonstrate that the analytical reformulations closely approximate the exact formulation, and that their theoretical analysis can predict in which portfolio a given load's marginal contribution to reserves is greatest.

Comparative evaluation of a multi-antigen malaria rapid diagnostic test against qPCR and microscopy in a co-endemic area of Southern Ethiopia.

Bogale A, Mitiku A, Tefera S et al.. Comparative evaluation of a multi-antigen malaria rapid diagnostic test against qPCR and microscopy in a co-endemic area of Southern Ethiopia.. Scientific reports. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of a new multi-antigen rapid diagnostic test for malaria in a region of southern Ethiopia where both *Plasmodium falciparum* and *P. vivax* co-circulate, and where limitations such as low parasite densities and gene deletions can undermine conventional testing. Febrile patients at health facilities were tested using the multi-antigen Bioline RDT, a conventional HRP2-based RDT, expert microscopy, and quantitative PCR during peak transmission season. The multi-antigen RDT performed well against microscopy, showing high sensitivity and specificity with near-perfect agreement, but its sensitivity dropped notably when qPCR served as the reference, revealing that submicroscopic, low-density infections were frequently missed. qPCR detected the highest overall prevalence and identified more species-specific and mixed infections than either RDT or microscopy. The findings support the use of the multi-antigen RDT for routine clinical diagnosis but underscore the need for more sensitive tools in surveillance and control settings where low-density infections are epidemiologically important.

Characterization of arbovirus infections in patients within Haiti by screening discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes.

Matulis GA, Smith HP, Hall G et al.. Characterization of arbovirus infections in patients within Haiti by screening discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes.. PLoS neglected tropical diseases. 2026

This study investigated circulating arboviruses in Haiti during a period when conventional surveillance was disrupted by security concerns, using an innovative approach of extracting nucleic acids from discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes collected from febrile patients across two Haitian departments. Samples were screened using a series of real-time PCR assays targeting flaviviruses, alphaviruses, and Oropouche virus, with positive flavivirus samples subjected to further typing and next-generation sequencing with phylogenetic analysis. Dengue virus serotype three was identified as the predominant pathogen, with all positive detections occurring in samples collected in a single year, and phylogenetic analysis showed high similarity to sequences circulating elsewhere in the Caribbean during the same period. No evidence of alphavirus or Oropouche virus infections was detected in the subset of samples tested. The findings demonstrate that discarded malaria rapid diagnostic tests can serve as a practical and effective surveillance tool for arboviral pathogens in resource-limited or politically unstable settings.

ZigBee Based Low Latency IoT and AI Integrated Framework for Real Time Telehealth Monitoring

B. V. Ravi Kumar, Mohammad Shahnawaz Shaikh. ZigBee Based Low Latency IoT and AI Integrated Framework for Real Time Telehealth Monitoring. Emerging Science Journal. 2026

This paper presents an IoT and AI-integrated wireless health monitoring framework designed for elderly and chronically ill patients in remote or auxiliary care settings. The system uses ZigBee wireless communication to transmit physiological data, including heart rate, body temperature, and oxygen levels, from wearable sensors to a centralized cloud-based processing unit with low power consumption and minimal latency. Multiple machine learning algorithms, including random forest, support vector machine, and logistic regression, were applied to detect health anomalies, with the random forest model achieving the highest reported performance in accuracy and recall. Testing in a real-world care facility demonstrated the system's capability for near-real-time data transmission and sustained low-power operation, suggesting suitability for long-term deployment. The authors conclude that the framework addresses key limitations of existing telehealth systems, such as high latency and poor integration, while offering scalability through the potential addition of further sensors such as blood pressure and glucose monitors.

Advances in Natural Product Extraction: Established and Emerging Technologies

Carsyn R. Travis, Jared McMaster, Fatima Rivas. Advances in Natural Product Extraction: Established and Emerging Technologies. Molecules. 2026

This review examines recent advances in the methods used to extract and isolate natural products from biological sources such as plants, microorganisms, and marine organisms, which remain central to drug discovery due to their structural diversity and pharmacological potential. The authors surveyed developments across a range of extraction technologies, including green and designer solvent systems, pressurized and ultrasound-assisted extraction, supercritical fluid techniques, and integrated purification workflows. A particular focus is placed on the application of artificial intelligence and machine learning to optimize solvent selection, extraction conditions, and metabolite dereplication within complex biological matrices. The review finds that these innovations, taken together, have substantially improved compound recovery, selectivity, processing speed, and analytical throughput compared to conventional approaches. The authors conclude that combinatorial extraction strategies and AI-driven platforms represent especially significant developments in expanding the range of detectable and characterizable metabolites available for biological evaluation.

Biosynthesis of cinchona alkaloids

Blaise Kimbadi Lombe, Tingan Zhou, Gyumin Kang et al.. Biosynthesis of cinchona alkaloids. Nature. 2026

This paper investigated the long-unknown biosynthetic pathway by which cinchona plants produce their characteristic alkaloids, including the antimalarial drug quinine and the chiral catalyst cinchonidine. Using a combination of isotopic labelling, gene silencing, single-nucleus RNA sequencing, and comparative transcriptomics, the researchers identified the genes responsible for assembling the distinctive quinoline-quinuclidine scaffold of these compounds and uncovered several unexpected enzymatic transformations, including an unusual cyclization reaction that generates a previously unreported quaternary amine intermediate. Heterologous expression of these genes in *Nicotiana benthamiana* enabled production of several cinchona alkaloid precursors, and the same platform could convert non-native halogenated tryptamine substrates into novel alkaloid analogues. These findings resolve a longstanding mystery in plant biochemistry and open avenues for producing cinchona alkaloids and their derivatives through metabolic engineering.

Impact of air pollution on life expectancy in Asian developing countries: Does renewable energy adoption matter?

Bo Yang, Fatma Ahmed Hassan, Waqar Ahmad et al.. Impact of air pollution on life expectancy in Asian developing countries: Does renewable energy adoption matter?. Frontiers in Public Health. 2026

This study investigated how air pollution affects life expectancy across developing Asian countries and whether transitioning to renewable energy can buffer these harmful effects. Using panel data from thirty developing Asian nations spanning over two decades, the researchers employed multiple econometric approaches, including fixed effects, system GMM, and instrumental variable estimation, to account for endogeneity and unobserved heterogeneity. Air pollution was proxied through carbon dioxide and nitrous oxide emissions given data limitations on direct ambient pollutant measures. The findings show that both emission types significantly reduce life expectancy, with carbon dioxide exerting a stronger adverse effect, while greater renewable energy adoption weakens the negative relationship between emissions and longevity. The authors conclude that clean energy transitions carry meaningful co-benefits for public health alongside environmental sustainability, with implications for international development goals related to health, affordable energy, and climate action.

Real‐world comprehensive care of people living with schizophrenia: recommendations across different settings and clinical stages

Paolo Fusar‐Poli, Toby Pillinger, Robert A. McCutcheon et al.. Real‐world comprehensive care of people living with schizophrenia: recommendations across different settings and clinical stages. World Psychiatry. 2026

This paper addresses the global challenge of managing schizophrenia by developing the first comprehensive, real-world care recommendations that span different clinical stages, treatment approaches, and resource settings. The recommendations were derived through a critical review of the scientific literature and collaborative input from clinical academics treating people with schizophrenia worldwide, including experts from the Global South and people with lived experience of the disorder. The resulting framework identifies multiple pillars of comprehensive care, ranging from early detection and pharmacological and psychological treatments to psychosocial interventions, community and inpatient care, and population-level prevention. A central overarching recommendation is to implement evidence-based, culturally sensitive care that addresses disparities between high- and low-resource settings, promotes early intervention, and employs task-sharing models involving non-professional health workers and traditional healers. The authors also highlight that future digital solutions may improve care efficiency across all settings, but that meaningful progress ultimately requires stronger mental health funding, improved access to care, and attention to the social determinants of health.

UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons

Linzhan Mou, Jiahui Lei, Zhiyang Dou et al.. UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of generating motion for arbitrary 3D rigged skeletons, a task where existing methods fail because they are constrained to specific skeleton topologies or require per-skeleton fine-tuning and reference motions at inference time. The authors introduce UniMate, a unified foundation model that takes a rigged 3D asset and a text prompt as input and synthesizes articulated motion without any test-time optimization or retraining. The core technical contribution is a topology-aware diffusion transformer that encodes skeletal structure into attention through graph-aware attention biases based on joint relations and geodesic distances, a spectral rotary position embedding adapted for arbitrary kinematic trees via the graph Laplacian, and a global topological conditioning mechanism. To support training across diverse creature types, the authors curated a large dataset spanning bipedal, quadrupedal, avian, marine, insectoid, serpentine, and rigid articulated objects with unified canonicalization and text descriptions. UniMate outperforms existing baselines in quality, generalization, and efficiency, and additionally supports zero-shot cross-topology transfer, motion in-betweening, expansion, and text-guided editing.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study confirms two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets orbiting solar-type stars near the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert, a region of parameter space where short-period planets with intermediate sizes are statistically rare. Using space-based photometry and a large set of ground-based radial velocity measurements, the authors characterized both planets' physical properties and discovered additional companions in each system, including a distant giant planet and an additional transiting sub-Neptune. Despite receiving extreme stellar irradiation, the densities of all three transiting planets require significant volatile content, and atmospheric escape modeling strongly favors water-dominated "steam world" interiors over primordial hydrogen/helium envelopes, which would be stripped on timescales far shorter than the systems' ages. A population-level analysis revealed a statistically significant excess of outer giant companions specifically among short-period sub-Neptunes residing within the tidal survival band near the desert's lower edge, suggesting that high-eccentricity tidal migration plays a major role in delivering planets to this region. Together, these findings indicate that the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert is shaped not solely by photoevaporation of light gas envelopes but also by dynamical migration processes that scatter planets onto tight orbits where tidal circularization determines their survival.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether machine learning can reconstruct the primordial density field of the Universe from observations made during the Epoch of Reionization, thereby recovering cosmological information that is otherwise obscured by non-linear gravitational evolution and astrophysical processes. The authors used simulated 21-cm and CO line-intensity maps generated at high redshift to train a three-dimensional U-Net neural network tasked with inferring the initial conditions at a much earlier epoch. The two emission lines were chosen because they trace complementary cosmic environments, with 21-cm radiation probing diffuse neutral gas and CO tracing dense, star-forming regions, and the simulations incorporated realistic instrumental effects from next-generation surveys. Combining both tracers yielded the best reconstruction performance, achieving high cross-correlation with the true initial density field across a range of scales even under noisy observational conditions. Crucially, applying simulation-based inference to power-spectrum summaries derived from the reconstructed fields tightened constraints on key cosmological parameters, with uncertainties on the amplitude of matter fluctuations and the spectral index approximately halving, demonstrating that joint multi-tracer reconstruction offers a viable path to recovering otherwise inaccessible cosmological information from forthcoming surveys.

WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data

Ji Soo Lee, Xilun Chen, Pierce Chuang et al.. WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces WearableQA, a benchmark designed to evaluate whether AI systems can reason over real-world longitudinal wearable health data from actual users. The benchmark consists of multiple-choice questions drawn from wearable time series, blood biomarkers, and demographic information spanning hundreds of days per user, preserving authentic data characteristics such as device noise and individual variability. To probe distinct reasoning capabilities, the authors defined question types along two axes — data versus health reasoning, and single- versus cross-signal reasoning — and used a dual-grounding framework combining literature-based physiological findings with statistically validated population patterns to construct reliable questions at scale. Evaluation of a range of proprietary and open-source large language models revealed substantial variation in performance and demonstrated that the benchmark effectively differentiates model capabilities. Notably, most models performed well below ceiling, indicating that reasoning over real-world wearable data remains a largely unsolved challenge for current AI systems.

Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence

Urja Pawar, Rajitha Ramanayake, Nabeel Kemal et al.. Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the explanations produced by large language models for their decisions accurately reflect the factors that actually drive those decisions. The researchers operationalized two interpretations of explanatory factors: necessity, testing whether altering a named factor changes the model's output, and sufficiency, testing whether retaining a named factor while removing other information preserves the output. Using controlled black-box interventions across two synthetic tasks—recommending advisors to clients and judging prompts for harmfulness—they evaluated eight models from three major model families. They found modest correlations between the factors models cite as most influential and measured necessity or sufficiency scores, and in a substantial proportion of cases an uncited factor demonstrated stronger influence than the weakest-ranked cited factor. The authors conclude that while model-generated explanations carry some useful signal, they do not reliably identify the factors with the greatest behavioural influence, raising concerns for their use in agent monitoring, error diagnosis, and escalation decisions.

Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models

Matthias Busch, Marius Tacke, Sviatlana V. Lamaka et al.. Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether large language models performing well on molecular property benchmarks are genuinely predicting properties or simply retrieving memorized published values. The authors audited a broad set of frontier language models across multiple molecular regression benchmarks, testing for verbatim digit-level retrieval of previously published numbers. They found that verbatim retrieval is widespread but uneven across benchmarks, with the majority of tested models exhibiting it on roughly half the datasets while it appears only sporadically on others. A notable finding is that reasoning level strongly influences retrieval behavior, with higher reasoning settings triggering flagged retrieval far more frequently than lower ones using identical prompts and molecules. When retrieval was suppressed through input transformations, prediction errors across models converged, suggesting that memorization inflates apparent performance differences and that genuine predictive capability is not simply a function of how many values a model has memorized.

Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.

Walzl G, McHugh TD, Chakaya J et al.. Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.. The Lancet. Microbe. 2027

This review synthesizes advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarker research over roughly a five-year period, covering how new evidence has shaped updated WHO policy guidance that consolidates recommendations for detecting tuberculosis disease and drug resistance into a unified framework. Digital chest radiography supported by computer-aided detection has emerged as a core screening and triage tool, while decentralised molecular diagnosis has expanded through multiple classes of rapid nucleic acid amplification tests, including both automated and manual platforms deployed at or near the point of care. Novel specimen types such as tongue swabs and non-sputum samples including stool and nasopharyngeal material have improved bacteriological confirmation in children and others who cannot produce sputum, and targeted next-generation sequencing has been endorsed for comprehensive drug-susceptibility testing at reference laboratory level. Host blood transcriptomic signatures show promise for prognosis and triage, and a range of proteomic, metabolomic, and other biomarker classes are under clinical evaluation, yet none has met the WHO target product profile for a point-of-care test capable of reliably distinguishing infection from disease, including subclinical forms. The authors conclude that significant gaps in diagnostic performance, implementation, and equity remain, defining priorities for future development.

The changing landscape of diagnosing HIV-associated tuberculosis.

Maartens G. The changing landscape of diagnosing HIV-associated tuberculosis.. South African medical journal = Suid-Afrikaanse tydskrif vir geneeskunde. 2026

This narrative review examines how the diagnosis of HIV-associated tuberculosis has evolved from 1990 to the present, focusing on the particular challenges posed by co-infection. Tuberculosis is especially difficult to diagnose in people with HIV because sputum bacillary loads are lower, extrapulmonary and disseminated disease is more common, and clinical features overlap with other opportunistic infections, all while disease progression can be rapid in those with advanced immunosuppression. The review traces how sole reliance on smear microscopy prior to the introduction of nucleic acid amplification tests and urine lipoarabinomannan lateral flow assays led to both underdiagnosis and overdiagnosis, each carrying serious clinical consequences. The advent of antiretroviral therapy alongside these novel rapid diagnostics has substantially reduced tuberculosis incidence and mortality, yet screening algorithms perform less well in people already on antiretroviral therapy and no currently available rapid test achieves ideal sensitivity. The authors conclude that, because only urine lipoarabinomannan detection combines affordability with point-of-care suitability, further development of accurate and accessible point-of-care diagnostics remains an urgent priority.

Near-point-of-care Diagnosis of Tuberculosis With LyocartE MTB Assay: a Multicenter Diagnostic Accuracy Study.

Ma Z, Yu X, Yu Y et al.. Near-point-of-care Diagnosis of Tuberculosis With LyocartE MTB Assay: a Multicenter Diagnostic Accuracy Study.. Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America. 2026

This multicenter prospective study evaluated the diagnostic accuracy of the LyocartE assay, a near point-of-care tuberculosis test, using both sputum and tongue swab specimens from individuals with presumptive tuberculosis at three sites in China. Participants underwent index testing with LyocartE alongside established comparator methods including Xpert Ultra, smear microscopy, and culture, with performance assessed against both Xpert Ultra results and a composite microbiological reference standard. On sputum specimens, LyocartE demonstrated high sensitivity and specificity comparable to Xpert Ultra, while tongue swab specimens also achieved strong specificity with somewhat lower sensitivity. Diagnostic accuracy remained consistent across different specimen types, clinical presentations, and comorbidities, and LyocartE cycle threshold values correlated strongly with those from Xpert Ultra. The authors conclude that LyocartE is a promising rapid diagnostic tool for tuberculosis, including from non-sputum specimens, though further evaluation in asymptomatic and paucibacillary populations is needed.

Operationalization of rapid molecular test coverage for tuberculosis in different international contexts: a scoping review.

Silva DHMD, Valença ABM, Ferreira MRL et al.. Operationalization of rapid molecular test coverage for tuberculosis in different international contexts: a scoping review.. Revista da Escola de Enfermagem da U S P. 2026

This scoping review examined how coverage of rapid molecular testing for tuberculosis has been defined and measured across different international settings. The authors systematically searched multiple major databases spanning over a decade to identify and synthesize relevant studies on this topic. They found substantial conceptual heterogeneity in how coverage was operationalized, with definitions ranging from proportions of tested populations to measures of equipment availability and geographic accessibility, and no standardization across countries. Reported coverage values varied enormously, and socioeconomic, structural, and financial barriers consistently hindered test access and utilization. The authors conclude that meaningful global monitoring of rapid molecular test coverage requires governmental commitment, decentralization of testing services, and the adoption of uniform measurement criteria aligned with World Health Organization guidance.

Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Urine-Based Molecular Testing for Urogenital Tuberculosis.

Fang L, Chen X, Chen J et al.. Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Urine-Based Molecular Testing for Urogenital Tuberculosis.. Archivos espanoles de urologia. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis evaluated the diagnostic accuracy of urine-based molecular tests — including Xpert Mycobacterium tuberculosis/rifampicin, multiplex polymerase chain reaction, and loop-mediated isothermal amplification — for detecting urogenital tuberculosis, a condition for which conventional culture is slow and inconsistent. The authors pooled results from diagnostic accuracy studies enrolling patients with high clinical suspicion of urogenital tuberculosis, assessing sensitivity, specificity, likelihood ratios, and diagnostic odds ratio using a bivariate random-effects model. The analysis found that urine-based molecular testing achieved high specificity and a strong area under the summary receiver operating characteristic curve, though sensitivity was moderate, indicating the tests reliably confirm disease when positive but are less reliable for ruling it out when negative. The choice of reference standard influenced specificity estimates, with bacterial culture as the reference yielding perfect specificity compared to composite reference standards. The authors conclude that urine-based molecular testing has substantial clinical utility as a rapid adjunctive tool for early confirmation of urogenital tuberculosis, best interpreted alongside clinical, imaging, and culture findings.

lIs molecular rapid diagnostic test for rifampicin and isoniazid tuberculosis resistance financially feasible? An economic evaluation in Indonesia.

Dharmawan INIP, Sumroni BIR, Hulwani S et al.. lIs molecular rapid diagnostic test for rifampicin and isoniazid tuberculosis resistance financially feasible? An economic evaluation in Indonesia.. International journal of infectious diseases : IJID : official publication of the International Society for Infectious Diseases. 2026

This study examined whether adding isoniazid resistance detection to existing rifampicin-only molecular rapid diagnostic testing for tuberculosis is economically justifiable in Indonesia. Using a decision-tree model, the researchers simulated diagnostic and treatment pathways for a large cohort of adults with presumptive pulmonary tuberculosis, tracking outcomes including treatment success, failure, death, and loss to follow-up across two treatment cycles. They found that simultaneous rifampicin and isoniazid testing increased upfront direct medical and non-medical costs in the first treatment cycle while meaningfully improving treatment success rates. However, this approach substantially reduced downstream costs associated with isoniazid-resistant tuberculosis in the second cycle. Overall, while the reduction in downstream costs from better detection of isoniazid resistance is a consistent finding, the total cost difference between the two testing strategies remains uncertain when parameter variability is accounted for.

Implementation of the NHS England Lung Cancer Screening Programme over 5 years

Richard W. Lee, Arjun Nair, Haval Balata et al.. Implementation of the NHS England Lung Cancer Screening Programme over 5 years. Nature Medicine. 2026

The paper reports on the implementation and early outcomes of the NHS England Lung Cancer Screening Programme, which offers low-dose computed tomography to ever-smokers aged 55 to 74 who meet risk thresholds determined by multivariable models. The programme was launched in 2019 and is delivered through regionally federated clinical infrastructure guided by national strategic, clinical, and economic frameworks, with the goal of achieving full national coverage by 2030. Over five years, more than two million people were invited, resulting in thousands of lung cancer diagnoses, the majority detected at an early tumour stage, representing a notable shift toward earlier-stage diagnosis across England and particularly in socioeconomically deprived regions. The authors conclude that large-scale, rapid implementation is achievable through centralized protocols and effective project management, and that the programme has demonstrated feasibility in reaching high-risk and underserved populations. They argue these findings support broader adoption of lung cancer screening across the United Kingdom and internationally, while acknowledging that inequalities in participation remain a challenge requiring further attention.

Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications

Deepak S. Chauhan, Rajendra Prasad, Mukesh Dhanka et al.. Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines nanoparticle-based phototherapy systems, focusing on how combining nanoparticles with light-activated treatment modalities enhances therapeutic precision across a broad range of diseases. The authors survey core components and classification schemes of these systems, including gold nanoparticles and hybrid constructs used in photothermal and photodynamic therapies, as well as delivery platforms such as liposomes and dendrimers that optimize biodistribution and drug release. At the molecular level, the review details how light-triggered interventions induce oxidative stress, activate apoptotic and autophagic pathways, and modulate immune responses including cytokine profiles and T-cell activity, with relevance extending beyond oncology to cardiovascular, neurodegenerative, metabolic, and autoimmune conditions. The authors also critically assess currently approved agents and recent clinical trial findings, identifying key obstacles to broader clinical adoption such as nanoparticle optimization, effective light delivery, and regulatory challenges. Overall, the review positions nanoparticle-based phototherapy as an emerging noninvasive strategy with transformative potential for treating a wide spectrum of diseases.
