EULAR recommendations for the management of Behçet’s syndrome: 2025 update

Gulen Hatemi, Sofia Ramiro, Yeşim Özgüler et al.. EULAR recommendations for the management of Behçet’s syndrome: 2025 update. Annals of the Rheumatic Diseases. 2026

This paper presents the updated clinical recommendations from a major European rheumatology alliance for managing Behçet's syndrome, a relapsing-remitting inflammatory disease with diverse organ manifestations. A large multinational task force spanning multiple specialties conducted a systematic literature review, used Delphi survey methods to formulate research questions, and then voted on updated guidance based on new evidence and expert consensus. The resulting framework comprises overarching principles and a set of specific recommendations organized by organ involvement, with one entirely new recommendation and the majority of existing ones modified to reflect current evidence. Key therapeutic guidance includes colchicine as the preferred first-line treatment for mucocutaneous and joint disease, with apremilast and TNFα inhibitors reserved for refractory cases, while more aggressive immunosuppression and early monoclonal anti-TNFα therapy are advised for organ-threatening or life-threatening manifestations. The recommendations also emphasize individualized treatment, multidisciplinary care, patient education, and shared decision-making as central principles for optimizing patient outcomes.

A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression

Kasper Favere, Matthias Bosman, Peter Delputte et al.. A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression. PubMed. 2026

This systematic review examined how different forms of exercise affect the expression of Toll-like receptors (TLRs), which are immune cell receptors that trigger inflammatory signalling cascades and have been implicated in chronic low-grade inflammation associated with sedentary behaviour and non-communicable diseases. The authors searched multiple databases for human studies in which exercise served as an isolated intervention, ultimately including dozens of articles categorised by exercise type and duration, covering acute and chronic resistance, aerobic, and combined training modalities. The review found that acute resistance exercise tended to upregulate TLR expression, while acute aerobic exercise showed a more neutral or downregulatory effect, particularly in healthy individuals. Chronic resistance and combined training programs were associated predominantly with unchanged or decreased TLR levels, whereas chronic aerobic exercise produced mixed results with most measurements showing no significant change. The authors conclude that exercise modulates TLR signalling in a manner dependent on exercise characteristics, but emphasise that substantial heterogeneity in study designs limits firm conclusions and that further research is needed to clarify the underlying mechanisms and pathophysiological implications.

The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Mahmoud M. Dahab et al.. The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development. Frontiers in Pharmacology. 2026

This narrative review examines the global crisis of antimicrobial resistance with a focus on the bacterial pathogens designated as priorities by the World Health Organization. The authors synthesize current understanding of the molecular mechanisms by which these pathogens resist antibiotics, encompassing reduced membrane permeability, efflux pump overexpression, enzymatic drug inactivation, target modification, biofilm formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review critically evaluates why conventional antibiotic discovery pipelines have largely failed to keep pace with resistance, and assesses a range of emerging therapeutic strategies—including artificial intelligence-assisted drug discovery, phage therapy, antimicrobial peptides, CRISPR-based approaches, and natural product-derived compounds—while addressing their translational, regulatory, and clinical challenges. The authors also emphasize the importance of antimicrobial stewardship and surveillance within a One Health framework that integrates human, animal, and environmental health sectors. The overarching conclusion is that combating priority resistant pathogens will require integrated, multidisciplinary approaches spanning molecular biology, pharmacology, clinical translation, and public health.

UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons

Linzhan Mou, Jiahui Lei, Zhiyang Dou et al.. UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of generating motion for arbitrary rigged 3D skeletons, a bottleneck in animation pipelines where existing methods are constrained to specific skeleton topologies or require per-skeleton fine-tuning and reference motions at inference time. The authors introduce UniMate, a unified diffusion transformer model that takes a rigged 3D asset and a text prompt as input and synthesizes articulated motion without any test-time optimization or retraining. To handle arbitrary skeletal topologies, the model incorporates three mechanisms: a graph-aware attention bias based on pairwise joint relations and geodesic distances, a spectral rotary positional embedding generalized to arbitrary kinematic trees via the graph Laplacian, and a global topological conditioning module. The authors also curate a large-scale dataset spanning a diverse range of creature and object categories with unified canonicalization and text pairing to support training. UniMate is reported to outperform state-of-the-art baselines in quality, generalization, and efficiency, while enabling zero-shot cross-topology transfer, motion in-betweening, expansion, and text-guided editing.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigates two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets situated near the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert, a region of orbital parameter space notably deficient in planets of intermediate size. Using space-based photometry and a large set of ground-based radial velocity measurements, the researchers confirmed the existence and measured the physical properties of TOI-426 b and TOI-1839 b, both on very short orbital periods and receiving over a thousand times Earth's stellar irradiation, yet both retaining substantial volatile content despite this intense heating. Interior structure and atmospheric escape modeling strongly favors a water-dominated "steam world" interpretation for these planets over a primordial hydrogen/helium envelope, as hydrogen/helium envelopes would be lost on timescales far shorter than water-dominated ones under the observed irradiation levels. The team also identified additional companions in both systems, including a long-period giant planet orbiting TOI-426, and conducted a population-level analysis revealing a striking overabundance of outer giant companions among short-period sub-Neptunes near the desert's lower edge compared to those outside the relevant tidal survival band. The authors conclude that the persistence of volatile-rich planets in this region cannot be explained by atmospheric evaporation alone, and that high-eccentricity tidal migration driven by outer giant companions likely plays a significant role in populating and shaping the lower edge of the Neptunian desert.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated whether machine learning could recover the primordial density field of the Universe from observations of the Epoch of Reionization, thereby retrieving cosmological information otherwise lost to non-linear gravitational evolution and astrophysical processes. The researchers used simulated line-intensity maps from two complementary tracers — 21-cm emission, which traces neutral low-density gas, and CO rotational line emission, which traces dense star-forming regions — generated with a dedicated simulation framework, and trained a three-dimensional U-Net to reconstruct the initial conditions at very high redshift. Instrumental effects representative of planned radio and millimeter-wave surveys were incorporated to emulate realistic observational conditions, including finite angular resolution and thermal noise. Combining both tracers yielded the best reconstruction performance, maintaining high cross-correlation with the true initial density field across a broad range of scales even in the presence of noise. Crucially, performing simulation-based inference of cosmological parameters from power-spectrum summaries after reconstruction substantially tightened parameter constraints, with uncertainties on the amplitude of matter fluctuations and the spectral index roughly halving, demonstrating that joint multi-tracer reconstruction can recover cosmological information that would otherwise remain inaccessible.

WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data

Ji Soo Lee, Xilun Chen, Pierce Chuang et al.. WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a benchmark designed to evaluate whether AI systems can reason meaningfully over real-world longitudinal wearable health data. The benchmark was constructed from physiological time series, blood biomarkers, and demographic information collected from real users over extended periods, organized into multiple-choice questions spanning sixteen distinct question types that probe both data computation and physiological interpretation, as well as single-signal versus multi-signal reasoning. Question construction relied on a dual-grounding framework combining literature-derived physiological knowledge with statistically validated patterns observed in the actual dataset, ensuring that questions capture genuine health-relevant relationships rather than artifacts. A large set of proprietary and open-source large language models were evaluated, revealing substantial performance variation across models and demonstrating that the benchmark effectively differentiates model capabilities. Despite this spread, most models performed well below ceiling, indicating that sophisticated reasoning over real-world wearable data remains an unsolved challenge for current AI systems.

Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence

Urja Pawar, Rajitha Ramanayake, Nabeel Kemal et al.. Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the explanations that large language models provide alongside their decisions actually reflect the factors that behaviorally drive those decisions. The researchers tested two interpretations of model-cited explanatory factors: necessity, meaning that altering a named factor would change the model's output, and sufficiency, meaning that preserving only that factor would be enough to maintain the original output. Using controlled black-box interventions across two synthetic tasks—recommending advisors to clients and judging prompts for harmfulness—they measured necessity and sufficiency scores for cited and uncited factors across multiple models from major commercial families. They found only moderate correlations between the ranking of factors cited by the models and the empirically measured necessity and sufficiency scores, and in a substantial proportion of cases, factors the models did not cite scored higher than the lowest-ranked factor the models did cite. The study concludes that while model-generated explanations carry useful information, they do not reliably identify the factors with the strongest measured behavioral influence, raising concerns for their use in agent monitoring and oversight.

Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models

Matthias Busch, Marius Tacke, Sviatlana V. Lamaka et al.. Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether large language models evaluated on molecular property benchmarks are genuinely predicting chemical properties or simply retrieving memorized values from their training data. The researchers audited a broad set of frontier language models across numerous regression benchmarks, testing for digit-level verbatim retrieval of published experimental values. They found that verbatim retrieval is widespread but uneven across benchmarks, with the majority of tested models showing retrieval behavior on roughly half the datasets while other datasets showed only isolated cases. Notably, the level of reasoning employed by the model significantly influenced retrieval rates, with higher reasoning settings flagged for retrieval far more often than lower ones, and even attempts to disrupt retrieval using transformed molecular representations were partially overcome by stronger models. The study concludes that verbatim retrieval inflates apparent performance differences between models, and that suppressing it brings model errors closer together, suggesting that genuine predictive capability is distinct from the degree of data memorization.

sexually transmitted infection point-of-care testing

Topic mode · generated September 07, 2026 at 17:40 UTC · 17 items

Community peer-led STI/HIV point-of-care testing and knowledge, attitude and practice (KAP) survey in men who have sex with men (MSM) attending an NGO-led clinic in the outskirts of Beirut (Lebanon).

Abou Shahla W, Rossoni I, Padovese V. Community peer-led STI/HIV point-of-care testing and knowledge, attitude and practice (KAP) survey in men who have sex with men (MSM) attending an NGO-led clinic in the outskirts of Beirut (Lebanon).. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This pilot study investigated the utility of point-of-care testing for HIV, syphilis, and hepatitis among men who have sex with men attending a non-governmental organisation-led clinic in Beirut, Lebanon, while also assessing their sexual health knowledge, attitudes, and practices. Using a cross-sectional design over four months, researchers conducted sexual health consultations, STI and HIV testing, and an anonymous questionnaire covering demographics, STI awareness, condom use, chemsex, sexual violence, and perceived stigma. The study found that high levels of STI and HIV awareness among participants coexisted with condomless sex and recreational drug use, and that a substantial proportion of participants were living with HIV. HIV-related stigma and perceived community beliefs emerged as significant barriers to seeking testing. The authors conclude that integrating point-of-care testing into sexual health services enables same-day diagnosis, immediate counselling, and rapid linkage to confirmatory testing and care, and that addressing stigma is essential to reducing risky behaviours in this population.

Point-of-care screening for curable sexually transmitted infections in antenatal care in Zimbabwe: a costing study.

Martin K, Bath D, Nyamwanza R et al.. Point-of-care screening for curable sexually transmitted infections in antenatal care in Zimbabwe: a costing study.. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This study examined the costs of integrating point-of-care screening for three curable sexually transmitted infections into antenatal care in Zimbabwe, compared to the standard syndromic management approach. Researchers implemented screening at two primary healthcare clinics in Harare, collecting economic cost data through staff interviews, time-and-motion studies, and administrative records, then modelled implementation in a low-volume routine care setting over a twelve-month period. A substantial proportion of pregnant women tested positive for at least one of the targeted infections, confirming the relevance of screening in this context. The largest cost drivers were test kits and personnel, which together accounted for the majority of total intervention costs. The authors conclude that the incremental cost of point-of-care antenatal STI screening over syndromic management is primarily driven by diagnostic consumable and staffing expenses, highlighting the need for cheaper point-of-care tests to make such programmes more feasible at scale.

Cost-effectiveness of a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections in remote primary care health services in Australia.

Watts CG, Causer LM, Matthews SJ et al.. Cost-effectiveness of a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections in remote primary care health services in Australia.. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This study evaluated the cost-effectiveness of a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections in remote Australian First Nations communities, where the burden of chlamydia, gonorrhoea, and trichomonas is particularly high among young adults. Researchers constructed a decision analytic model comparing the point-of-care programme against standard laboratory-based testing over a ten-year horizon, using a government payer perspective with cost per quality-adjusted life year as the primary outcome. The model incorporated high observed infection positivity rates and accounted for downstream complications including pelvic inflammatory disease and adverse birth outcomes. The point-of-care programme was found to be cost-effective relative to standard care, and was predicted to meaningfully reduce rates of diagnosed pelvic inflammatory disease and preterm or low birth weight births among those with an infection. The analysis concluded that sustained, scaled implementation of this testing approach in remote primary care settings is likely to be a worthwhile investment with broader reproductive health benefits.

Novel Multiplex Near-Point-of-Care PCR Assay for Detecting Sexually Transmitted Infections Among PrEP Users in Western Kenya: Protocol for a Cross-Sectional Pilot Study With an Epidemiological Assessment.

Sohaili A, Mogaka F, Alexiou Z et al.. Novel Multiplex Near-Point-of-Care PCR Assay for Detecting Sexually Transmitted Infections Among PrEP Users in Western Kenya: Protocol for a Cross-Sectional Pilot Study With an Epidemiological Assessment.. JMIR research protocols. 2026

This study aims to validate a novel near-point-of-care molecular diagnostic platform, FlashDx, for detecting multiple sexually transmitted infections in a resource-limited setting where access to centralized laboratory diagnostics is constrained. The researchers designed a cross-sectional pilot study enrolling young pre-exposure prophylaxis users in Kisumu, Kenya, collecting urine samples and testing them with the FlashDx multiplex chip-based assay capable of simultaneously detecting six pathogens, including Chlamydia trachomatis, Neisseria gonorrhoeae, Trichomonas vaginalis, Mycoplasma genitalium, Mycoplasma hominis, and Ureaplasma species. Results from FlashDx are being compared against a certified reference real-time PCR assay processed in the Netherlands to assess diagnostic sensitivity, specificity, and predictive values. Data collection has been completed and the study is currently in the analysis phase, with final results anticipated in the near future. The authors anticipate that FlashDx will demonstrate sufficient accuracy and reliability to support broader decentralized implementation across Kenya and other low- and middle-income countries.

Mechanisms, management and prevention of anorectal sexually transmitted infections.

Latt PM, Kong FYS, Khosropour CM et al.. Mechanisms, management and prevention of anorectal sexually transmitted infections.. Nature reviews. Gastroenterology & hepatology. 2026

This review examines the epidemiology, pathogenic mechanisms, diagnosis, and prevention of anorectal bacterial sexually transmitted infections, focusing primarily on chlamydia, gonorrhoea, and syphilis. The authors synthesize current evidence on why these infections are particularly challenging to control, noting that they are frequently asymptomatic—especially among men who have sex with men—due to bacterial adaptation to rectal tissues and attenuated local immune responses. Molecular diagnostics have greatly improved detection, but distinguishing viable from non-viable organisms, expanding point-of-care access, and managing antimicrobial stewardship remain significant obstacles. Treatment is complicated by evolving antimicrobial resistance, particularly for gonorrhoea, while prevention advances such as doxycycline post-exposure prophylaxis show promise in reducing incidence of certain infections, alongside emerging artificial intelligence applications. The authors identify critical knowledge gaps and propose research priorities to improve control strategies for these infections, whose global incidence continues to rise despite available testing.

Compulsive sexual behavior disorder (CSBD) and problematic pornography use (PPU): A comprehensive, interdisciplinary, and expert-informed narrative review with suggested future directions

Campbell Ince, Stephanie Antons, Sarah Ashton et al.. Compulsive sexual behavior disorder (CSBD) and problematic pornography use (PPU): A comprehensive, interdisciplinary, and expert-informed narrative review with suggested future directions. Journal of Behavioral Addictions. 2026

This narrative review examined the current state of scientific and clinical knowledge on Compulsive Sexual Behavior Disorder and Problematic Pornography Use, two closely related but debated constructs with implications for mental health, relationships, and well-being. Drawing on contributions from experts across psychology, psychiatry, sexology, addiction science, and neuroscience, the authors synthesized empirical and theoretical literature across eleven thematic domains, including historical context, assessment, epidemiology, etiology, co-occurring disorders, consequences, and treatment. The review also addressed underrepresented populations such as adolescents, women, and LGBTQ+ individuals, as well as cultural factors that shape how these behaviors are understood and experienced. The authors identify persistent methodological challenges and ongoing conceptual controversies surrounding how these behaviors should be classified and studied. The work concludes by calling for greater interdisciplinary collaboration, more rigorous research methods, and better integration of findings into clinical practice and public health policy.

An epidemiological approach to sexually transmitted diseases – with special reference to contact tracing and screening

Kristina Ramstedt. An epidemiological approach to sexually transmitted diseases – with special reference to contact tracing and screening. Acta Dermato Venereologica. 2026

This thesis examined epidemiological strategies for controlling sexually transmitted diseases, with particular focus on contact tracing and screening as tools for identifying asymptomatic infected individuals. The research evaluated contact tracing routines, compared characteristics of chlamydia-infected women and their partners across different clinical settings, and investigated whether contact tracing data could inform mathematical models of partner choice and disease spread. Key findings included that contact tracing effectively controlled a gonorrhoea microepidemic and identified substantial numbers of previously undiagnosed HIV-positive individuals, while screening at family planning clinics revealed meaningful chlamydia prevalence associated with factors such as younger age, short relationship duration, and lack of condom use. Comparing chlamydia cases identified through screening versus those presenting at sexually transmitted disease clinics showed only minor differences in partner numbers and infection rates, suggesting that management ambitions should not differ between these groups. Partner choice data indicated that steady partnerships were formed in a restricted manner while casual partnerships followed a more random pattern, meaning that a purely random mixing model was insufficient to describe disease transmission dynamics.

Multifactorial determinants of health status: insights from the MEDIET4ALL large-scale survey on eco-sociodemographic, psychological, and lifestyle (diet, physical activity, and sleep) factors

Achraf Ammar, Atef Salem, Mohamed Ali Boujelbane et al.. Multifactorial determinants of health status: insights from the MEDIET4ALL large-scale survey on eco-sociodemographic, psychological, and lifestyle (diet, physical activity, and sleep) factors. Frontiers in Public Health. 2026

This large-scale survey study examined the multifactorial determinants of health status across Mediterranean and neighboring countries, focusing on how geo-demographic, socioeconomic, psychological, and lifestyle factors collectively shape individual health outcomes. Researchers analyzed data from thousands of participants using a multinational e-survey, applying multinomial logistic regression and related statistical models to categorize respondents as healthy, at-risk, or having known diseases. Body mass index emerged as the strongest negative predictor of health status, with both obesity and underweight substantially increasing the odds of being at risk or having disease, while psychological distress, insomnia, alcohol consumption, and prolonged sitting also negatively predicted health. Conversely, life satisfaction was the strongest protective factor, followed by higher education, better sleep quality, and adherence to the Mediterranean diet and lifestyle, with rural residence, employment, female sex, and living in Mediterranean regions also associated with more favorable health outcomes. The findings underscore the complex interplay among lifestyle behaviors, mental health, and socioeconomic circumstances in shaping health, and the authors call for longitudinal research and multi-level public health interventions to address these interconnected determinants.

A Novel Index Measure of Housing-Related Risk as a Predictor of Overdose Among Young People Who Inject Drugs and Injection Networks

Kathleen Kristensen, Leslie D. Williams, Charlie Kaplan et al.. A Novel Index Measure of Housing-Related Risk as a Predictor of Overdose Among Young People Who Inject Drugs and Injection Networks. Journal of Urban Health. 2026

This study examined the relationship between housing instability and drug overdose risk among young people who inject drugs, arguing that existing research has been limited by treating housing instability as a single-dimensional construct. The researchers developed a multi-dimensional housing instability risk index composed of five dichotomous variables and applied it to baseline data from a network-based longitudinal study of young people who inject drugs and their social networks in metropolitan Chicago. Using negative binomial regression adjusted for multiple covariates, they found a significant positive association between the housing instability risk index score and lifetime overdose count. The findings support the value of a multi-dimensional approach to measuring housing instability over simpler single-variable operationalizations, and the authors argue that interventions targeting overdose risk should adopt a more holistic framework when addressing housing instability.

Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads

Karan Malhotra, Louise L. Hansen, Samuel Han et al.. Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads. Journal of the American Chemical Society. 2026

This paper developed an enzyme-free platform for ultrasensitive protein detection aimed at making advanced diagnostic assays accessible in resource-limited settings. The researchers created a method called enzyme-free MOSAIC (efMOSAIC), which uses hybridization chain reaction to generate signal amplifiers on magnetic beads without requiring enzymes, simplifying the workflow compared to existing digital protein detection technologies like Simoa and conventional MOSAIC. The platform achieves detection limits comparable to Simoa for most proteins, demonstrates multiplexed biomarker detection, and was validated for sensitive cytokine detection from human plasma. A key practical advantage is that the signal amplifier reagents remain stable for up to six months when stored at room temperature in the dark, eliminating cold-chain requirements. The authors conclude that efMOSAIC's simple workflows and temperature-stable reagents position it as a transformative tool for ultrasensitive protein quantification in low- and middle-income countries and point-of-care settings.

Malaria in the 21st Century: Global Disease Burden, Epidemiological Insights, and Strategic Control Approaches

Basmah F. Alharbi, Mawahib A. Ahmed. Malaria in the 21st Century: Global Disease Burden, Epidemiological Insights, and Strategic Control Approaches. Biology. 2026

This review examines the contemporary global burden of malaria, a parasitic disease transmitted primarily through infected female Anopheles mosquitoes but also via blood transfusions, organ transplants, and congenital routes. Drawing on the most recent World Health Organization World Malaria Report and Global Burden of Disease estimates, the authors synthesize current epidemiological data on regional trends, socio-economic determinants, and existing control strategies. The review highlights persistent challenges including drug resistance, climate change, and insufficient funding that continue to undermine progress despite decades of intervention efforts, with the greatest mortality concentrated in low- and middle-income countries. The authors argue that effective disease prevention and management require multifactorial, locally tailored approaches spanning primary, secondary, and tertiary health prevention, supported by contextually relevant health education. The review concludes by outlining policy priorities necessary to achieve the WHO Global Technical Strategy target of malaria elimination by the end of this decade.

UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons

Linzhan Mou, Jiahui Lei, Zhiyang Dou et al.. UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons. arXiv. 2026 preprint

UniMate is a unified foundation model designed to generate articulated motion for arbitrary 3D skeletons from a rigged asset and a text prompt, addressing the limitation of existing animation systems that are constrained to specific skeleton topologies or require per-skeleton fine-tuning. The model introduces a topology-aware diffusion transformer that encodes skeletal structure into attention mechanisms through graph-aware attention biases based on joint relations and geodesic distances, a spectral rotary positional embedding generalized to arbitrary kinematic trees via the graph Laplacian, and a global topological conditioner derived from the rest-pose skeleton. To support training across diverse creature types, the authors curated a large dataset spanning bipedal, quadrupedal, avian, marine, insectoid, serpentine, and articulated rigid-object motions with unified canonicalization and text pairing. UniMate outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in motion quality, generalization, and efficiency without requiring test-time optimization, and supports zero-shot cross-topology transfer, motion in-betweening, sequence expansion, and text-guided editing.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigated two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets situated near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert, a region in orbital period–radius space largely devoid of intermediate-sized planets. Using space-based photometry and a large set of ground-based radial velocity measurements, the researchers confirmed the existence of these two planets and characterized their masses, radii, and orbital properties, also detecting additional companions in both systems. Interior structure and atmospheric escape modeling strongly favor a water-dominated "steam world" composition for all three transiting planets discovered, as hydrogen/helium envelopes would be lost on timescales far shorter than the age of the systems. The survival of volatile-rich planets in such extreme irradiation environments challenges the idea that the lower desert boundary is determined solely by photoevaporation of primordial hydrogen/helium atmospheres. A population-level analysis further reveals a statistically significant excess of outer giant companions around sub-Neptunes near the desert edge compared to those further away, supporting high-eccentricity tidal migration as a key mechanism for delivering planets to, and shaping, the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert.

Energy-Efficient Waveform Design for ISAC Systems: An Ambiguity-Domain QoS Perspective

Ngoc-Son Duong, Trung-Hieu Nguyen, Quang-Truong Can. Energy-Efficient Waveform Design for ISAC Systems: An Ambiguity-Domain QoS Perspective. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of designing transmit waveforms for integrated sensing and communication systems that simultaneously meet sensing quality, communication quality, and hardware compatibility requirements while minimizing energy consumption. The authors introduce a novel sensing quality metric called the ambiguity-domain sensing signal-to-interference-plus-noise ratio, which measures the ratio of the desired ambiguity function mainlobe power to weighted sidelobe leakage and noise within a localized delay-Doppler region, providing a more targeted and noise-aware characterization of sensing performance. The waveform design problem must additionally satisfy per-user communication signal-to-interference-plus-noise ratio constraints and a peak-to-average power ratio constraint for practical radio-frequency implementation. Because the resulting optimization problem is nonconvex owing to fractional objectives, quartic ambiguity function terms, and the waveform-dependent hardware constraint, the authors develop a fractional-programming successive-convex-approximation algorithm to solve it. Simulation results confirm that the proposed approach meets all imposed requirements while achieving effective suppression of ambiguity function sidelobes within the target delay-Doppler region.

Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence

Urja Pawar, Rajitha Ramanayake, Nabeel Kemal et al.. Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the explanations provided by large language models alongside their decisions are faithful to the models' actual decision-making behaviour. The researchers tested two interpretations of faithfulness—necessity, asking whether changing a cited factor would alter the output, and sufficiency, asking whether retaining a cited factor while removing others would preserve the output—across two synthetic use cases involving advisor recommendation and prompt harmfulness monitoring. Using controlled black-box interventions across multiple models from three major model families, they measured how well the factors models claim influenced their decisions correspond to factors that behaviourally matter under each interpretation. They found only moderate correlations between the cited factor rankings and measured necessity and sufficiency scores, and in a substantial proportion of cases an uncited factor showed stronger measured influence than the weakest cited factor. The authors conclude that while model-provided explanations carry useful information, they do not reliably identify the most influential factors, and propose their framework as a practical black-box reliability check for explanations used in agent oversight contexts.

Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models

Matthias Busch, Marius Tacke, Sviatlana V. Lamaka et al.. Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether large language models evaluated on molecular property benchmarks are genuinely predicting properties or simply memorizing and retrieving published numerical values. The authors audited a large set of frontier language models across multiple regression benchmarks, testing for verbatim digit-level retrieval of known results. They found that verbatim retrieval is widespread but benchmark-specific, with more than half of tested models showing retrieval behavior on some datasets while others showed it only sporadically. Notably, higher reasoning levels substantially increased the frequency of detected retrieval compared to lower reasoning levels, and even when retrieval was disrupted through transformed molecular representations, the strongest models could still recognize original labels. The study concludes that suppressing retrieval brings model prediction errors closer together, suggesting that memorized values inflate apparent performance differences and that genuine predictive capability is not simply a function of how much a model has memorized.

Propagation Model for SSC attacks: Why SBOM (tools) don't tell the whole truth

Ljubica Grgic, Lazar Maksimovic, Pavel Laskov. Propagation Model for SSC attacks: Why SBOM (tools) don't tell the whole truth. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper examines limitations of Software Bill of Materials (SBOM) tools in securing software supply chains, arguing that these tools fail to capture how vulnerabilities propagate through an entire chain. To investigate this gap, the authors developed a four-stage propagation model and empirically tested four open-source SBOM tools against each stage using three real-world projects and the Log4j vulnerability as a case study. Their findings reveal that current SBOM tools consistently address only the first two stages — structural exposure and vulnerability class presence — while lacking the capabilities needed for code reachability analysis and taint path analysis. The authors contend that this blind spot means SBOM tools cannot adequately explain or predict exploitability effects that spread across supply chain components, potentially allowing cyber risks to escalate into systemic risks. They call for propagation effects to be placed at the center of future software supply chain security research and tool design.

CRISPR based diagnostics infectious disease detection

Topic mode · generated September 07, 2026 at 17:38 UTC · 18 items

CRISPR-Cas12a assay for rapid and specific detection of &lt;i&gt;Shigella flexneri 2a&lt;/i&gt; in clinical samples.

Liao J, Su Y, Jiang F. CRISPR-Cas12a assay for rapid and specific detection of &lt;i&gt;Shigella flexneri 2a&lt;/i&gt; in clinical samples.. Journal of clinical microbiology. 2026

The paper developed and validated a CRISPR-Cas12a diagnostic assay for the rapid and specific detection of *Shigella flexneri 2a*, the predominant serotype causing shigellosis and a major public health burden in developing countries. The assay was designed using two guide RNAs targeting genes unique to the *S. flexneri 2a* serotype, coupled with recombinase polymerase amplification to enable isothermal signal amplification detectable by either fluorescence or lateral flow without specialized equipment. Analytical testing showed the assay achieved a detection limit comparable to conventional PCR, with complete specificity and no cross-reactivity against other bacteria, and the entire reaction was completed within one hour at room temperature. Clinical validation using a large cohort of stool specimens demonstrated diagnostic sensitivity and specificity comparable to PCR, confirming the assay's accuracy in real-world samples. The authors conclude that this approach offers a simple, rapid, and highly accurate tool well suited for both clinical diagnostics and field-based surveillance in resource-limited settings.

An integrated centrifugal microfluidic CRISPR-based diagnostics platform for multiplexed point-of-care testing of respiratory pathogens.

Li L, Wei H, Wei M et al.. An integrated centrifugal microfluidic CRISPR-based diagnostics platform for multiplexed point-of-care testing of respiratory pathogens.. Biosensors & bioelectronics. 2026

The paper developed and validated a centrifugal microfluidic diagnostic platform called iCARD for rapid, multiplexed detection of respiratory pathogens at the point of care. The system integrates CRISPR/Cas13a chemistry with lyophilized reagents pre-loaded onto a polymethyl methacrylate disc, paired with a portable fluorescence analyzer and a novel resin-based nucleic acid purification method for throat swab samples. The platform enables simultaneous screening of six respiratory pathogens across four independent samples in a single automated run within tens of minutes, achieving single-copy analytical sensitivity without cross-reactivity. Clinical validation using retrospective patient throat swabs demonstrated very high diagnostic sensitivity and perfect specificity, supporting its accuracy relative to established methods. The authors conclude that iCARD represents a robust tool for decentralized molecular diagnostics and epidemiological surveillance of respiratory infections.

CRISPR RNA Engineering Enables Single Nucleotide Polymorphism Discrimination in Nucleic Acid Detection.

Sun T, Yuan A, Xie W et al.. CRISPR RNA Engineering Enables Single Nucleotide Polymorphism Discrimination in Nucleic Acid Detection.. Angewandte Chemie (International ed. in English). 2026

This review examines how CRISPR-based diagnostic systems can be engineered to achieve single nucleotide polymorphism discrimination in nucleic acid detection, addressing the persistent problem of off-target activation and insufficient sequence specificity that limits clinical reliability. The authors synthesize advances in CRISPR RNA engineering strategies, including spacer length optimization, intentional mismatch design, secondary-structure modulation, chemical modification, strand-displacement gating, and synergistic design frameworks, explaining how these approaches govern the energetic and kinetic thresholds governing accurate target cleavage. Computational prediction, high-throughput screening, and hybrid guide architectures are reviewed for their capacity to improve single-nucleotide discrimination and enable multiplexed detection relevant to pathogen profiling and mutation identification. The review concludes that while progress has been rapid, significant challenges remain, including interference from complex clinical samples, absence of unified evaluation standards, and scalability barriers that impede clinical translation. The authors argue that integrated crRNA design combined with system-level optimization and standardized benchmarking will be essential to realize ultrasensitive, portable point-of-care diagnostics capable of supporting precision medicine and infectious disease surveillance.

Attomolar Detection of HIV-1 With Label-Free RCA-rCRISPR on Smartphone.

Mohammad N, Steksova A, Tang Y et al.. Attomolar Detection of HIV-1 With Label-Free RCA-rCRISPR on Smartphone.. Advanced science (Weinheim, Baden-Wurttemberg, Germany). 2026

This paper developed a low-cost, label-free diagnostic platform for detecting HIV viral load in resource-limited settings where conventional equipment is unavailable. The approach combines rolling circle amplification with a ratiometric CRISPR system, leveraging the triple enzymatic functions of phi29 DNA polymerase to amplify long HIV genome sequences without pre-fragmentation, followed by Cas12a detection that converts supercoiled plasmid reporters to relaxed forms for quantification. The platform achieves single-digit attomolar sensitivity when tested on patient-derived HIV samples and was integrated into a smartphone-based miniature gel electrophoresis device to enable equipment-free viral load testing. The system was also demonstrated to detect BRAF point mutations, suggesting broad applicability beyond HIV diagnostics.

SARS-CoV-2 Point-of-Care Testing Modalities: Integrating Molecular, Immunological, Biosensor, and AI Approaches.

Hetta HF, Ahmed R, Haseeb A et al.. SARS-CoV-2 Point-of-Care Testing Modalities: Integrating Molecular, Immunological, Biosensor, and AI Approaches.. Diagnostics (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This review examined the landscape of point-of-care testing technologies for COVID-19, motivated by the pandemic's demonstration that rapid, decentralized diagnostics are essential for clinical decision-making and public health surveillance. The authors conducted a structured literature search across major scientific databases to evaluate molecular assays, antigen and antibody tests, biosensor platforms, and artificial intelligence-assisted diagnostic approaches. Molecular methods such as rapid RT-PCR, LAMP, and CRISPR-based technologies were found to offer high sensitivity and specificity suitable for decentralized use, while antigen tests provide speed and cost-effectiveness but with variable performance depending on viral load and circulating variants. Antibody-based testing proved most useful for seroprevalence and immune monitoring rather than acute diagnosis, and emerging biosensor and AI-enabled systems showed promise but largely remain at prototype stages requiring further clinical validation. The review concludes that no single modality is universally optimal and that combining complementary approaches, alongside advances in multiplexing, digital connectivity, and variant-resilient detection, will be key to strengthening future infectious disease preparedness.

Wax-in-a-Tube: A Simple, Rapid, One-Pot Platform for Molecular Detection.

Hou C, Yang R, Guan X et al.. Wax-in-a-Tube: A Simple, Rapid, One-Pot Platform for Molecular Detection.. ACS sensors. 2026

This paper developed a one-pot molecular detection platform that combines isothermal amplification with CRISPR-based nucleic acid detection, addressing workflow complications inherent to conventional two-step assays. The researchers created a wax-in-a-tube system in which a molded wax separator physically partitions different reaction components within a single tube, melting upon heating to merge the reagents and eliminate manual mixing steps such as vortexing or centrifugation. The platform demonstrated extremely high detection sensitivity for HSV-2 DNA and was validated against clinical swab samples, with results comparable to standard PCR methods. By removing manual transfer steps, the design also reduces the risk of aerosol contamination, a key concern in nucleic acid testing. The authors conclude that this approach offers a simple, rapid, and sensitive solution well suited to point-of-care infectious disease detection and surveillance.

New approach methodologies for drug discovery

Wenqiang Liu, Paul Pang, Catherine A. Wu et al.. New approach methodologies for drug discovery. Cell. 2026

This review examines the growing field of new approach methodologies as alternatives to traditional animal-based drug discovery, which has historically suffered from high failure rates. The authors survey recent advances in stem cell-based, organoid-based, and computational methodologies, situating them within evolving regulatory frameworks from major health agencies. The review covers how these tools are being applied across the full drug-discovery continuum, from disease modeling and drug design through efficacy testing, and notably considers cases where the methodologies themselves may serve as therapeutic treatments. The authors identify key biological, technical, and regulatory bottlenecks that currently impede broad translational adoption, and conclude by outlining future human-centric discovery pipelines alongside the societal considerations necessary for responsible implementation.

Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications

Deepak S. Chauhan, Rajendra Prasad, Mukesh Dhanka et al.. Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines nanoparticle-based phototherapy systems, which use light-activated mechanisms to treat a broad range of diseases by enhancing light absorption, targeting, and amplification effects such as reactive oxygen species generation and localized heating. The authors survey the core components and classification of these systems, including gold nanoparticles, hybrid constructs, liposomes, and dendrimers, which improve biodistribution and release kinetics in both photodynamic and photothermal therapy contexts. At the molecular level, the review dissects how light-triggered interventions induce oxidative stress, apoptotic and autophagic cascades, and immune modulation through cytokine and T-cell activity changes, with relevance not only to cancer but also to cardiovascular, neurodegenerative, metabolic, and autoimmune conditions. The authors further evaluate regulatory-approved agents and recent clinical trial findings, while identifying key barriers to clinical translation including nanoparticle optimization, light delivery efficiency, and regulatory challenges. Overall, the review positions nanoparticle-based phototherapy as a transformative noninvasive strategy with the potential to reshape treatment across a wide spectrum of diseases.

Cell death in cancer

Marcus Conrad, Andreas Strasser, Philipp J. Jost et al.. Cell death in cancer. Cell. 2026

This review examines the diverse programmed cell death pathways relevant to cancer biology and therapy, focusing on how their evasion enables tumor development and treatment resistance. The authors synthesize two decades of research during which the field expanded well beyond apoptosis to encompass necroptosis, pyroptosis, ferroptosis, and other emerging modalities. While apoptosis remains the principal mechanism through which radiation and chemotherapy kill cancer cells, non-apoptotic programs are recognized as drivers of inflammatory signaling that shape interactions among tumor cells, stromal components, and immune cells, thereby influencing immunotherapy outcomes. Ferroptosis is highlighted as a distinct modality governed by iron-dependent lipid peroxidation and perturbations in lipid, iron, and redox metabolism rather than a canonical induction signal. The review proposes a unified conceptual framework for understanding how these cell death programs collectively influence cancer progression, treatment response, immune suppression, and resistance mechanisms.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

Yuqing Liu, Kun Zhu, Weijun Peng et al.. Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines how the integration of multiomics technologies with artificial intelligence is reshaping precision drug discovery and clinical translation. The authors synthesized literature spanning genome-wide association studies, transcriptomic profiling, proteomic interaction mapping, and metabolomic sequencing alongside deep learning methodologies to assess their combined utility across the drug development pipeline. The review covers how this convergence supports target deconvolution, drug repositioning, de novo compound discovery, virtual screening, pharmacokinetic modeling, and safety assessment. Applications in oncology, neurodegenerative diseases, and cardiovascular conditions are used to illustrate how the combined approach enables more comprehensive molecular understanding and more precise therapeutic interventions. The authors also identify persistent challenges, including harmonization of disparate omics data, reproducibility concerns, and algorithmic bias, while arguing that overcoming these obstacles will define a new era in bench-to-clinic translational medicine.

The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Mahmoud M. Dahab et al.. The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development. Frontiers in Pharmacology. 2026

This narrative review examines the global threat posed by multidrug-resistant bacterial pathogens designated as priorities by the World Health Organization, with the aim of informing future antimicrobial research and drug development. The authors synthesize current understanding of the molecular mechanisms by which these pathogens evade existing antibiotics, encompassing reduced membrane permeability, efflux pumps, enzymatic inactivation, target modification, biofilm formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review critically assesses why conventional antibiotic discovery pipelines have failed to keep pace with resistance, and evaluates a range of emerging therapeutic strategies including artificial intelligence-assisted drug discovery, phage therapy, antimicrobial peptides, CRISPR-based systems, and natural product-derived compounds, while carefully considering their translational barriers, toxicity, dosing challenges, and regulatory hurdles. The authors also emphasize that antimicrobial stewardship, global surveillance, and a One Health framework integrating human, animal, and environmental health sectors are essential complements to new drug development. Their overarching conclusion is that combating WHO-priority resistant pathogens will require integrated, multidisciplinary approaches spanning molecular biology, pharmacology, clinical translation, and public health.

Beyond Extracellular Vesicle (EV) Hype: Practical Solutions and Remaining Hurdles in EV Research, Manufacturing, and Clinical Translation

David J. Lundy, Zoe L. Chau, Sheng‐You Chen et al.. Beyond Extracellular Vesicle (EV) Hype: Practical Solutions and Remaining Hurdles in EV Research, Manufacturing, and Clinical Translation. Advanced Science. 2026

This review critically examines the current state of extracellular vesicle research, focusing on the analytical and translational challenges that constrain the field's progress toward clinical application. The authors synthesize advances in EV isolation, detection, and single-particle analytics, which have revealed substantial molecular and functional heterogeneity among vesicle populations while simultaneously exposing fundamental limitations in how EV identity and biological activity are defined. Key obstacles identified include incomplete mechanistic understanding, absence of standardized measurements, inconsistent regulatory classification, and difficulties in establishing reproducible quality control frameworks for therapeutic EV preparations. The review addresses evolving regulatory frameworks and updates to established guidelines, advocating for operational definitions based on source material, isolation method, and molecular markers, alongside orthogonal characterization approaches and potency assays. The authors argue that overcoming these hurdles requires transforming EV research from largely descriptive work into a rigorous, quantitative measurement science capable of supporting reliable manufacturing and clinical translation.

WorldSculpt: Generating Compositional Worlds from Grounded Videos

Muyao Niu, Jixuan He, Ruihan Yu et al.. WorldSculpt: Generating Compositional Worlds from Grounded Videos. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of generating compositional three-dimensional representations of densely cluttered scenes containing hundreds of objects, where each object is represented as an individual mesh placed within a shared world coordinate frame. The authors propose adapting a strong single-object generative prior to handle multi-view observations, instantiated through a model called Pixal3D extended with a multi-view conditioning pathway that grounds object generation in multiple posed views. Crucially, the model is fine-tuned only on single objects in canonical space yet generalizes to complex, heavily occluded scenes without any scene-level training, demonstrating the scalability of this compositional generation paradigm. To support evaluation, the authors introduce a photorealistic benchmark of densely cluttered scenes with per-object annotations and ground-truth meshes. Their approach consistently outperforms prior methods across evaluations of varying scene complexity, with performance gains increasing as occlusion and clutter become more severe.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study confirmed two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets near the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert using space-based photometry and a large set of radial velocity measurements, while also detecting additional companions in both systems. Despite receiving extreme stellar irradiation at very short orbital periods, both planets retain substantial volatile content, and interior structure modeling combined with atmospheric escape calculations strongly favors water-dominated rather than hydrogen/helium envelopes, pointing to a steam-world interpretation. A population-level analysis revealed a striking overrepresentation of outer giant planet companions around sub-Neptunes located specifically within the tidal survival band near the desert's lower edge compared to those outside it, suggesting these short-period planets arrived via high-eccentricity tidal migration rather than in-situ formation or disk migration. The persistence of volatile-rich compositions in such extreme irradiation environments challenges models in which the desert boundary is determined solely by photoevaporation of primordial hydrogen/helium atmospheres. Together, the results argue that both the arrival mechanism—high-eccentricity migration driven by outer giant companions—and the composition of planetary envelopes play critical roles in shaping the lower edge of the Neptunian desert.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether machine learning can reconstruct the primordial density field of the Universe from late-time observations during the Epoch of Reionization, with the goal of recovering cosmological information otherwise lost to non-linear structure formation. The researchers used simulated 21-cm and CO line-intensity maps at high redshift, generated with the LIMFAST code, as inputs to a three-dimensional U-Net architecture trained to infer initial conditions at very high redshift. The two tracers were chosen deliberately for their complementarity, as 21-cm emission probes neutral, low-density intergalactic gas while CO traces dense, star-forming regions. In idealized noiseless conditions, combining both tracers yielded high-fidelity reconstruction across a broad range of spatial scales, and even under realistic instrumental noise conditions mimicking next-generation surveys, meaningful reconstruction was achieved on large scales. Crucially, performing simulation-based inference on power-spectrum summaries after reconstruction substantially tightened constraints on key cosmological parameters, demonstrating that joint multi-tracer analysis combined with this reconstruction approach can recover cosmological information that would otherwise remain inaccessible.

Beyond Scalar Flexibility: From Eligible AI Workloads to Dependable Load Relief

Meiyi Li. Beyond Scalar Flexibility: From Eligible AI Workloads to Dependable Load Relief. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how artificial intelligence data center workloads can provide flexible electricity demand reduction to support power grid operations, challenging the common practice of representing data center flexibility as a simple fixed percentage of load. The researchers reconstructed hourly power observations from a large GPU fleet trace spanning roughly six months, deriving a workload-aware flexibility envelope that accounts for which jobs are actually eligible for curtailment and how that eligibility persists over time. They found that immediately eligible curtailment represents only a modest fraction of total facility power, and that available relief declines meaningfully as event duration extends from one hour to twenty-four hours, with the distribution across reliability levels being impossible to capture with a single scalar value. Using a scalar flexibility assumption—whether calibrated to the mean or to a specific duration—introduces systematic errors that either overstate or understate available capacity depending on the timeframe considered, with the appropriate scalar varying by a factor of roughly one and a half across duration and reliability combinations. The study concludes that data center demand-response commitments must be specified in terms of duration, reliability level, portfolio aggregation, and a realizability fraction rather than a single percentage, and that delay-based flexibility from deferring new job arrivals contributes negligibly to available capacity.

WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data

Ji Soo Lee, Xilun Chen, Pierce Chuang et al.. WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a benchmark designed to evaluate whether AI systems can reason about longitudinal wearable health data from real users. The benchmark consists of thousands of multiple-choice questions built from wearable time series, blood biomarkers, and demographic information spanning hundreds of days per user, preserving authentic data characteristics such as device noise and individual variability. Questions are organized across two reasoning axes — data versus health reasoning, and single- versus cross-signal reasoning — and are constructed using a dual-grounding framework that draws on both published physiological findings and statistically validated patterns observed in the real-world data. Evaluation of a range of proprietary and open-source large language models revealed substantial variation in performance, with most models falling below the threshold needed to consider the benchmark solved, despite the best models substantially outperforming chance. The authors conclude that WearableQA serves as a realistic and diagnostically informative benchmark for assessing LLM reasoning over real-world wearable health data.

Diffusion TV: Experiencing Diffusion Models through Tangible, Embodied Interaction

Sihwa Park. Diffusion TV: Experiencing Diffusion Models through Tangible, Embodied Interaction. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents Diffusion TV, an interactive art installation designed to make diffusion model AI processes tangible and intuitively understandable through physical interaction. The installation uses a modified CRT television whose antenna serves as a hands-on control mechanism, with manipulation of the antenna metaphorically enacting the denoising process central to diffusion-based image and sound generation. A tuning knob allows participants to switch between three thematic channels featuring AI-generated animals representing extinct, endangered, and speculative future species, embedding the technical experience within an ecological and temporal narrative. Rather than explaining diffusion models through explicit instruction, the work uses continuous audiovisual feedback to foreground intermediate generative states as experiential material in their own right. The authors argue that this embodied mode of engagement constitutes an alternative form of explainable AI, fostering exploratory reflection on generative technologies.

Diagnostic Accuracy of Molecular Testing on Saliva and Oral Swabs for Pulmonary Tuberculosis.

Vargas DA, Fuertes-Bucheli JF, Sanchez-Hidalgo A et al.. Diagnostic Accuracy of Molecular Testing on Saliva and Oral Swabs for Pulmonary Tuberculosis.. Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America. 2026

This study evaluated whether molecular testing on saliva and oral swabs could serve as accurate, non-sputum alternatives for diagnosing pulmonary tuberculosis. Using a nested case-control design within a prospective cohort of adults and children at primary care centers in Colombia, the researchers collected paired saliva and nylon-flocked oral swab samples alongside sputum, then applied Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra testing to the non-sputum specimens and compared results against sputum liquid mycobacterial culture as the reference standard. Saliva demonstrated high sensitivity and specificity for culture-confirmed tuberculosis, surpassing the World Health Organization's target threshold for non-sputum diagnostic tests, while oral swabs showed somewhat lower sensitivity but very high specificity. Saliva sensitivity significantly exceeded that of oral swabs, though the two specimen types did not differ meaningfully in specificity. Both collection methods were well accepted by participants, supporting the potential of saliva in particular as a feasible, patient-friendly alternative to sputum for tuberculosis diagnosis.

Animal Welfare-Friendly Noninvasive Oral Fluid in Swine Diagnostics: Current Applications in Disease Monitoring, Key Challenges and Future Directions.

Chouhan CS, Coiacetto F, Abraham S et al.. Animal Welfare-Friendly Noninvasive Oral Fluid in Swine Diagnostics: Current Applications in Disease Monitoring, Key Challenges and Future Directions.. Veterinary medicine international. 2026

This review examines the use of oral fluid sampling as a noninvasive diagnostic tool for monitoring herd health in swine, exploring how this approach can replace or supplement traditional blood-based methods. Oral fluid, composed of saliva, mucosal transudate, and plasma-derived components, contains immunoglobulins, acute-phase proteins, and nucleic acids that reflect both local and systemic physiological states, enabling detection of infectious pathogens and immune biomarkers. Collection via cotton ropes or swabs allows sampling at the individual or pen level with minimal animal handling, making it more welfare-friendly than conventional methods, and PCR-based pathogen detection along with antibody and biomarker measurement have been demonstrated across multiple studies. However, diagnostic performance is hampered by lower analyte concentrations compared to serum, poor pig participation, dominance-related rope chewing, contamination, analyte instability during transport, and a lack of validated assays specific to this matrix. The review calls for standardised collection and handling protocols, matrix-specific diagnostic thresholds, regulatory validation, and proteomic biomarker discovery to advance oral fluid as a reliable tool for continuous herd health surveillance in global swine production.

Ultrabright PCNs@Coumarin 6-Enhanced Wireless Lateral Flow Immunoassay Platform for Ultrasensitive Detection of Porphyromonas gingivalis.

Zhou M, Shen H, Long Z et al.. Ultrabright PCNs@Coumarin 6-Enhanced Wireless Lateral Flow Immunoassay Platform for Ultrasensitive Detection of Porphyromonas gingivalis.. ACS applied materials & interfaces. 2026

This study developed an ultrasensitive wireless lateral flow immunoassay platform for detecting *Porphyromonas gingivalis*, a key bacterial pathogen linked to periodontal and systemic diseases, addressing limitations of traditional detection methods such as low sensitivity and procedural complexity. The researchers synthesized a novel fluorescent metal-organic framework nanocomposite that exhibited markedly enhanced fluorescence intensity and prolonged lifetime compared to commercial counterparts, and integrated this with a quadruped DNA walker system that further amplified fluorescence signals through enzyme-dependent activity. A self-developed wireless analyzer was incorporated to enable rapid and accurate interpretation of lateral flow test strips. The platform achieved an exceptionally low detection limit and a wide linear detection range, and was validated against clinical saliva samples, demonstrating high accuracy and strong resistance to interference. Compared to polymerase chain reaction, the platform was found to be less costly and more operationally convenient, positioning it as a practical tool for point-of-care testing in clinical, home, and community settings.

Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration

Thirumalaisamy Suryaprabha, Chunghyeon Choi, Yanfang Wu et al.. Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration. npj Flexible Electronics. 2026

This review examines the current state of smart wearable and implantable biosensors designed for continuous, real-time monitoring of biophysical and biochemical signals in the context of personalized and preventive healthcare. The paper surveys advances in flexible, stretchable, and biocompatible materials that enable long-term body integration, as well as multimodal and multi-analyte sensing approaches that improve measurement robustness. The role of artificial intelligence in enhancing signal processing and generating predictive health insights is also assessed. The review identifies persistent challenges including motion artifacts, energy autonomy, data privacy, and clinical interpretability that must be addressed before widespread deployment. Overall, the paper synthesizes progress across materials science, device architecture, and AI-assisted strategies to map the trajectory of this rapidly evolving field.

Analytical Performances of Polymer-Based Biosensors for Real Samples Application

Marcello Mascini, Sara Palmieri, Fabiola Eugelio et al.. Analytical Performances of Polymer-Based Biosensors for Real Samples Application. Biosensors. 2026

This review examined how polymer-based biosensors perform analytically when applied to real-world complex sample matrices, surveying a decade of original research across food, beverage, environmental, and clinical domains. The authors conducted a meta-trend analysis of studies covering four polymer classes—conductive polymers, redox-mediator polymers, hydrogels, and molecularly imprinted polymers—evaluating their effectiveness in managing matrix interference and maintaining detection reliability. Electrochemical detection was found to predominate across the literature, with each polymer class offering distinct advantages: conductive polymers reduce interference from electroactive species, hydrogels preserve enzyme function in protein-rich environments, and molecularly imprinted polymers enable stable trace detection in harsh conditions. However, the review identified serious validation gaps, noting that the vast majority of studies omit limits of quantification and that substantial fractions lack reproducibility and precision reporting, undermining the field's credibility. The authors conclude that maintaining consistent calibration across diverse hostile environments remains the central obstacle to commercial deployment, while emerging architectures such as nanocapsulation, hierarchical nanocomposites, and microneedle-integrated systems represent promising routes toward improved fouling resistance and operational stability.

A review of nanofiber-based drug delivery systems: fabrication, characterization, advances, and future prospects

A. M. Saleh, Radwa N. Selim, Manar T El-Morsy et al.. A review of nanofiber-based drug delivery systems: fabrication, characterization, advances, and future prospects. Journal of Materials Science Polymers. 2026

This review examines nanofiber-based drug delivery systems, focusing on their structural properties that make them advantageous for controlled and sustained therapeutic release. The authors systematically analyze multiple fabrication techniques, including electrospinning, self-assembly, phase separation, printing methods, and template-assisted approaches, evaluating the strengths and limitations of each. The role of synthetic, natural, and composite polymers in determining nanofiber performance is explored alongside key challenges such as scalability, biocompatibility, and in vivo applicability. Recent advances are highlighted, particularly the integration of artificial intelligence and three-dimensional bioprinting to enable personalized treatment strategies, as well as the development of hybrid and biomimetic nanofiber systems. The review concludes by identifying future directions centered on bioresponsive and smart nanofiber platforms, implantable and in situ-forming systems, and scalable sustainable manufacturing pathways to bridge the gap between laboratory research and clinical translation.

Harmonizing definitions in liquid biopsy: A terminology framework by the international society of liquid biopsy

Eloísa Jantus-Lewintre, Silvia Calabuig-Fariñas, David Gandara et al.. Harmonizing definitions in liquid biopsy: A terminology framework by the international society of liquid biopsy. The Journal of Liquid Biopsy. 2026

The paper addresses the problem of inconsistent terminology that has emerged alongside the rapid growth of liquid biopsy research in oncology, which has created barriers to communication, result comparability, and clinical translation. The International Society of Liquid Biopsy developed a comprehensive, harmonized terminology framework to standardize language across the field. The framework defines liquid biopsy broadly as the minimally invasive analysis of cells, nucleic acids, proteins, metabolites, and extracellular vesicles from bodily fluids to interrogate pathological or physiological conditions. It rigorously clarifies analyte-specific terms—including circulating free and tumor DNA and RNA, tumor fraction, variant allele frequency, circulating tumor cells, extracellular vesicles, and metabolomic signatures—as well as the analytical methodologies and clinical applications spanning early detection, molecular residual disease assessment, predictive biomarker evaluation, and tumor monitoring. The authors present this vocabulary as a foundational reference intended to evolve with scientific progress and guide the integration of liquid biopsy into precision medicine.

Biomaterials in personalized drug delivery: innovations, challenges, and future directions

Adna Hrapović, Nadia Islam, Asmaa Al Bourghli et al.. Biomaterials in personalized drug delivery: innovations, challenges, and future directions. PeerJ. 2026

This review examines the role of biomaterials in personalized drug delivery, covering the evolution of material classes—including polymers, metals, ceramics, and composites—from inert biological interfaces to rationally engineered therapeutic systems. The authors survey how advances in regenerative medicine, nanotechnology, and precision healthcare have expanded the design space for drug delivery, with tunable properties such as degradation kinetics, surface chemistry, and mechanical stability enabling controlled release and site-specific targeting. Polymer-based systems achieve sustained release through diffusion, degradation, or stimulus-responsive mechanisms, while nanostructured carriers such as liposomes, micelles, and dendrimers improve solubility, cellular uptake, and targeting through surface functionalization and ligand-mediated interactions. Despite these innovations, the authors identify persistent barriers to clinical translation, including immune–biomaterial interactions, manufacturing variability, long-term biodegradation uncertainty, and regulatory scalability constraints. The review concludes that future progress requires precision fabrication, good manufacturing practice–compatible production processes, and biologically informed design strategies that accommodate patient-specific variability.
