WorldSculpt: Generating Compositional Worlds from Grounded Videos

Muyao Niu, Jixuan He, Ruihan Yu et al.. WorldSculpt: Generating Compositional Worlds from Grounded Videos. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the problem of generating compositional three-dimensional representations of densely cluttered scenes containing hundreds of individual objects, where each object is represented as a separate mesh placed within a shared world frame. The authors adapt a strong single-object generative prior to handle multi-view observations by extending an existing model with a multi-view conditioning pathway, allowing object generation to be grounded in multiple posed views without requiring any scene-level training. Despite being fine-tuned only on isolated objects in canonical space, the approach generalizes to large, heavily occluded scenes, demonstrating that compositional scene generation can scale beyond the simple scenes handled by prior methods. To support evaluation, the authors introduce a new photorealistic benchmark of densely cluttered scenes with per-object annotations and ground-truth meshes. The method consistently outperforms prior geometry-based and compositional approaches, with performance gains becoming larger as scene complexity and occlusion increase, and is further shown to convert existing three-dimensional Gaussian splatting worlds into compositional mesh representations.

UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons

Linzhan Mou, Jiahui Lei, Zhiyang Dou et al.. UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of generating motion for arbitrary rigged 3D character skeletons from text prompts, a task that existing methods struggle with due to their reliance on fixed topologies or per-skeleton fine-tuning. The authors introduce UniMate, a unified diffusion-based foundation model built around a topology-aware transformer that encodes skeletal structure through graph-aware attention biases, a spectral extension of rotary position embeddings adapted to arbitrary kinematic trees via the graph Laplacian, and a global topological conditioning mechanism derived from the rest-pose skeleton. To train the model, they curated a large-scale dataset spanning a wide variety of creature and object categories with unified canonicalization and text annotations. UniMate requires no test-time optimization or retraining for new skeletons and outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods in motion quality, generalization, and efficiency. The model additionally supports zero-shot cross-topology transfer, motion in-betweening, skeleton expansion, and text-guided editing.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigated two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets near the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert—a region largely devoid of intermediate-sized planets at short orbital periods—using space-based photometry and ground-based radial velocity measurements. The researchers confirmed both planets' properties through detailed modeling of stellar activity and interior structure, finding that despite receiving extreme stellar irradiation, the planets retain significant volatile content inconsistent with primordial hydrogen/helium envelopes. Atmospheric escape modeling shows that hydrogen/helium envelopes would be stripped on timescales orders of magnitude shorter than water-dominated ones, strongly favoring a steam-world interpretation for all three transiting planets in these systems. The work also detected outer giant companions in both systems and conducted a population-level analysis revealing a strong excess of giant companions to low-mass sub-Neptunes specifically within the orbital zone where high-eccentricity tidal migration would be expected to operate. Together, these findings challenge the view that photoevaporation alone defines the lower desert edge, instead implicating high-eccentricity tidal migration as a key dynamical pathway delivering planets to this region.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the primordial density field of the Universe can be reconstructed from Epoch of Reionization observations, thereby recovering cosmological information lost to non-linear gravitational evolution and astrophysical processes. The authors generated simulated 21-cm and CO line-intensity maps at redshift eight using the LIMFAST code and trained a three-dimensional U-Net to infer the corresponding initial density field at redshift around 300, incorporating instrumental effects representative of SKA1-Low and COMAP-ERA surveys. The two tracers were found to complement each other well because 21-cm emission probes neutral, underdense intergalactic regions while CO emission probes dense, star-forming environments. In noiseless conditions, combining both tracers yielded high cross-correlation with the true initial field across a broad range of scales, and even with realistic observational noise the joint reconstruction maintained meaningful fidelity at intermediate scales. Applying simulation-based inference to power-spectrum summaries, the authors found that reconstruction tightened constraints on key cosmological parameters, approximately doubling the precision on the amplitude of matter fluctuations and the spectral index, demonstrating that joint multi-tracer surveys at the Epoch of Reionization can partially recover otherwise inaccessible primordial information.

Beyond Scalar Flexibility: From Eligible AI Workloads to Dependable Load Relief

Meiyi Li. Beyond Scalar Flexibility: From Eligible AI Workloads to Dependable Load Relief. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated how to accurately characterize the demand flexibility of large-scale AI data centers for use in grid reliability applications, challenging the common practice of representing data-center flexibility as a simple fixed percentage of load. Using a multi-month production trace from a large GPU fleet, the researchers reconstructed hourly power observations and derived a workload-semantic flexibility envelope that accounts for which computational jobs are actually eligible for curtailment and how that eligibility evolves across event durations. They found that immediately eligible curtailment is a modest fraction of total facility power, and that the amount of reliable load relief declines meaningfully as event duration extends from one hour to twenty-four hours. A scalar flexibility assumption calibrated to the mean overstates available relief substantially at longer durations, while tail-calibrated scalars introduce errors in the opposite direction at shorter durations, with no single scalar reproducing the full duration-reliability surface. The paper concludes that accurate demand-response and interconnection contracts for AI data centers require explicit terms for duration, reliability level, portfolio aggregation, and the fraction of eligible load that can actually be realized, rather than a single assumed flexibility percentage.

WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data

Ji Soo Lee, Xilun Chen, Pierce Chuang et al.. WearableQA: A Benchmark for Health Reasoning over Real-World Wearable Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a benchmark designed to evaluate whether AI systems can reason over real longitudinal wearable health data from actual users. The benchmark comprises thousands of multiple-choice questions drawn from wearable time series, blood biomarkers, and demographic information spanning hundreds of days per user, preserving authentic data characteristics such as device noise and individual variability. Questions are organized across two analytical axes that distinguish between computational and physiological reasoning and between single-signal and multi-signal integration, with question construction grounded in both scientific literature and statistically validated population-level patterns. Evaluating a broad set of proprietary and open-source large language models revealed wide performance variation, with most models falling well below strong accuracy thresholds despite substantially exceeding chance performance. The authors conclude that the benchmark effectively differentiates model capabilities and remains far from solved, positioning it as a realistic diagnostic tool for assessing AI reasoning over real-world wearable health data.

The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Mahmoud M. Dahab et al.. The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development. Frontiers in Pharmacology. 2026

This narrative review examines the global threat posed by multidrug-resistant bacterial pathogens prioritized by the World Health Organization, with the aim of synthesizing knowledge to guide future antimicrobial development. The authors survey the molecular mechanisms by which these pathogens evade existing antibiotics, including reduced membrane permeability, efflux pump overexpression, enzymatic inactivation, target modification, biofilm formation, and horizontal gene transfer. They critically assess why conventional antibiotic discovery pipelines have failed and evaluate a range of emerging therapeutic strategies—such as artificial intelligence-assisted drug discovery, phage therapy, antimicrobial peptides, CRISPR-based systems, and natural product-derived compounds—while addressing practical challenges of delivery, toxicity, dosing, and regulatory barriers. The review further highlights the importance of antimicrobial stewardship, surveillance systems, and a One Health framework that integrates human, animal, and environmental health sectors to slow resistance dissemination. The authors conclude that overcoming the challenge of WHO-priority pathogens will require integrated, multidisciplinary approaches spanning molecular biology, pharmacology, clinical translation, and public health policy.

Advances in Natural Product Extraction: Established and Emerging Technologies

Carsyn R. Travis, Jared McMaster, Fatima Rivas. Advances in Natural Product Extraction: Established and Emerging Technologies. Molecules. 2026

This review examines recent advances in the extraction and isolation of natural products, which form the foundation of drug discovery efforts drawing on microorganisms, plants, and marine organisms as sources of structurally novel bioactive compounds. The authors survey innovations in extraction methodology developed over approximately the past five years, including green and designer solvent systems, pressurized and ultrasound-assisted extraction platforms, and supercritical fluid techniques, all aimed at improving compound recovery, purity, and analytical throughput. A particular focus is placed on the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning tools for solvent selection, extraction optimization, and metabolite dereplication, which have expanded the range of detectable and characterizable metabolites in complex biological samples. The review also addresses combinatorial extraction strategies and next-generation purification workflows that reduce processing time while enhancing selectivity. Overall, the authors argue that these technological developments have collectively restored natural products to prominence in modern drug discovery by improving the efficiency and reliability of the isolation process that underpins all downstream biological evaluation.

Harnessing biogenic nanoparticles for combating antibiotic resistance: green synthesis, mechanistic insights, and biotechnological applications

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Ibrahim Mohammed Hussaini et al.. Harnessing biogenic nanoparticles for combating antibiotic resistance: green synthesis, mechanistic insights, and biotechnological applications. Frontiers in Bioengineering and Biotechnology. 2026

This review examines biogenic nanoparticles as a potential strategy to address the global crisis of antimicrobial resistance, focusing on materials synthesized using biological sources such as plants, microorganisms, algae, and isolated biomolecules. The authors surveyed recent literature on how biological metabolites govern the reduction of metal ions and the nucleation, growth, and stabilization of nanoparticles, thereby determining their physicochemical properties and antimicrobial behavior. Multiple metal-based nanoparticles were considered, and the review describes how they can act through several simultaneous mechanisms including membrane disruption, reactive oxygen species generation, interference with metabolism, DNA and protein damage, quorum sensing inhibition, and biofilm suppression against WHO-priority and ESKAPE pathogens. The review also covers experimental applications of these materials in combination with antibiotics or phytochemicals, as well as their use in wound-related systems, antimicrobial coatings, drug delivery, biosensing, and food packaging. The authors conclude that while biogenic nanoparticles represent a promising experimental platform, significant obstacles remain, including inconsistent synthesis protocols, limited scalability, and insufficient long-term toxicological and environmental safety data, all of which must be resolved before clinical translation can be pursued.

Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances

Pranay Amruth Maroju, Angad S. Sidhu, Amogh R. Motaganahalli et al.. Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances. Antibiotics. 2026

This review paper examines the biology, clinical significance, and therapeutic management of bacterial biofilms, which are structured microbial communities encased in a self-produced matrix of polysaccharides, proteins, extracellular DNA, and lipids. The authors synthesize current knowledge on how biofilms develop, how their matrix composition creates heterogeneous microenvironments that block antimicrobial penetration and evade host immunity, and how mechanisms such as persister cell formation, efflux pump upregulation, enzymatic antibiotic inactivation, and horizontal gene transfer collectively drive tolerance and resistance. The review maps the clinical contexts in which biofilms pose particular challenges, including cystic fibrosis, chronic wounds, osteomyelitis, device-associated infections, and breast implant illness, where persistent biofilm-associated inflammation has been hypothesized to contribute to systemic manifestations. Conventional and emerging detection and real-time monitoring strategies are also discussed alongside therapeutic approaches that pair antibiotics with matrix-disrupting enzymes, quorum-sensing inhibitors, bacteriophages, metabolic reactivators, and nanotechnology-based delivery systems. The authors conclude that eradicating biofilm infections requires integrated strategies combining accurate detection with multifaceted treatments, and that advances in multi-omics and systems-level modeling will be critical for developing next-generation prevention and therapy.

This review examines carbon quantum dots as a class of fluorescent nanomaterials with broad potential in biomedical research, focusing on how their structural and surface properties govern biological behavior. The authors surveyed advances in synthesis approaches, including top-down, bottom-up, and green routes, and assessed how these methods influence emission profiles, heteroatom doping, and surface functionalization. A central focus is the mechanistic relationship between defined structural attributes and outcomes such as antimicrobial activity, organelle-specific targeting, intracellular transport, and integrated imaging-therapy platforms. UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons

Linzhan Mou, Jiahui Lei, Zhiyang Dou et al.. UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons. arXiv. 2026 preprint

UniMate is a unified foundation model designed to generate motion for arbitrary rigged 3D skeletons from a text prompt alone, addressing the limitation of existing methods that are constrained to specific skeleton topologies or require per-skeleton fine-tuning. The model introduces a topology-aware diffusion transformer that encodes skeletal structure into the attention mechanism through graph-aware attention biases, a spectral rotary position embedding derived from the graph Laplacian, and a global topological conditioning signal from the rest-pose skeleton. To support training across diverse creature and object categories—including bipedal, quadrupedal, avian, marine, insectoid, serpentine, and articulated rigid forms—the authors curated a large motion dataset with unified canonicalization and text annotations. UniMate requires no test-time optimization or reference motions at inference, and is demonstrated to outperform existing baselines in motion quality, generalization, and efficiency while enabling zero-shot cross-topology transfer, motion in-betweening, skeleton expansion, and text-guided editing.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigates two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets situated near the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert, a region notably depleted of planets at short orbital periods, using space-based photometry and ground-based radial velocity measurements. The researchers confirmed the planetary nature and characterized the physical properties of both planets, modelled stellar activity using sophisticated Gaussian process techniques, and detected additional companions in each system, including an outer giant planet and a second transiting sub-Neptune. Despite receiving irradiation far exceeding Earth's, the measured densities of all three transiting planets are too low to be explained by rocky or hydrogen/helium-dominated compositions, and atmospheric escape modelling strongly favors water-dominated "steam world" interiors capable of surviving on timescales far longer than hydrogen/helium envelopes. A population-level analysis revealed a statistically significant excess of outer giant companions around low-mass sub-Neptunes specifically within the tidal survival band near the desert's lower edge, pointing to high-eccentricity tidal migration as an important delivery mechanism for planets into this region. Together, these findings suggest that the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert is shaped not by atmospheric photoevaporation alone but also by dynamical migration processes that deposit volatile-rich planets on short-period orbits where some fraction can survive.

Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations

Anchal Saxena, P. Daniel Meerburg, Guochao Sun et al.. Tracing the Cosmic Origins: Machine Learning Reconstruction of the Primordial Density Field from EoR Observations. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether machine learning can reconstruct the primordial density field of the Universe from observations of the Epoch of Reionization, thereby recovering cosmological information obscured by non-linear structure formation. The researchers used simulated 21-cm and CO line-intensity maps generated with the LIMFAST simulation framework, applying a three-dimensional U-Net neural network to reconstruct initial conditions at very high redshift from these late-time tracers. The two tracers were chosen because they probe complementary cosmic environments, with 21-cm emission sensitive to underdense neutral regions and CO tracing dense star-forming structures. Combining both tracers yielded the most accurate reconstructions, maintaining high cross-correlation with the true initial density field across a range of scales even when realistic instrumental noise and finite angular resolution mimicking next-generation surveys were included. Crucially, using the reconstructed fields for simulation-based inference of cosmological parameters substantially tightened parameter constraints, approximately doubling the precision on the amplitude of matter fluctuations and the spectral index, demonstrating that joint multi-tracer reconstruction can recover otherwise inaccessible cosmological information.

Early against Late: A contrast on dark energy in the light of DESI DR2

Miguel A. Zapata, Karim Carrion, Gabriela Garcia-Arroyo. Early against Late: A contrast on dark energy in the light of DESI DR2. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether recent observational data favor extensions to the standard cosmological constant model of dark energy, explicitly distinguishing between models that modify early-time versus late-time cosmic evolution. Using Bayesian model comparison with a validated harmonic-mean estimator, the authors tested Early Dark Energy acting before recombination against the Chevallier–Polarski–Linder parameterization describing late-time dynamics, both benchmarked against standard cosmology, using combinations of DESI baryon acoustic oscillation measurements, Type Ia supernovae, and cosmic microwave background data. They found that while parameter-level posteriors for both extensions show apparent multi-sigma deviations from the standard model, proper Bayesian evidence accounting for prior-volume penalties tells a sharply different story: Early Dark Energy is decisively rejected when CMB data are included, whereas the late-time parameterization remains only moderately disfavored. The results reveal a stark dichotomy between early- and late-time dark energy dynamics in terms of statistical support, demonstrating that the apparent observational preferences reported in parameter space are substantially weakened or eliminated once model complexity is properly penalized. The authors conclude that the cosmic epoch of dark energy activity critically determines how strongly models are disfavored relative to the cosmological constant, a finding further supported by functional reconstructions of the dark energy equation of state and density evolution.

Decompactification and the Flat-Space Limit of AdS$_5\times$ S$^5$ Master Correlators

Burkhard Eden, Paul Heslop, Harshal Kulkarni et al.. Decompactification and the Flat-Space Limit of AdS$_5\times$ S$^5$ Master Correlators. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the flat-space limit of AdS/CFT holography in the specific setting of AdS₅ × S⁵, carefully accounting for the decompactification of the internal sphere that occurs simultaneously with the flat-space limit. The authors introduce master propagators and correlators that resum the full tower of Kaluza–Klein modes, providing a unified framework for taking this combined limit. They find that boundary correlators in this limit correspond to scattering amplitudes with kinematics restricted to five dimensions, while bulk correlators recover full ten-dimensional flat-space amplitudes with unrestricted kinematics. By analytically continuing the sphere to a de Sitter geometry, they further show that the flat-space and boundary limits commute, yielding expressions interpretable as amplitudes in a flat space with two time directions subject to simultaneous null constraints. The results are discussed in the context of flat-space holography and its relationship to the Carrollian limit of the dual conformal field theory.

Towards Scaling Quantum Fine-Tuning of Foundational Time Series Models for Classification

Sang Hyub Kim, Julien Baglio, Rajiv Krishnakumar et al.. Towards Scaling Quantum Fine-Tuning of Foundational Time Series Models for Classification. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated whether quantum machine learning models can effectively leverage embeddings from classical time-series foundation models, and how hybrid classical-quantum architectures might be scaled for classification tasks. The researchers fine-tuned a foundational time-series model called Chronos for power-grid event classification by attaching a quantum circuit as a classification head to the model's embeddings. They found that grouping embeddings by physical sensor type before feeding them to the quantum head surpassed published baselines, and the quantum head outperformed a larger classical neural network on the same inputs, though performance gains eventually saturated due to a data intake bandwidth bottleneck rather than insufficient circuit expressiveness or information supply. To address this bottleneck, the authors introduced a "wing module," a small few-qubit circuit that feeds additional information into the core circuit through sparse one-way coupling, allowing the system to be scaled by adding wings incrementally. Their experiments demonstrated that balanced accuracy improved with each added wing, and ablation studies confirmed that adding qubits only helps when those qubits carry new input information, reframing quantum scaling as a question of input bandwidth rather than parameter count.

Innovative epitope-based ELISA assay for detecting Group B Streptococcus carriage in pregnant women.

Brzychczy-Włoch M, Dobrut A, Drożdż K et al.. Innovative epitope-based ELISA assay for detecting Group B Streptococcus carriage in pregnant women.. Applied microbiology and biotechnology. 2026

This study investigated three synthetic peptide epitopes derived from *Streptococcus agalactiae* proteins—GroEL, enolase, and EF-Tu—as components of an ELISA-based immunodiagnostic test for detecting Group B Streptococcus infection and carriage in pregnant women. The researchers developed and evaluated this epitope-based assay using IgG, IgM, and IgA antibody detection across serum samples from GBS-positive and GBS-negative individuals, with the goal of providing an alternative to the current gold standard of bacterial culture, which is time-consuming and can yield ambiguous results. All three peptides, whether used individually or in combination, significantly distinguished between GBS-positive and GBS-negative sera, with the enolase- and EF-Tu-derived epitopes performing best as individual candidates due to their higher immunoreactivity at lower concentrations. Combining epitopes in various mixtures further improved immunoreactivity beyond what was achieved with single peptides alone. The authors propose this prototype as a promising and potentially pioneering alternative diagnostic approach, as no comparable epitope-based test currently exists on the market.

Microbial Detection in Community-Acquired Pleural Space Infections Using the BIOFIRE Joint Infection Panel and Individual Polymerase Chain Reaction Assays.

Mira P, Dyrhovden R, Tellevik MG et al.. Microbial Detection in Community-Acquired Pleural Space Infections Using the BIOFIRE Joint Infection Panel and Individual Polymerase Chain Reaction Assays.. Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America. 2026

This study investigated molecular diagnostic approaches for detecting pathogens in community-acquired pleural space infections, where rapid and accurate microbial identification can guide treatment. Researchers compared the performance of the cassette-based BIOFIRE Joint Infection Panel, used off-label, with and without supplementary in-house polymerase chain reaction assays, against 16S rRNA gene PCR with sequencing and conventional culture using pleural fluid samples from two clinical centers. The BIOFIRE panel supplemented with targeted in-house assays for specific organisms achieved a high pathogen detection rate and provided complete microbial profiles in a substantial proportion of cases, outperforming culture substantially but falling somewhat short of 16S rRNA gene PCR sequencing for complete profiles. Culture performed considerably worse than either molecular approach in both detecting bacteria and yielding complete microbial characterization. The findings support the adoption of panel-based PCR diagnostics for pleural infections and suggest that the BIOFIRE Joint Infection Panel, originally designed for synovial fluid, could be adapted into a practical diagnostic tool for pleural fluid testing.

Nasopharyngeal microbiota profiles and lytA-PCR status in adult pneumococcal pneumonia: distinct microbial communities and diagnostic implications.

Dietl B, Henares D, Blanco-Fuertes M et al.. Nasopharyngeal microbiota profiles and lytA-PCR status in adult pneumococcal pneumonia: distinct microbial communities and diagnostic implications.. Pneumonia (Nathan Qld.). 2026

This study investigated whether nasopharyngeal microbiota profiles differ between adults with confirmed pneumococcal pneumonia who test positive versus negative for the pneumococcal marker lytA by quantitative PCR on nasopharyngeal samples. In a prospective observational study of hospitalized adults, nasopharyngeal samples were collected before antibiotic treatment and analyzed using both lytA-PCR quantification and bacterial community profiling via 16S rRNA gene sequencing. While overall microbial diversity did not differ between lytA-positive and lytA-negative patients, compositional analysis revealed distinct community structures, with Streptococcus species enriched in lytA-positive patients and Corynebacterium and Dolosigranulum more abundant in lytA-negative patients. Specific Streptococcus variants consistent with S. pneumoniae correlated with pneumococcal load in lytA-positive samples. The authors conclude that these two microbiota patterns likely reflect distinct pathogenetic pathways—colonization-driven versus invasion-driven disease—and suggest that integrating microbiota profiling with molecular diagnostics could improve understanding of pneumococcal pneumonia pathogenesis in adults.

Vertical transmission of Group B Streptococcus in a public referral hospital

Pires TS, Amaral WNd, Cardoso JL et al.. Vertical transmission of Group B Streptococcus in a public referral hospital. 2026

This study investigated the vertical transmission of Group B Streptococcus from colonized mothers to their newborns at a public referral hospital in Brazil. Using a cross-sectional design, researchers collected vaginal, anorectal, nasal, and auricular swabs from parturients and their newborns, analyzing samples through microbiological culture, real-time PCR, and antimicrobial susceptibility testing. Maternal colonization rates were high, and vertical transmission occurred in the majority of colonized cases, resulting in substantial neonatal prevalence. The isolates displayed considerable resistance to tetracycline, erythromycin, and clindamycin, while beta-lactam antibiotics retained high efficacy, supporting their continued use as first-line intrapartum prophylaxis. The findings underscore the need for routine prenatal screening and highlight qPCR as a more sensitive diagnostic tool compared to culture, particularly when prenatal care is limited.

Bridging the diagnostic gap: the role of culture methods in the era of syndromic panels for central nervous system infections.

Sberna G, D'Arezzo S, Valli MB et al.. Bridging the diagnostic gap: the role of culture methods in the era of syndromic panels for central nervous system infections.. Frontiers in cellular and infection microbiology. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of a syndromic molecular panel for central nervous system infections by comparing it against conventional culture methods using cerebrospinal fluid samples collected over three years at a national reference center. The researchers assessed concordance, sensitivity, specificity, and predictive values across a large dataset of paired molecular and culture results. They found moderate overall agreement between the two approaches, with the syndromic panel demonstrating high specificity but limited sensitivity when all culture-identified pathogens were considered; however, when the analysis was restricted to bacterial targets actually included in the panel, both agreement and sensitivity improved substantially. Critically, conventional culture detected clinically relevant pathogens absent from the syndromic panel's target list, including organisms with direct implications for antimicrobial management. The authors conclude that syndromic panels cannot serve as standalone diagnostics and must be systematically combined with conventional culture to ensure comprehensive pathogen detection and guide appropriate treatment.

Epidemiological and Laboratory Evaluation of Central Nervous System Infections Using the BioFire FilmArray Meningitis/Encephalitis Panel in a Tertiary Care Hospital in İstanbul.

Çuğlan SS, İrvem A, Aslan M et al.. Epidemiological and Laboratory Evaluation of Central Nervous System Infections Using the BioFire FilmArray Meningitis/Encephalitis Panel in a Tertiary Care Hospital in İstanbul.. Infectious diseases & clinical microbiology. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of the BioFire FilmArray Meningitis/Encephalitis multiplex PCR panel compared with conventional cerebrospinal fluid analysis methods for identifying central nervous system pathogens in a tertiary care hospital setting. Researchers retrospectively analyzed a large series of cerebrospinal fluid samples over three years using routine culture, Gram staining, cytological examination, biochemical testing, and multiplex PCR in parallel. The panel detected pathogens in a notable proportion of samples, with viral pathogens outnumbering bacterial ones, and conventional culture identified only two positive cases, both concordant with PCR results. Biochemical markers, particularly lactate dehydrogenase and the proportion of polymorphonuclear leukocytes, showed strong discriminatory performance for distinguishing bacterial from viral infections, and age-related differences in pathogen distribution were also observed. The authors conclude that combining the multiplex PCR panel with routine cerebrospinal fluid biochemical and cytological parameters offers a rapid and reliable strategy for differentiating bacterial from viral central nervous system infections.

Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.

Walzl G, McHugh TD, Chakaya J et al.. Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.. The Lancet. Microbe. 2027

This review synthesizes major advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarker research from 2020 to 2025, covering how these developments have shaped updated WHO policy guidance consolidating recommendations for disease detection and drug-resistance testing into a single framework. The authors examined progress across screening, diagnostic, and drug-resistance testing modalities, including digital chest radiography with computer-aided detection, decentralised molecular platforms such as low-complexity automated and manual nucleic acid amplification tests, and novel specimen types including tongue swabs and pooled sputum. Expanded molecular testing across non-sputum specimens has improved bacteriological confirmation in children and sputum-scarce populations, while targeted next-generation sequencing is now endorsed at reference laboratory level for comprehensive drug-susceptibility testing, and urine lipoarabinomannan assays improve yield in people with HIV and advanced disease. Host blood transcriptomic signatures show promising triage and prognostic performance, and proteomic, metabolomic, and other biomarker classes continue through clinical evaluation, yet no biomarker has met WHO target product profile criteria for a point-of-care test that reliably distinguishes infection from disease, including subclinical tuberculosis. The authors identify substantial remaining gaps in performance, implementation, and equity that continue to limit the full impact of these diagnostic advances.

Development of a microfluidic isothermal amplification nucleic acid detection (mFIND) platform for simultaneous detection of respiratory pathogens.

He P, Feng Y, Zhou X et al.. Development of a microfluidic isothermal amplification nucleic acid detection (mFIND) platform for simultaneous detection of respiratory pathogens.. Biosensors & bioelectronics. 2026

The paper describes the development of a centrifugal microfluidic platform designed for simultaneous isothermal nucleic acid amplification and detection of multiple respiratory pathogens, including influenza A and B, SARS-CoV-2, and RSV. The system integrates lyophilized reagents into independent on-chip reaction chambers and pairs with a portable analyzer that automates fluid handling, temperature control, and real-time fluorescence readout, completing the entire workflow within minutes using very small reaction volumes. Analytical testing demonstrated extremely low detection limits and no cross-reactivity against a broad panel of non-target pathogens, while clinical validation against a large set of confirmed positive samples showed complete agreement with quantitative PCR. The platform is capable of processing multiple samples in parallel across four targets simultaneously, making it well suited for decentralized or point-of-care diagnostic settings. The authors position this integrated approach as a practical solution for rapid respiratory pathogen detection without requiring laboratory infrastructure.

Development and evaluation of a rapid duplex qPCR assay for Treponema pallidum using the GeneSoC microfluidic platform.

Kawahata T, Banno F, Hama M. Development and evaluation of a rapid duplex qPCR assay for Treponema pallidum using the GeneSoC microfluidic platform.. Diagnostic microbiology and infectious disease. 2026

This study developed and evaluated a rapid duplex quantitative PCR assay for detecting Treponema pallidum, the causative agent of syphilis, using the GeneSoC microfluidic platform. The assay simultaneously targets two genomic regions and was tested against DNA standards, a reference strain, and clinical specimens including lesion swabs and urine, with and without prior DNA extraction. The assay demonstrated good linearity, high amplification efficiency, no cross-reactivity with other pathogens, and was capable of detecting the pathogen in a sample collected during the seronegative window period. Compared to a conventional thermal cycler requiring substantially longer run times, the GeneSoC platform completed detection within fifteen minutes, enabled by faster temperature transitions inherent to microfluidic thermal cycling. The authors conclude that this approach offers a promising point-of-care solution for early syphilis diagnosis, though DNA extraction may still be beneficial when target DNA concentrations are low.

Trends of nucleic acid - based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases.

Hatem H, Mysara M, Ramadan R. Trends of nucleic acid - based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases.. Journal of biological engineering. 2026

This review examines the landscape of nucleic acid-based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases, motivated by the global need for decentralized, rapid, and accessible testing. The authors surveyed existing platforms—including lateral flow assays, biochips, and biosensors—by organizing their analysis around the three core operational steps of nucleic acid extraction, amplification, and detection. For extraction, they evaluated approaches such as magnetic bead-based, paper-based, and integrated microfluidic methods with emphasis on simplicity and equipment-free operation. Amplification strategies reviewed include both thermal cycling methods like PCR and isothermal alternatives such as LAMP, RPA, and NASBA, all adapted for portable and low-power conditions. On the detection side, the review spans colorimetric and fluorescent lateral flow formats through to advanced biosensor technologies incorporating CRISPR/Cas systems and toehold switch mechanisms, which offer highly sensitive and programmable nucleic acid recognition.

A One-Pot CRISPR-Cas12b Assay for Rapid Detection of Human Adenovirus Serotypes 3 and 7.

Wan L, Zhou J, Yu L et al.. A One-Pot CRISPR-Cas12b Assay for Rapid Detection of Human Adenovirus Serotypes 3 and 7.. Journal of medical virology. 2026

This paper developed a rapid diagnostic assay for detecting two clinically important human adenovirus serotypes responsible for acute respiratory tract infections in children, which pose a notable public health burden. The researchers combined multiple cross displacement amplification with CRISPR-Cas12b activity in a single-tube, isothermal reaction that produces a fluorescent signal within under an hour at a fixed temperature. The dual amplification strategy—isothermal nucleic acid amplification followed by CRISPR-mediated collateral cleavage—achieved an extremely low limit of detection using target plasmids and showed no cross-reactivity against a panel of other respiratory pathogens. When validated against clinical pharyngeal swab samples, the assay showed complete concordance with quantitative PCR, confirming its diagnostic accuracy. The authors conclude that this point-of-care compatible platform is a rapid, specific, and clinically reliable tool suitable for both routine diagnosis and public health surveillance.

Advances in rapid detection technologies for zoonotic diseases: a one health-oriented review.

Zhao J, Hou X, Chang G. Advances in rapid detection technologies for zoonotic diseases: a one health-oriented review.. Frontiers in cellular and infection microbiology. 2026

This review examines rapid detection technologies for zoonotic pathogens through the lens of the One Health framework, which recognizes the interconnection of human, animal, and environmental health. The authors surveyed recent advances across immunological assays, molecular diagnostics, isothermal amplification platforms, biosensors, and microfluidic systems, evaluating each against the distinctive challenges posed by zoonotic disease surveillance. A central contribution is the scenario-oriented analysis, which assesses how well different technologies suit specific contexts such as wildlife reservoir monitoring, farm-level screening, outbreak-site emergency response, and laboratory confirmation. The review also considers how cross-species transmission dynamics, diverse sample matrices, resource-limited field conditions, and needs for multiplexing and real-time data integration reshape technical requirements for diagnostic tools. The authors identify current bottlenecks and propose future directions toward integrated, intelligent, and field-deployable diagnostic systems capable of supporting coordinated zoonotic disease surveillance and control.

Loss-of-function mutation in Omicron variants reduces spike protein expression and attenuates SARS-CoV-2 infection

Michelle N. Vu, R. Elias Alvarado, Dorothea R. Morris et al.. Loss-of-function mutation in Omicron variants reduces spike protein expression and attenuates SARS-CoV-2 infection. Nature Communications. 2026

Data-Centric Foundation Models in Computational Healthcare: A Survey

Yunkun Zhang, Jin Gao, Zheling Tan et al.. Data-Centric Foundation Models in Computational Healthcare: A Survey. ACM Computing Surveys. 2026

This survey examines the intersection of foundation models and computational healthcare through a data-centric lens, focusing on how data quality, scale, and characterization shape AI performance in clinical settings. The authors review a broad range of data-centric approaches spanning the full pipeline from model pre-training through inference, addressing longstanding healthcare challenges such as data scarcity, annotation burden, patient privacy, and ethical considerations. The survey covers key issues in AI security, evaluation, and alignment with human values as they apply to healthcare foundation models. The authors conclude with an outlook on how foundation model-based analytics can improve patient outcomes and clinical workflows, and they maintain a curated repository of relevant models and datasets to support ongoing research in the field.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

Yuqing Liu, Kun Zhu, Weijun Peng et al.. Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review paper examines how the integration of multiomics technologies and artificial intelligence is reshaping precision drug discovery and clinical medicine. The authors survey how diverse omics approaches — including genomic, transcriptomic, proteomic, and metabolomic data — combined with deep learning architectures can decode complex biological datasets to uncover disease mechanisms and molecular targets. The paper analyzes specific applications across the drug development pipeline, including target deconvolution, drug repositioning, de novo compound discovery, virtual screening, pharmacokinetic modeling, and safety assessment. Case examples drawn from oncology, neurodegenerative diseases, and cardiovascular conditions are used to illustrate the practical potential of this convergence. The authors conclude that while challenges such as data harmonization, reproducibility, and algorithmic bias remain, the fusion of multiomics and artificial intelligence represents a foundational shift toward more precise, data-driven translational medicine.

In vivo endosomal escape assay identifies mechanisms for efficient hepatic LNP delivery

Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances

Pranay Amruth Maroju, Angad S. Sidhu, Amogh R. Motaganahalli et al.. Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances. Antibiotics. 2026

This review examines bacterial biofilms, which are structured microbial communities encased in a self-produced matrix of polysaccharides, proteins, extracellular DNA, and lipids that enables adhesion, structural stability, and creation of protective microenvironments. The authors synthesize current knowledge on biofilm developmental stages, matrix composition, and the physiological heterogeneity that emerges during maturation, as well as mechanisms by which biofilms resist antimicrobial treatment, including restricted antibiotic diffusion, enzymatic inactivation, efflux pump upregulation, persister cell formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review maps biofilm involvement across a range of clinical contexts, including cystic fibrosis, chronic wounds, osteomyelitis, implant-associated infections, and breast implant illness, where persistent biofilms are hypothesized to drive chronic inflammation and systemic manifestations. Both conventional and emerging approaches to biofilm detection and real-time monitoring are discussed alongside therapeutic strategies that combine antibiotics with matrix-disrupting enzymes, quorum-sensing inhibitors, bacteriophages, metabolic reactivators, and nanotechnology-based delivery systems. The authors conclude that the interconnected nature of biofilm protective mechanisms necessitates integrated detection and multifaceted treatment strategies, and that advances in multi-omics and systems-level modeling will be critical for developing next-generation interventions.

UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons

Linzhan Mou, Jiahui Lei, Zhiyang Dou et al.. UniMate: One Unified Model to Animate Diverse Skeletons. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of generating motion for arbitrary 3D skeletal structures, a bottleneck in animation pipelines where rigging has become automated but motion synthesis remains topology-constrained. The authors developed UniMate, a unified foundation model that takes a rigged 3D asset and a text prompt as input and synthesizes articulated motion without any test-time optimization or per-skeleton retraining. The model employs a topology-aware diffusion transformer that encodes skeletal structure through graph-aware attention biases derived from pairwise joint relations and geodesic distances, a spectral rotary position embedding adapted to arbitrary kinematic trees via the graph Laplacian, and a global topological conditioner pooled from the rest-pose skeleton. To support training across diverse creature types, the authors curated a large dataset spanning bipedal, quadrupedal, avian, marine, insectoid, serpentine, and articulated rigid object categories with unified canonicalization and text annotations. UniMate is reported to outperform existing baselines in quality, generalization, and efficiency, while also enabling zero-shot cross-topology transfer, motion in-betweening, sequence expansion, and text-guided editing.

Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert

A. Castro-González, O. Barragán, D. J. Armstrong et al.. Two extremely irradiated volatile-rich sub-Neptunes with companions in the TOI-426 and TOI-1839 systems: Insights into arrival and survival near the lower edge of the Neptunian desert. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study confirms two highly irradiated sub-Neptune planets, TOI-426 b and TOI-1839 b, orbiting solar-type stars near the lower boundary of the Neptunian desert, a region where close-in planets of intermediate size are rare. Using space-based photometry and a large set of ground-based radial velocity measurements, along with Gaussian process models to handle stellar activity, the authors characterized the planets' sizes and masses and also detected additional companions in both systems, including a long-period giant planet around TOI-426 and a transiting sub-Neptune around TOI-1839. Despite receiving extreme levels of stellar irradiation, the densities of all three transiting planets require substantial volatile content, and atmospheric escape modeling strongly favors water-dominated rather than hydrogen/helium envelopes, supporting a steam-world interpretation. The survival of these volatile-rich planets at such close orbits challenges the idea that the desert's lower edge is shaped purely by photoevaporation of primordial envelopes. A statistical analysis further reveals a strong excess of outer giant companions around low-mass sub-Neptunes located specifically within the tidal survival band near the desert edge, implicating high-eccentricity tidal migration as an important mechanism in determining which planets reach and survive in this region.

Parametric and feedback-controlled multiparameter quantum estimation in a double cavity optomechanics: steady and dynamical state

Hamza Harraf, Mohamed Amazioug, Amjad Sohail et al.. Parametric and feedback-controlled multiparameter quantum estimation in a double cavity optomechanics: steady and dynamical state. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates multiparameter quantum estimation in a coupled-cavity optomechanical system where two mechanical modes interact with driven optical cavities, incorporating two-mode squeezed vacuum, intracavity degenerate parametric amplification, and coherent optical feedback. Using continuous-variable Gaussian-state formalism and linearized quantum Langevin dynamics, the authors derive steady-state covariance matrices and evaluate quantum Fisher information to bound the simultaneous estimation precision of the optomechanical coupling strength and cavity dissipation rate. The study employs both symmetric and right logarithmic derivative formalisms to characterize precision limits across steady-state and transient regimes. The key findings are that intracavity parametric amplification substantially enhances multiparameter sensitivity over a broad range of operating conditions, while coherent feedback produces a nonmonotonic effect on estimation precision that depends sensitively on feedback phase, reflectivity, squeezing strength, and thermal occupation. This nonmonotonic behavior establishes coherent feedback not as a simple enhancing or degrading mechanism, but as a tunable resource for engineering quantum fluctuations and parameter-dependent correlations in the optomechanical state.

A Generalizable Feature Extractor for Alzheimer's-Related Brain MRI Tasks

Reza Rajabli, D. Louis Collins. A Generalizable Feature Extractor for Alzheimer's-Related Brain MRI Tasks. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated whether a compact supervised model pretrained on brain-age prediction could function as a generalizable foundation model for multiple Alzheimer's-related neuroimaging tasks. The researchers froze the weights of a three-dimensional convolutional neural network previously trained for brain-age estimation and adapted it to downstream tasks using Low-Rank Adaptation, which introduces only about one percent additional trainable parameters per task. The approach was evaluated across six experiments spanning classification of cognitive status, cross-cohort generalization without any retraining, prediction of mild cognitive impairment progression, amyloid positivity detection from structural MRI, and volumetric estimation of hippocampal and white matter hypointensity volumes. The adapted model performed strongly across all tasks and transferred to an entirely unseen cohort with no additional training while remaining competitive with much larger architectures typically used for segmentation tasks. The authors conclude that a carefully pretrained brain-age model can serve as an effective and reusable backbone for diverse Alzheimer's-related tasks even under strict data constraints.

Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence

Urja Pawar, Rajitha Ramanayake, Nabeel Kemal et al.. Necessary or Sufficient? Evaluating LLM Explanations With Behavioural Evidence. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether the explanations produced by large language models alongside their decisions accurately reflect the factors that actually drive those decisions. The researchers tested two formal interpretations of explanatory factors: necessity, meaning a factor genuinely influences the output when altered, and sufficiency, meaning a factor alone can preserve the output when other information is removed. Using controlled black-box interventions across two synthetic tasks—recommending advisors to clients and judging prompts for harmfulness—they measured necessity and sufficiency scores for both cited and uncited factors across multiple models from three major model families. They found only moderate correlation between a model's cited factor rankings and the behaviorally measured scores, and uncited factors frequently outscored the weakest of the three cited factors, particularly in the advisor recommendation task. The authors conclude that while model-provided explanations carry useful signal, they do not reliably identify the most influential factors under either interpretation, raising concerns for their use in agent monitoring, error diagnosis, and escalation decisions.

Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models

Matthias Busch, Marius Tacke, Sviatlana V. Lamaka et al.. Molecular Déjà Vu: Digit-Level Retrieval of Published Values in Frontier Language Models. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates whether large language models are genuinely predicting molecular properties or simply retrieving memorized numerical values from their training data when evaluated on standard benchmarks. The authors audited a broad set of frontier language models across multiple molecular regression benchmarks, testing for digit-level verbatim retrieval of published experimental values. They found that verbatim retrieval is widespread but unevenly distributed across benchmarks, with the majority of models showing retrieval behavior on roughly half the datasets tested. Notably, the level of reasoning employed by the model significantly affects retrieval rates, with higher reasoning levels triggering flagged retrieval far more frequently than minimal reasoning on identical inputs. The study also demonstrates that suppressing retrieval through molecular structure transformations reduces performance differences between models, suggesting that apparent benchmark superiority is partly an artifact of memorization rather than genuine predictive capability.
